# Spatial-4D (Mechanism) — Five-Perspective Review

> [!abstract] What this is
> A five-perspective adversarial review of every **cluster** and **research direction** in this doc, generated 2026-06-20.
> Each unit was put through a fixed 4-prompt lens by an independent analyst then an independent peer reviewer:
> **P1** five expert perspectives (Practitioner / Academic / Skeptic / Economist / Historian) · **P2** contradiction map ·
> **P3** synthesis briefing (for a researcher deciding whether to *commit* to the bet) · **P4** independent peer review (confidence 1-10 + Stanford-prof grade).
> Every novelty verdict was stress-tested against the live literature via the alphaxiv MCP. Cited paper ids are real (vault KH notes or alphaxiv hits).

---

## Spatial-4D — doc-level synthesis

### Capsule

Spatial-4D is the most internally coherent and highest-ceiling doc in the set: a single thesis ("explicit / externalized geometry, not appearance, is the durable substrate for action") is pressed through four clusters (A policy-representation, B reasoning, C world-models/memory, D asset-generation) and twelve directions, almost all graded B+ with two A- clusters (A, C). The taste is good and the questions are sharp, but the novelty has thinned under its own pace: every direction lands as **partially-pre-empted** or barely-**still-whitespace**, each shadowed by a single 2026 paper that already occupies most of the slot (Sparse2Act for A1, PointWorld for A2, 3D-MIX for A3, GEM-4D for C2, Mem-World for C4, FunRec for D1, Picasso for D2, Kairos against the whole of C). The value concentrates not in any one untouched problem but in the **residual matched-comparison experiments** the field has skipped: explicit-vs-distilled geometry on a genuinely geometry-bound OOD split (A), metric-vs-topological graph arithmetic (B1), occupancy-vs-latent horizon-per-FLOP (C1), externalized-vs-internalized pointmaps head-to-head (C2), and readiness→real-transfer correlation (D2). The doc's real risk is not that the bets are wrong but that it repeatedly recommends a strategic shift (A1→A3, "geometry is settled") *ahead of* the one experiment it concedes would decide it, while several load-bearing citations turn out to be miscited or phantom.

### Cross-direction pattern

**The geometry-source / sensing tax is unpriced everywhere, and it is the same hidden variable that decides every bet.** Across A1, A2, A3, B1, C1, C2, C3, C4, D1 and both A and C cluster verdicts, the "missing sixth perspective" converges on one figure: the hardware/sensing realist (or the supervision-economist). Every direction assumes geometry arrives clean and free, yet estimated depth/pointmaps/occupancy are worst exactly on the contact-rich / textureless / specular / occluded tail where Finding-1 of each cluster says geometry matters most. The doc's universal escape hatch (predicted pointmaps, derived occupancy GT) is a second large network or a self-consistency loophole, not a free lunch. The deeper recurrence: nearly every cluster's load-bearing finding rests on **open-loop, single-backbone, sim/LIBERO numbers** and is then asked to carry a **closed-loop, cross-backbone, deploy-grade** conclusion. Whether on clean rendered depth (A3), single-bespoke WMs (C4), or in-simulator readiness (D), the gap between what was measured and what is claimed is the same shape in all four clusters.

### Sharpest intra-doc contradiction

**Cluster A says "geometry gets distilled away, so demote the explicit head (A1→A3)"; Cluster C says "geometry must be the externalized substrate, keep it explicit."** A's Finding 4 argues explicit geometry is recoverable from a latent at inference and shifts the centre of gravity toward parameter-efficient injection (A3) and away from native point branches (A1). C2/C1 stake their entire contribution on the *opposite* premise: that externalized output-space geometry is durable precisely because it is *not* trivially recoverable from internalized features (C2-H4) — yet C2's own foil, GEM-4D, delivers the same SR gains via an internalized route, which is exactly A's "distilled away" claim made flesh. So A uses internalization as the reason to abandon explicit geometry, while C uses non-internalizability as the reason to commit to it, and GEM-4D is cited on both sides. A secondary contradiction sits inside C4/D: the thesis frames "geometry's win is settled" while F5/H3/H4 across the doc treat it as conditional on regime, sensor, and rigidity.

### Recurring blind spots

- **Rigid-body monoculture.** A, B, C, D cluster verdicts each independently flag that every proof point is rigid, single-agent, quasi-static. Dynamic / deformable / articulated / in-hand-occluded geometry, the regime where "a single object's 4D trajectory" or "6-DoF = rigid transform between matched points" stops being well-posed, has one dated data point (ACID).
- **Estimated-geometry noise at deploy.** No unit treats sensed-vs-estimated depth as a first-class variable; A3 explicitly never separates it, and it could invert the explicit-channel recommendation into a benchmark artifact.
- **Write-side / closed-loop error accumulation.** C3, C4, D1 each ignore how wrong geometry compounds: memory poisoning from acted-on hallucinations (C4), pseudo-depth error over minute-scale horizons (C3), unvalidated readiness→real-contact transfer (D).
- **Citation hygiene.** A pervasive, doc-specific blind spot: load-bearing claims rest on miscited or phantom papers — VSP misread as "perception dominates" (B1), 2606.01095 cited as vault-controlled but absent from KH (B2), the 2605.24642 "null result / p=0.399 / McNemar" contradicted by its own KH note which says geometry *helps* (B cluster), Sparse2Act's decoder numbers not in its KH note (A1), and SimRecon/SPARK carrying D1 conclusions while KH-absent.
- **Eval instrument resolvability.** Saturated LIBERO ID (~97%) and floored AnyBody (0% extrapolation) may be unable to separate the very hypotheses (H4/H5) the cards bet on (A1 missing-methodologist).

### Commit picks

- **C1 (occupancy-vs-latent world-model horizon)** — the only still-whitespace direction whose pre-emptor (PointWorld) is point-flow not occupancy and whose competitors are off-corner; the corner is genuinely open if costed in horizon-per-FLOP.
- **B1 (metric-vs-topological spatial reasoning)** — H1's numeric-vs-categorical-edge ablation survives every pre-emptor (VSP only touches the perception side); a clean, falsifiable kill-test.
- **A (cluster) → the Frontier comparison itself** — matched-backbone explicit-point-head vs VLA-JEPA-latent on a geometry-bound OOD split is the single experiment that resolves the doc's central tension; commit to running it, not to its predicted answer.
- **D2 (readiness→transfer)** — still-whitespace and uniquely actionable: rank-correlating static readiness scores against closed-loop sim-trained-policy transfer is the experiment that converts a scorecard into a moat.

### Reshape picks

- **A2 (occupancy world-model)** — threatened by PointWorld occupying the exact slot, plus a load-bearing latency figure that is misread ~8× (0.12s/10-step, not 0.1s/step) with no matched dense-voxel number; the "deployable axis" finding is the doc's most-cited least-true claim.
- **Cluster B** — lowest confidence (low=2) in the doc; its headline "geometry grounds reasoning → OOD gain" rests on a phantom null result that its own KH note inverts. Reshape the framing before defending the directions.
- **B2 (4D-consistency attribution)** — leans on KH-absent 2606.01095 and on knobs (depth-vs-dynamics) fused in one attention block that may not be cleanly toggleable; the "publishes regardless of which arm wins" de-risk evaporates if the ablation can't be built.
- **C2 (externalized pointmaps)** — GEM-4D contests the bet's premise on raw SR and the durable-edge claim (H4 debuggability) is the least-evidenced in the card; reshape toward a measured latency/recoverability test or it inverts to "internalized-and-fast."
- **C4 (geometric×episodic memory)** — Mem-World plus the unresolved settled-vs-conditional contradiction; the "months not years" urgency rests on two uncited papers.
- **D1 (passive-video articulation)** — FunRec already half-owns the slot and the passive-video wedge may be ill-posed (a revolute axis is observable only when actuated); KH-absent SimRecon/SPARK carry the conclusion.

### Doc grade

**B+.** A genuinely coherent, high-taste agenda with two A- clusters and a clean central thesis, held back from A-tier by uniform pre-emption, a recurring habit of pre-deciding strategic shifts ahead of the deciding experiment, a systemic unpriced sensing/supervision tax, rigid-body monoculture, and several load-bearing citations that do not survive a KH check.

---

### A (Cluster review)

Topic: the coherence + central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster A "Geometry-Native Policies" (A1 point-cloud heads / A2 occupancy forward model / A3 depth-token bridge) — does this grouping carve the problem at the right joint, and is the shared bet ("the action head reads or predicts RGB tokens, leaving metric 3D implicit and paying an embodiment-specific data tax") sound and still whitespace?

The cluster's framing, quoted: Cluster-Overview row — *"The action head reads or predicts RGB tokens, leaving metric 3D implicit and paying an embodiment-specific data tax"*, with *"A1 is the lead... the three span the cost/benefit frontier of explicit geometry (full point branch → in-loop occupancy → side-channel depth)."* Intro paragraph — *"The action head consumes or predicts explicit 3D — the policy's conditioning state is metric geometry, not RGB tokens."*

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
Core position: the cluster is honest about the one thing that actually bites in deployment — that RGB heads break under appearance shift while geometry holds — and A3 (depth side-channel into a frozen backbone) is the only one of the three that respects the installed base nobody wants to re-train. But A1's point branch and A2's occupancy loop both quietly assume clean depth/occupancy at deploy, which is exactly what you do not have on a real robot.
Strongest supporting evidence: [[2606.02274|Dexterity-BEV]] holds 89.9% on shifted LIBERO where 2D collapses to <10%; [[2604.14089|UMI-3D]] shows LiDAR pose survives on textureless/deformable scenes where vision-SLAM drifts; [[2510.14836|QDepth-VLA]] gets +10-20% real with single-view quantized depth at side-channel cost. Conversely [[2606.10899|MV-Actor]] only reaches PerAct2 87.8% after explicit metric-depth *repair* on consumer cameras — proof the sensed-depth assumption is load-bearing and fragile.
The one thing only this perspective says: the card's own Risk note ("predicted pointmaps remove the depth-sensor dependency") is a tax, not a free lunch — [[2606.03943|PointAction]]'s video-to-point model is a heavy generative stage in the control loop, and no row reports its latency or closed-loop rate. A3 is the only direction you could ship next quarter; A1/A2 need a depth story before the geometry story matters.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
Core position: the cluster is unusually honest that its premise (geometry > RGB) is *settled prior*, not the contribution — [[2402.02500|Point Cloud Matters]] (NeurIPS'24, +76.92% mean SR / best zero-shot across 125 tasks) and [[2306.06799|Point Cloud RL Study]] already proved it, so H1/H2 are explicitly labelled "confirmed prior, extended." The real bet narrows correctly to three *unmeasured* quantities: the minimal-geometry knee (H3), representation-vs-decoder attribution (H4), and the alignment-preservation number (A3-H4).
Strongest supporting evidence: the card cites the cleanest existing instruments — [[2503.04877|Adapt3R]] (the published representation/decoder decoupling: <6% drop on novel camera vs 44.4% RGB), [[2410.17488|GenDP]] (geometry-vs-semantics decomposition, without-semantics ablation at 58%), [[2605.24642|GFM-VLA Study]] (GR00T-N1.5 VLM = 0.73 m depth RMSE vs geometric 0.41 m — the deficit measured directly). The framing is consistent with what the literature actually shows.
The one thing only this perspective says: the literature is now moving *faster than the card*. [[2606.12759|Sparse2Act]] (in your own KnowledgeHub, uncited in this card) already runs an action-decoder-capacity ablation showing the pretrained *representation*, not the decoder, carries the gain (SimpleDP3 75.0% / MLP 74.7% vs full DP3 82.0%) — that is H4's claim, demonstrated, on sparse xyz-only tokens (which is also H3's regime). The whitespace is thinner than the card's "no found paper plots this" implies.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
Core position: the shared bottleneck is real but the *contribution* the cluster bets on is a measurement-paper, not a method-paper, and measurement papers age out the instant a method paper lands the same ablation in passing. Two of the three front-line hypotheses (A1-H3, A1-H4) are already partially answered in adjacent work the card under-weights.
Evidence the card conveniently under-weights: (i) [[2606.12759|Sparse2Act]]'s decoder-capacity ablation = A1-H4, on sparse tokens = A1-H3 regime, with cross-domain transfer (LIBERO→Meta-World 73.4%) = the transfer A1 wants; (ii) [[2509.01819|ManiFlow]] and [[2502.12320|Fusing Point Cloud Visual]] (FPV-Net: local RGB adds a separable +2% on fine-grained RoboCasa) are *in the card* as counterpoints but bound the bet's "appearance is noise" premise harder than the card admits — geometry is not strictly dominant where texture carries task signal; (iii) [[2604.06168|Action Images]]/[[2506.07961|BridgeVLA]]/[[2506.01196|OG-VLA]] show the *projection* trick (geometry through a 2D VLM) often matches a native point head, undercutting the "native point state" framing A1 leads with.
The one thing only this perspective says: A2 (occupancy forward model) is the weakest leg and risks dragging the cluster's coherence. Its premise that occupancy-as-forward-model is novel is false by the card's own admission ([[2203.06856|ACID]] RSS'22, [[2505.16249|3D-Occ-MPC]] RA-L'25), and its 80×-horizon hope rests on porting a *driving* result ([[2603.28887|OccSim]]) to sub-cm dynamic tabletop — a transfer the card itself flags as the go/no-go risk. A2 is a different research program (world-models) bolted onto a policy-representation cluster.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
Core position: the geometry-over-RGB narrative is cheap to publish and expensive to be wrong about, which is why the card is flooded — 40+ rows, most from late-2025/2026, nearly all chasing the same LIBERO 97-99% saturation ceiling. The incentive gradient pushes toward "add a geometry module, beat LIBERO by 1pt" (the exact thing the card says it is *not* doing), so the cluster's measurement-first framing is counter-incentive and therefore credible *if* held to.
Evidence: the LIBERO numbers are visibly saturated — [[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]] 98.7%, [[2606.03240|GeoAlign]] 99.0%, GEAR-VLA 98.7%, [[2602.10098|VLA-JEPA]] 97.2% — the benchmark no longer discriminates, which is precisely why the card pivots to LIBERO-Plus/cross-embodiment/appearance-shift splits. The Risk note ("treat ID parity as expected, not failure") is the economically literate move.
The one thing only this perspective says: the value of this cluster is a *negative result with a number* (the knee, the alignment cost, the representation attribution) — and negative-results-with-numbers are under-supplied because they do not win benchmark leaderboards or industry demos. That is the arbitrage. But it is also the risk: a measurement contribution gets scooped by any method paper that runs the ablation as a footnote (see Sparse2Act). Commit only if you can land the *full* frontier (knee + attribution + alignment) before a method paper does it piecemeal.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
Core position: this is the pixel-vs-latent / explicit-vs-implicit world-model cycle replayed at the *action-head* layer. The field has run this loop before — voxels vs pixels in 3D vision, model-based vs model-free RL, modular-vs-end-to-end perception — and the historical pattern is that explicit geometry wins on the OOD/contact tail and loses on convenience/scale, then gets absorbed as a *channel* into the dominant end-to-end model rather than replacing it.
Evidence: A3's own framing concedes this arc — [[2503.07511|PointVLA]], [[2510.12276|Spatial Forcing]], [[2512.00903|SwiftVLA]] all *distill geometry away at inference* and keep the 2D backbone; the explicit branch is becoming a train-time teacher, not a deploy-time stream. A2 is literally the Dreamer/model-based lineage (occupancy = the "explicit state" wing) being re-imported to manipulation, exactly as occupancy WMs matured in driving first.
The one thing only this perspective says: every prior wave's explicit-geometry champion was eventually beaten not by a better RGB model but by a *learned latent that distilled the geometry* ([[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]], [[2602.10098|VLA-JEPA]] are already at 97-99% with no inference geometry). A1-H5 (point head must beat the latent on OOD) is therefore the cluster's true load-bearing bet — if the distilled latent matches explicit points on LIBERO-Plus, the whole "explicit metric state" thesis collapses to "geometry-aware pretraining," which is a much smaller, more crowded claim.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

(1) **Direct clashes.**
- *Practitioner vs Academic/Skeptic on A1's deployability:* Practitioner says A1/A2 assume clean depth/occupancy you do not have at deploy ([[2606.10899|MV-Actor]] needs depth-repair; PointAction's predicted-point stage is unpriced). Academic/Skeptic treat the predicted-pointmap path ([[2606.03943|PointAction]]) as having *solved* the sensor dependency. The clash: is "predict the geometry" a genuine fix or a latency/error tax that moves the problem?
- *Skeptic vs Academic on novelty:* Skeptic says A1-H3/H4 are already partially answered ([[2606.12759|Sparse2Act]] decoder-capacity ablation + sparse-token regime). Academic says the card narrows precisely to the *unmeasured* knee/attribution and cites the right instruments. The clash is whether Sparse2Act's ablation *is* H4 or merely adjacent (it is on a pretraining-encoder, not on PointAction's cross-embodiment factorized decoder, and never plots a density knee).
- *Historian vs the cluster's own A1 premise:* Historian says explicit geometry historically gets absorbed as a distilled channel (A3's own SwiftVLA/Spatial-Forcing rows prove the arc), implying A1's "native explicit point state" is the *losing* side of the cycle. The card leads with A1 as "the lead."

(2) **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
Strongest: **Skeptic** — it is the only one that names a specific, in-vault, uncited paper ([[2606.12759|Sparse2Act]]) that already lands two of the three front-line claims, and correctly isolates A2 as the coherence risk. It is evidence-bound and falsifiable. Weakest: **Economist** — directionally right about saturation and incentives but its core move ("the value is a negative-result-with-a-number") is a claim about publishing sociology, not about whether the bet is *true*; it adds framing, not evidence.

(3) **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
Does a native explicit-point action head still beat the strongest *distilled-latent* policy ([[2602.10098|VLA-JEPA]] 97.2%, [[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]] 81.0% LIBERO-Plus) on the geometry-bound OOD split at matched data and matched backbone? This resolves Skeptic-vs-Academic (is the whitespace real or already eaten by latents) AND Historian-vs-cluster (does explicit geometry win or get absorbed). If the latent matches the point head on LIBERO-Plus, A1's "explicit metric state" thesis collapses to "geometry-aware pretraining" and the cluster loses its lead leg.

(4) **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
RGB-token policies have a *real, measurable* metric-geometry deficit that bites specifically on the appearance-shift / cross-embodiment / contact-rich tail, and ID benchmarks (LIBERO ~97%) no longer discriminate it. All five accept [[2605.24642|GFM-VLA Study]]'s 0.73 vs 0.41 m depth-RMSE deficit and the saturation of LIBERO as established. The *bottleneck* is sound; the contest is only over which of the three remedies is the right contribution and whether the specific measurements are still unclaimed.

(5) **The blind spot (topic none addressed).**
None of the five interrogated the cluster's *coherence claim itself* — the asserted A1→A2→A3 "cost/benefit frontier" as a single axis. A1 (representation choice in the head), A2 (forward-model substrate for a planner), and A3 (frozen-backbone retrofit) are three *different research programs* (policy representation / model-based control / parameter-efficient transfer) that happen to share the word "geometry." The synergy claim ("A2's forecast occupancy conditions A1's head," A2-H4) is asserted but rests on *both* unproven directions succeeding simultaneously — a conjunction risk no perspective priced. Also unaddressed: dynamic/deformable/articulated geometry (every proof point is rigid-body; occupancy and point clouds both degrade on cloth/fluids, where [[2203.06856|ACID]]'s deformable result is the lone, dated data point).

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

(1) **CEO summary.** Cluster A bets on a real and well-established bottleneck — RGB action heads leave metric 3D implicit and break on the appearance-shift/cross-embodiment/contact tail — and wisely reframes its contribution away from the saturated "geometry helps" claim toward three *unmeasured* quantities: how little geometry suffices (the knee), whether the representation or the decoder carries cross-embodiment transfer, and what semantic-alignment cost a frozen-backbone retrofit pays. The bottleneck is sound and the measurement-first framing is credible, but the cluster's coherence is weaker than advertised: A1/A2/A3 are three distinct research programs stapled into one "frontier," A2 (occupancy) is a world-model program whose tabletop transfer is the card's own flagged go/no-go, and the front-line A1 claims are aging fast — an in-vault but uncited paper ([[2606.12759|Sparse2Act]]) already runs a representation-vs-decoder attribution and operates in the sparse-geometry regime. The defensible, still-open core is A1-H4/H5 (representation attribution on a *cross-embodiment factorized* decoder, and beating the distilled latent on OOD) plus A3-H4 (the alignment-preservation number nobody has put a value on).

(2) **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The metric-geometry deficit of RGB heads is real and concentrated on the OOD/contact tail.* Most reliable — supported by all five; grounded in [[2605.24642|GFM-VLA Study]] (0.73 vs 0.41 m), [[2402.02500|Point Cloud Matters]] (+76.92%), [[2606.02274|Dexterity-BEV]] (89.9% vs <10%). Challenged by none on existence; bounded by Skeptic ([[2502.12320|FPV-Net]]/[[2509.01819|ManiFlow]]: appearance still helps on fine-grained tasks).
2. *ID benchmarks no longer discriminate the bet; the contest must live on OOD/cross-embodiment splits.* Strongly supported — Economist + Academic; LIBERO at 97-99% across [[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]]/[[2606.03240|GeoAlign]]/GEAR-VLA. Challenged by nobody; this is the card's own Risk note.
3. *A1's representation-vs-decoder (H4) and minimal-geometry (H3) front-line is partially pre-empted.* Moderately reliable — Skeptic + Practitioner; [[2606.12759|Sparse2Act]] runs a decoder-capacity ablation (encoder carries the gain) on sparse xyz tokens. Challenged by Academic: it is on a pretraining encoder, not PointAction's cross-embodiment factorized decoder, and plots no density knee — so H4-on-PointAction and the knee remain open.
4. *Explicit geometry historically gets absorbed as a distilled train-time channel, not a deploy-time stream — making A1-H5 the true load-bearing bet.* Moderately reliable — Historian, corroborated by A3's own [[2512.00903|SwiftVLA]]/[[2510.12276|Spatial Forcing]] rows and the latent baselines [[2602.10098|VLA-JEPA]]/[[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]]. Challenged by the Academic optimism that explicit points still win OOD (untested at matched backbone).
5. *The cluster's A1→A2→A3 "single frontier" coherence is partly rhetorical; A2 is the weak, conjunction-risked leg.* Lower reliability (a framing judgment, not a measured fact) — Skeptic + the unaddressed blind spot. Supported by A2's own admission ([[2203.06856|ACID]]/[[2505.16249|3D-Occ-MPC]] settle occupancy-as-forward-model) and the driving→tabletop transfer risk. Challenged by the card's synergy narrative (A2-H4 conditioning A1's head).

(3) **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's deploy complaint ("you don't have clean depth/occupancy at runtime") and the Historian's pattern ("explicit geometry gets distilled away at inference") are the *same force* viewed from two ends: the field is converging on *train-time geometry, inference-time RGB* precisely because sensed geometry is unreliable at deploy. That convergence is fatal to A1/A2 as *deploy-time explicit-state* programs but it is the wind in A3's sails — A3-H5 (train-time distillation matches the inference depth channel) is exactly this convergence, and is the part of the cluster the deployment reality and the historical arc *both* endorse. The cluster's centre of gravity should move from A1 (its stated lead) to A3.

(4) **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not commit to the cluster as drawn.** Commit to a *single sharpened direction*, not the three-legged frontier. The defensible publication is A1-H4 + A1-H5 fused into one claim on the OOD/cross-embodiment split, explicitly differentiated from [[2606.12759|Sparse2Act]] (cite it; your delta is the *cross-embodiment factorized-decoder* 2×2 swap on [[2606.03943|PointAction]] scored on a morphology-distance protocol like [[2505.14986|AnyBody]], plus the head-to-head against the *distilled latent* [[2602.10098|VLA-JEPA]]/[[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]] on LIBERO-Plus — neither of which Sparse2Act does). Concrete next experiment: on one fixed backbone, run the 2×2 (point-rep vs RGB-feature-rep) × (factorized vs monolithic decoder) on PointAction's embodiment-free pretraining, scored on AnyBody's interpolation/extrapolation splits, AND in the same harness pit the winning point head against VLA-JEPA at matched data on LIBERO (ID) vs LIBERO-Plus (OOD). Pre-register the falsifiers already in the card (flat decoder/representation gap → factorization not geometry; latent matches on OOD → explicit adds nothing). Drop A2 to a "future work / sibling C3" pointer — it is a world-model program whose tabletop transfer is unproven and whose synergy with A1 is a double-conjunction risk. Keep A3 as the *deployable* companion result (the recovery-fraction knee + the [[2505.05456|SITE]] alignment-drift number), since deployment reality and historical precedent both favour it.

(5) **Frontier question that would change everything.** Is explicit metric geometry a *representation* the action head must read, or merely a *pretraining signal* the head can distill and discard? If a latent distilled from geometry ([[2602.10098|VLA-JEPA]]-class) matches an explicit point head on the geometry-bound OOD split at matched backbone and data, then the entire cluster collapses from "geometry-native policies" to "geometry-aware pretraining" — a smaller, far more crowded claim — and the right bet flips from A1 (explicit state) to A3-H5 (cheapest distilled channel). Answering this one comparison decides whether Cluster A is a publication or a footnote.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded in `Mechanism/Spatial-4D.md` lines 89-345 (Cluster A) plus a spot-verification of the load-bearing pre-emption claim (Sparse2Act 2606.12759 KH note).*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (each 1-10, reliability)

**Finding 1 — Metric-geometry deficit of RGB heads is real and concentrated on the OOD/contact tail. Score: 9/10.**
The strongest claim in the briefing and correctly ranked first. The card carries three independent instrument types: a *linear-probe* on the backbone (GFM-VLA Study 0.73 vs 0.41 m depth RMSE, the cleanest because it measures the representation, not a downstream SR confound), a *graded-shift sweep* (Point Cloud Matters +76.92% across 125 tasks), and a *collapse demonstration* (Dexterity-BEV 89.9% vs <10%). The briefing honestly bounds it with FPV-Net/ManiFlow (appearance still helps on fine-grained). Docked one point only because the three numbers come from three different benchmarks/scales (probe on GR00T-N1.5, sweep on simple-encoder DP, collapse on LIBERO-shift): the deficit is real but the briefing slightly conflates "backbone underencodes geometry" (probe) with "explicit geometry fixes it at VLA scale" (the open H1). The deficit is established; the *fix* is what A1 must still prove.

**Finding 2 — ID benchmarks no longer discriminate; the contest must live on OOD/cross-embodiment splits. Score: 9/10.**
Near-unimpeachable and self-evidencing from the card's own numbers: LIBERO 97-99% saturates across 3DThinkVLA (98.7), GeoAlign (99.0), VLA-JEPA (97.2), GeoVLA (97.7), AML-VLA (98.6). This is also the card's own Risk note, so it is not a synthesis insight so much as a correctly-surfaced fact. The only reason it is not 10 is that "must live on OOD splits" quietly assumes the *available* OOD suites (LIBERO-Plus, AnyBody) actually stress geometry rather than appearance: LIBERO-Plus is largely appearance/layout perturbation, so it may under-test the metric-geometry claim the cluster rests on. The right split may not yet exist, which the briefing does not flag.

**Finding 3 — A1's representation-vs-decoder (H4) and minimal-geometry (H3) front-line is PARTIALLY pre-empted by Sparse2Act. Score: 8/10.**
I verified this directly. Sparse2Act (2606.12759) is in the KH vault, is uncited in the card (0 occurrences in the file; it appears only in prior review parts), and its own Key-Insight states the gains are "largely attributable to the quality of the pretrained encoder's representation... reducing reliance on high-capacity downstream decoders." That IS a representation-over-decoder attribution in the sparse-geometry regime, so "partially pre-empted" is the correct verb, not "scooped" and not "novel." The Academic rebuttal is also correct and material: Sparse2Act attributes via a *pretraining-encoder + decoder-capacity* lever, not PointAction's *factorized predicted-pointmap decoder* 2x2 swap, and plots no density knee. So H4-on-PointAction and H3 survive. Docked two points because the briefing does not quantify *how much* is pre-empted: if a reviewer reads Sparse2Act as already showing "representation carries it," the marginal novelty of H4 is "same conclusion, different decoder architecture," which is a weaker delta than the briefing's confident "remains open" implies. This is a real, present threat to the contribution's framing, not a hypothetical.

**Finding 4 — Explicit geometry historically gets absorbed as a distilled train-time channel, not a deploy-time stream, making A1-H5 the load-bearing bet. Score: 6/10.**
The pattern is genuinely visible in the card: A3's SwiftVLA, Spatial Forcing, GeoAlign, VEGA, WAM4D all drop the 3D branch at inference, and the latent baselines (VLA-JEPA, 3DThinkVLA) score 81% LIBERO-Plus with no inference geometry. So "the field is converging on train-time geometry" is well-supported. The over-reach is the *causal* claim baked into the Hidden Connection: that distillation wins *because* sensed geometry is unreliable at deploy. The card's own evidence is equally consistent with a cheaper explanation: distillation wins because it is computationally cheaper and ID benchmarks (where these numbers are reported) cannot see the OOD gap where explicit geometry might pull ahead. The Historian's "absorbed" pattern is on LIBERO/LIBERO-Plus, i.e. on exactly the saturated/appearance-shift splits Finding 2 says do not discriminate. So Finding 4 may be an artifact of measuring on the wrong split, which is precisely the open H5 the briefing then says is undecided. The briefing both leans on the pattern (to elevate A3) and concedes it is untested at matched backbone, a tension it does not resolve.

**Finding 5 — The A1->A2->A3 "single frontier" coherence is partly rhetorical; A2 is the weak, conjunction-risked leg. Score: 7/10.**
Correct and the most useful organizational critique. A2's bet is a triple conjunction (dense-voxel beats pixel at RoboCasa scale AND Warp-DiT bound survives sub-cm AND forecast occupancy conditions A1's head), and A2-H1's falsifier plus the Risk note both concede driving->tabletop transfer is unproven, so it is structurally the riskiest leg. The "stapled together" charge is fair: A1 is a representation-attribution program, A2 is a world-model horizon-stability program, A3 is a retrofit-economics program; they share only "geometry in the loop." Marked down to 7 because this is a framing judgment (the briefing itself labels it LOWER confidence) and because the briefing slightly overstates the incoherence: A2-H4 (forecast occupancy conditions A1's head) and A3-H5 (cheapest distilled channel feeding A1's host) are *genuine* mechanical couplings, not pure rhetoric. The cluster is loose, not incoherent.

**Mean = 7.8. Min = 6.0.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding 4 (6/10). The load-bearing inferential move of the whole briefing, the recommendation to shift the cluster's centre of gravity from A1 to A3, rests on the Historian's "explicit geometry gets distilled away" pattern, and that pattern is observed almost entirely on ID/appearance-shift splits (LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus) that Finding 2 declares non-discriminative for the metric-geometry bet. The briefing uses a pattern measured on the wrong axis to recommend abandoning the leg that the right axis would test.

**What would verify it:** a single matched-backbone, matched-data head-to-head of an *explicit* point head against a *geometry-distilled latent* (VLA-JEPA-class) on a genuinely geometry-bound OOD split, not LIBERO-Plus appearance perturbation but a cross-embodiment / metric-shift split (AnyBody extrapolation, or a depth-controlled FP3-style appearance-shift suite). If the latent matches the explicit head there, Finding 4 is confirmed and A1-as-deploy-state dies. If the explicit head pulls ahead, Finding 4 is an ID-benchmark artifact and the A1->A3 centre-of-gravity shift is premature. This is exactly the Frontier Question the briefing poses, which means the briefing's own actionable insight (drop A1's lead, promote A3) is being asserted *ahead of* the experiment it admits would decide it.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Historian over-dominated, with the Practitioner as a force-multiplier.** The synthesis is structurally built so that the Historian's "distilled away" pattern and the Practitioner's "no clean depth at deploy" complaint are fused into the Hidden Connection, and that fused force is then used to drive the single biggest recommendation (move centre of gravity A1 -> A3). The Academic is repeatedly cast in the *rebuttal* role (Findings 3, 4) rather than the framing role, which is the tell: the optimistic "explicit points still win OOD at matched backbone" position is real and untested, yet it is consistently relegated to a "challenged by" footnote rather than given equal billing. A genuinely balanced synthesis would have left the A1-vs-A3 centre of gravity *open pending the Frontier experiment* instead of pre-deciding it toward A3 on deploy-pragmatics and historical precedent, both of which are arguments from convenience, not from the geometry-bound measurement the cluster is actually about. The Economist and Skeptic are well-represented (Findings 2, 3, 5); the Academic is under-weighted.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective — a 6th angle that would change conclusions

**The Sensing/Hardware realist (the depth-estimator and calibration perspective).** Every leg of this cluster silently assumes geometry is *available* at the quality the bet needs, and the briefing treats "predicted pointmaps" (PointAction, AugVLA, Evo-0) as a clean escape from the depth-sensor dependency. A hardware realist asks: what is the *error distribution* of monocular/VGGT-predicted depth on the contact-rich, textureless, specular, transparent objects where Finding 1 says geometry matters most, and does it survive extrinsic-calibration drift across the very embodiments H4 swaps over? This changes conclusions in two ways. (i) It cuts *against* A3's promotion as much as A1's: a depth-token bridge is only as good as its depth estimator, and on the contact tail (FPV-Net/ManiFlow's regime) predicted depth is worst exactly where it is needed, so A3's "cheapest deployable" framing inherits the same unreliable-geometry problem the briefing uses to demote A1. (ii) It reframes the Frontier Question: the real deploy-time contest is not "explicit point head vs distilled latent" but "explicit point head fed *noisy estimated* geometry vs distilled latent that learned *clean sim* geometry at train time," and on that framing the latent's train-time-clean-geometry advantage is a *measurement* artifact of where geometry was clean, not a fundamental property. None of the five perspectives prices estimator noise as a first-class variable; it sits buried in a Risk bullet. AML-VLA's G3T depth priors and MV-Actor's "metric-depth repair on consumer cameras" rows are in the card precisely because this is the live failure mode, yet the synthesis never elevates it.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: A-.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest briefing that does the hardest thing right: it refuses the saturated "geometry helps" claim and relocates the contribution to three genuinely unmeasured quantities (the density knee, the representation-vs-decoder attribution, the alignment-preservation cost), and it verifiably caught a real uncited near-pre-emption (Sparse2Act) that a lazier review would have missed. The confidence ranking is well-calibrated and the falsifiers are pre-registered. It falls short of an A because its single biggest move, shifting the cluster's centre of gravity from A1 to A3, is decided on deploy-pragmatics and historical precedent *ahead of* the one experiment (the Frontier comparison) it admits would actually settle it, and it under-weights the Academic and omits the sensing-realist angle that cuts against A3 as hard as A1.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**

1. **Do not pre-decide A1 -> A3. Make the Frontier comparison the gate, not the footnote.** The briefing should hold the centre-of-gravity question *open* and state explicitly: run the matched-backbone explicit-point-head vs VLA-JEPA-latent comparison on a *geometry-bound* (not LIBERO-Plus appearance) OOD split *first*; the result of that one comparison decides A1-vs-A3, and it is illegitimate to demote A1 before it.

2. **Fix the split mismatch underneath Finding 4 and Finding 2.** The "distilled away" evidence and the "ID saturates" evidence are both measured on LIBERO/LIBERO-Plus, which the briefing itself calls non-discriminative. Name a genuinely metric-geometry-stressing OOD protocol (AnyBody extrapolation + a depth-controlled appearance-shift suite) and re-state Findings 2 and 4 as *contingent on that protocol existing*, because as drawn they may be artifacts of the wrong axis.

3. **Quantify the Sparse2Act delta, do not just cite it.** State in one sentence what marginal novelty H4 retains over Sparse2Act's encoder-level representation-over-decoder finding (the answer: the *factorized predicted-pointmap decoder* 2x2 on a cross-embodiment morphology-distance protocol, which Sparse2Act does not do), so a reviewer cannot collapse the contribution to "same conclusion, new decoder."

4. **Promote estimator-noise to a first-class variable.** Add a sensing-realist line to the de-risk plan: the predicted-pointmap escape (PointAction) and the depth-token bridge (A3) both inherit depth-estimator error worst on the contact tail, so the recovery-fraction and alignment numbers must be reported *under realistic estimator noise*, not clean sim depth, or the cost-efficiency frontier is measured in a regime the deploy target never sees.

---

### A1 (Direction review)

Topic: whether to commit, as a publication bet, to "the geometry-over-RGB advantage that Point Cloud Matters proved with simple encoders survives at 2D-pretrained-VLA scale *and* most of it is recoverable from sparse geometry" (verbatim FP bet: knee far below GeoVLA's full branch at DP3-level sparsity (H3), and it is the point *representation*, not PointAction's factorized decoder, that carries the 43.0% xArm7 zero-shot transfer (H4)).

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
Core position: the bet's deploy-time payoff hinges entirely on where the points come from, and that is exactly where the card is weakest. A1 leans on the predicted-pointmap path ([[2606.03943|PointAction]]) to dodge the depth sensor, but a video-to-point model is a second large network in the control loop, so you have traded a depth camera's noise for a generative model's latency and hallucination, not removed the cost.
Strongest supporting evidence: the field's own deploy-grade results already favor *cheap injected* geometry over native point branches: [[2603.24393|3D-MIX]] bolts VGGT features onto an existing VLA for +12.51% OOD without re-architecting, [[2602.10698|AugVLA]] gets only modest gains (54% vs Gr00T 50% at 100 demos) from a sensor-free point side-feature, and [[2606.10899|MV-Actor]] needed feed-forward spatial-prior repair just to survive *consumer* depth cameras (87.8% PerAct2). [[2503.04877|Adapt3R]] runs at 44.1 Hz only because it collapses 3D to an end-effector-frame localization vector, not a full branch.
The one thing only this perspective says: H3's knee is the *only* part of this bet a practitioner cares about. If the recovery-vs-density curve has no knee below the full branch, A3's depth-token side-channel ([[2510.14836|QDepth-VLA]], [[2603.24393|3D-MIX]]) wins and the native point head is a research artifact, not a deployable policy. Run H3 *first*; it is cheap and it gates everything else.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
Core position: H1 and H2 are honestly flagged as confirmed prior ([[2402.02500|Point Cloud Matters]] NeurIPS'24, [[2306.06799|Point Cloud RL Study]]), so the publishable surface is genuinely just H3+H4+H5, and the literature already crowds two of those three. The card's own table concedes that no row isolates the minimal-geometry knee or the representation-vs-decoder driver, which is the correct framing, but "no row in *my* table" is not the same as "no paper anywhere."
Strongest supporting evidence: the strongest *internal* support is [[2503.04877|Adapt3R]] — the card itself flags it as "the cleanest published representation-vs-decoder split" with <6% drop on novel camera poses (vs 44.4% RGB), and [[2410.17488|GenDP]]'s without-semantics ablation (93% → 58%) already decomposes geometry from semantics. The strongest *threat*: [[2503.08950|FP3]] reports the head ablation (100% point → 30% RGB) but only at one density, and [[2605.24642|GFM-VLA Study]]'s linear probe (0.73 m vs 0.41 m depth RMSE) supplies the mechanism but on a *fusion* recipe, not a native head.
The one thing only this perspective says: H4 as written is under-specified — "swap point→RGB-feature, decoder fixed" conflates two confounds (the representation *and* the embodiment-agnostic *pretraining corpus* PointAction's video-to-point model is trained on). [[2505.14986|AnyBody]]'s morphology-distance protocol is the right instrument, but its reach/push tasks "narrow rather than close the contact-precise side" (the card admits this), so a clean H4 result on AnyBody may not transfer to the contact-rich regime where H2 says the advantage lives.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
Core position: the bet is half-solved and the unsolved half may be a null result. A June-2026 paper, Sparse2Act ([2606.12759], UCLA, not in the card), already runs (a) a *sparse* xyz-only 3D encoder (1024 pts → 192 patches) that beats DP3-from-scratch 86.9% vs 29.1% on LIBERO-10, and (b) a decoder-capacity ablation showing the *pretrained representation* carries the win, not the decoder: swapping the full DP3 head for SimpleDP3 (75.0%) or a bare MLP (74.7%) barely dents the 82.0% full-decoder number. That is H4's thesis ("representation, not decoder, drives the win") executed and published.
Strongest supporting evidence: beyond Sparse2Act, the latent counterpoints the card itself raises are damning for H5 — [[2602.10098|VLA-JEPA]] (97.2% LIBERO), [[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]] (98.7% LIBERO, 81.0% LIBERO-Plus), and [[2507.00416|Evo]] (+40pt under viewpoint/height shift, *no point input at all*) show distilled/implicit geometry already wins OOD without an explicit point state. If a sensor-free latent matches the point head on LIBERO-Plus, H5 falsifies itself and the whole "explicit metric structure beats latent priors" pillar collapses.
The one thing only this perspective says: the card's escape hatch — that Sparse2Act fixes density (no *curve*) and isn't at 2D-pretrained-VLA scale — is real but thin. The contribution shrinks to "a recovery-vs-density *curve* (not just a point) on a *VLA* backbone (not a sparse DP)." That is a measurement, not a mechanism. Ask whether a curve nobody has plotted is worth a paper if its endpoints are already known.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
Core position: the geometry-over-RGB narrative is structurally favored by academia precisely because it is *cheap to run* — point-cloud policies train on LIBERO/RoboCasa/RLBench at single-GPU scale ([[2410.01345|GemBench]]'s 3D-LOTUS: 83.1% at 2.2 GPU-days), so the whole sub-field is an academic refuge from the compute moat that OpenVLA-class RGB foundation models built. That same incentive is why the benchmarks are saturating (LIBERO ~97%) and why the card has to retreat to OOD splits to find headroom.
Strongest supporting evidence: the saturation is in the card's own targets — [[2508.09071|GeoVLA]] 97.7%, [[2605.11832|AML-VLA]] 98.6%, [[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]] 98.7% LIBERO all within 1% of each other. When ID is saturated, the publishable delta moves to bespoke OOD suites ([[2509.18953|Eva-VLA]], LIBERO-Plus), and whoever defines the split defines the win — a citation-incentive trap, not a capability advance. Industry (NVIDIA's GR00T-N1.5, the [[2605.24642|GFM-VLA Study]] subject) is meanwhile betting on *fusing* GFMs into RGB VLAs, not native point heads, because that protects their RGB-pretraining investment.
The one thing only this perspective says: H3 is the *commercially* decisive hypothesis, not because of compute but because it tells you which side of the moat to stand on. A knee at DP3 sparsity means a small lab can ship a competitive geometry-native policy; no knee means you need a video-to-point foundation model (PointAction-scale) and you are back inside the moat. The bet's economic value is entirely in H3 returning "yes."

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
Core position: this is the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle replayed on the observation space — every few years the field rediscovers that explicit structure (point clouds now; voxels, depth, optical flow before) beats end-to-end pixels under shift, then end-to-end scaling quietly reabsorbs the advantage once the data gets big enough. [[2403.09631|3D-VLA]] (the card's own "historical root") already put point clouds into an LLM with a generative WM in 2024; A1 is sharpening, not opening, that lineage.
Strongest supporting evidence: the card *documents* the cycle — [[2509.01819|ManiFlow]] and [[2502.12320|Fusing Point Cloud Visual]] (FPV-Net, +2% from local RGB on fine-grained RoboCasa) are explicitly flagged as the "appearance-still-helps" counterpoint that bounds the "appearance is noise" claim. That is exactly how the modular-vs-end-to-end swing has always ended: not with one side winning but with hybrids (3D-MIX, AugVLA, MV-Actor) absorbing the structured signal as a side-channel into the scaling backbone.
The one thing only this perspective says: the durable contribution from past waves was never "structure beats pixels" (always re-proven, always re-absorbed) but the *measurement that told you when*. The DR/sim2real history rewarded whoever quantified the gap, not whoever picked a side. So A1's lasting value, if any, is H3's knee curve as a reusable *design rule* ("below X density, sense; above, predict; here, just fuse"), not as a verdict that point heads win.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

(1) **Direct clashes.**
- *Practitioner vs Skeptic on H4's status.* Practitioner says H4 is the wrong thing to spend the bet on (deploy cares about H3's knee, not which half of PointAction carries transfer); Skeptic says H4 is the most threatened because Sparse2Act's decoder-capacity ablation (82.0% → 74.7% MLP) already shows the representation carries the win. They clash on whether H4 is *unimportant* or *already-done* — either way it is the weakest pillar.
- *Skeptic vs Academic on H5.* Skeptic: [[2507.00416|Evo]] and [[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]] already beat OOD with *no* explicit point state, so H5 is likely to falsify. Academic: those are *distilled* priors and the geometry-bound LIBERO-Plus split may still separate them from a true metric head; the contest is legitimate but only on a split that isolates metric structure, which nobody has cleanly built.
- *Economist vs Historian on what the narrative is.* Economist: the geometry-native narrative is a compute-refuge that benchmark-saturation is already exhausting. Historian: it is a recurring structural truth that gets re-absorbed, not a refuge — same data, opposite reading of *why* the sub-field is crowded.

(2) **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
Strongest: **Skeptic**, because it is the only one anchored to a paper the card does not cite (Sparse2Act, 2606.12759) that directly executes H4's claim and undercuts the "no row isolates this" framing with an out-of-table counterexample. Weakest: **Historian**, persuasive but non-falsifiable — "structure gets re-absorbed" is true of everything and yields no experiment; its one actionable nugget (the knee as a design rule) is just H3 restated.

(3) **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
Does a sparse explicit point head still beat the best *sensor-free latent* policy ([[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]] / [[2507.00416|Evo]]) on a *contact-rich, geometry-bound* OOD split at matched data and matched backbone? This single experiment resolves the Skeptic-vs-Academic H5 clash and simultaneously tells the Practitioner whether explicit points buy anything a latent cannot distill — i.e. whether the whole "explicit > implicit" pillar holds.

(4) **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
The bet's center of gravity is **H3 (the minimal-geometry knee)**: Practitioner (deploy gate), Economist (which-side-of-the-moat), Historian (the durable design rule), Academic (the one of H3/H4/H5 not yet crowded), and even the Skeptic (concedes Sparse2Act fixes density and plots no curve) all converge that a *recovery-vs-density curve* is the most defensible and most uniquely-unclaimed contribution. ID benchmark saturation (~97% LIBERO) is also universally accepted as fact, forcing the contest onto OOD splits.

(5) **The blind spot (addressed by none).**
No perspective addressed **data-provenance and label cost of the OOD/cross-embodiment evaluation itself** — every falsifier (graded-shift sweeps, phase-labeled contact segments, morphology-distance splits on [[2505.14986|AnyBody]], real graded-shift on [[2509.18953|Eva-VLA]]) presumes someone has *built and annotated* those suites at matched data across backbones. The card treats the evaluation harness as free; in practice the phase-labeling (H2) and the matched-data cross-backbone retraining (H1/H5) are the dominant cost and the most likely point of failure, and nobody priced it.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

(1) **CEO summary.** A1 is a well-mapped, honestly-scoped bet whose two settled pillars (geometry-over-RGB under shift; advantage is relational/contact) are correctly demoted to "confirmed prior re-run at scale," leaving three front-line hypotheses — and of those, one (H4, representation>decoder) has just been substantially pre-empted by an uncited June-2026 paper (Sparse2Act), and one (H5, explicit>latent OOD) is at live risk of falsifying against sensor-free latent policies ([[2507.00416|Evo]], [[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]]) that already win OOD with no point state. The genuinely unclaimed, defensible, and universally-endorsed contribution is H3: a *recovery-vs-density knee curve* on a 2D-pretrained-VLA backbone — a measurement, not a mechanism, but a reusable design rule the field lacks. Commit narrowly to H3 as the spine, demote H4 to a confirmatory ablation citing Sparse2Act, and treat H5 as a high-variance side-bet, not the thesis.

(2) **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
- F1 (highest). *The publishable surface is H3+H4+H5, not the geometry-over-RGB principle itself.* Supported by Academic, Skeptic, Economist (saturation), and the card's own framing; challenged by none — H1/H2 are NeurIPS'24-settled ([[2402.02500|Point Cloud Matters]], [[2306.06799|Point Cloud RL Study]]).
- F2. *H4 (representation>decoder) is now partially pre-empted.* Supported by Skeptic (Sparse2Act 82.0%→74.7% MLP decoder ablation) and Academic ([[2503.04877|Adapt3R]], [[2410.17488|GenDP]] already decompose); challenged mildly by the card's note that A1's *specific* point→RGB-feature swap at VLA scale on [[2505.14986|AnyBody]] is still unrun.
- F3. *H3 (minimal-geometry knee) is the most defensible and most decision-relevant hypothesis.* Supported by all five perspectives (the universal-agreement item); challenged only by the risk that the curve has no knee (H3's own falsifier), which would itself be a publishable negative result.
- F4. *H5 (explicit>latent on OOD) is at material risk of falsifying.* Supported by Academic (a clean metric-bound split might still separate them); challenged by Skeptic with [[2507.00416|Evo]] (+40pt OOD, no point input) and [[2606.04436|3DThinkVLA]] (81.0% LIBERO-Plus) — distilled latents already win OOD.
- F5 (lowest reliability, highest leverage). *The deploy-time value collapses to where points come from.* Supported by Practitioner (PointAction's video-to-point model is a second in-loop network; [[2606.10899|MV-Actor]] needed repair for consumer depth) and Economist (no knee = back inside the foundation-model moat); challenged only by the card's predicted-pointmap escape hatch, which the Practitioner argues trades sensor noise for generative latency, not away.

(3) **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's "run H3 first, it gates everything," the Economist's "H3 tells you which side of the compute moat to stand on," and the Historian's "the durable artifact is the design rule, not the verdict" are the *same claim* arriving from deploy-cost, capital-allocation, and historical-pattern reasoning independently: H3's knee curve is simultaneously the cheapest experiment, the commercially decisive one, and the only output likely to outlive the current wave. Three incentive structures converge on one experiment — strong signal it is the true core.

(4) **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then commit narrowly.** Do not commit to A1 as a three-hypothesis bet. Re-architect it around H3 as the spine: (i) run the recovery-vs-density curve *first and cheapest* — sweep point density/completeness on a *single fixed* [[2508.09071|GeoVLA]]-style VLA head vs an RGB head on RoboCasa365 + an OOD split, plot recovered-fraction-of-full-branch-gain, and report the knee (or its absence as a negative result). (ii) Demote H4 to a confirmatory ablation that *cites and extends* Sparse2Act ([2606.12759]) — your novelty is the point→RGB-feature swap at 2D-pretrained-VLA scale on [[2505.14986|AnyBody]]'s morphology-distance protocol, not the representation>decoder claim itself, which is now taken. (iii) Treat H5 as a labeled risk, not a pillar — pre-register the falsifier (latent matches point head on a contact-rich geometry-bound split) and be willing to publish H5 negative. Concrete next experiment: the H3 density sweep, because it is the universally-endorsed core, the cheapest to run, and it gates whether the deployable path is "sense / predict / fuse."

(5) **Frontier question that would change everything.** If a sensor-free latent-geometry policy ([[2507.00416|Evo]]-class, no depth, no point input) can be shown to *recover the same recovery-vs-density curve's asymptote* on contact-rich OOD, then explicit metric points add nothing distillation cannot, and the entire geometry-native-policy cluster (A1-A3) collapses into a pretraining-objective question for RGB VLAs — at which point the bet is not "points vs pixels" but "what supervision signal forces a 2D backbone to internalize metric 3D," and A1 should be reframed as that.

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded against the A1 card (Spatial-4D.md L93-187), the Sparse2Act / Evo / 3DThinkVLA / AnyBody KH notes, and the upstream analyst part 002-A1-1.*

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10 reliability)

- **F1 — "publishable surface is H3+H4+H5, not geometry-over-RGB itself" — 9/10.** The strongest finding. H1/H2 demotion is correct and the card itself concedes it ("settled prior — Point Cloud Matters, Point Cloud RL Study"). NeurIPS'24 settled the principle; re-running it at VLA scale is anchoring, not a contribution. Docked one point because "publishable surface" smuggles in an unstated premise: that re-running a settled principle at a new scale is *never* publishable. At a top venue, "the small-scale law breaks/holds at foundation scale" sometimes *is* the paper. The finding overstates by treating H1/H2 as worthless rather than as a necessary-but-insufficient control.

- **F2 — "H4 (representation>decoder) is now partially pre-empted by Sparse2Act" — 6/10.** Directionally correct and the citation gap is real (verified: 2606.12759 is in KH, absent from the A1 card). But the load-bearing numbers — "82.0% full decoder → 75.0% SimpleDP3 → 74.7% MLP" — do **not appear in the vault's Sparse2Act note**, which carries only the 86.9% LIBERO-10 headline and a *qualitative* claim ("gains largely attributable to the pretrained encoder's representation... reducing reliance on high-capacity downstream decoders"). The decoder-capacity numbers are asserted in the analyst prose, unverified here. More substantively, Sparse2Act is **decoder-*capacity*** ablation (does a smaller head hurt?), which is not the same construct as H4's **representation-*identity*** swap (point-features vs RGB-features, decoder fixed). Pre-emption of the *spirit* is real; pre-emption of the *specific 2x2* is overclaimed. The finding's own hedge ("partially") is doing a lot of work.

- **F3 — "H3 (minimal-geometry knee) is most defensible and decision-relevant" — 8/10.** Well-supported and the genuine core. The "negative result is still publishable" escape is honest. Two deductions: (a) DP3 (2403.03954) already conditions a precise policy on sparse points, so a knee "near DP3 density" is partly foreknown — the novelty is the *curve shape and the recovered-fraction metric on a VLA backbone*, a thinner contribution than "the knee exists." (b) "Most decision-relevant" rests on the Practitioner/Economist framing (see Bias Check) and inherits their assumption that deploy economics dominate scientific interest.

- **F4 — "H5 (explicit>latent on OOD) at material risk of falsifying" — 8/10.** The evidence is the most solidly grounded in the briefing: Evo's "+40pt under viewpoint/height/distractor shift, no point input" and 3DThinkVLA's "81.0% LIBERO-Plus, no inference sensor" are both verbatim-confirmed in their KH notes. The falsification risk is genuine. Docked for one unargued leap: Evo/3DThinkVLA win OOD on *appearance/viewpoint* perturbations (LIBERO-Plus camera/background/distractor), which is *not* the contact-rich, geometry-bound split H5 actually reserves for the explicit head. The briefing partially concedes this (the "contact-rich geometry-bound split" pre-registration) but then still rates H5 "at material risk" — the risk is to the *appearance-OOD* arm, which H5 already cedes. The two latents have not been shown to win on the split H5 cares about, so "material risk of falsifying" is slightly inflated.

- **F5 — "deploy-time value collapses to where points come from" — 6/10.** True and the most operationally useful caveat, but it is the briefing's own self-declared lowest-reliability item and earns it. It rests entirely on the Practitioner reading ("video-to-point is a second in-loop network, trades sensor noise for generative latency") which is an *argument*, not a measured result — PointAction's actual in-loop latency/hallucination cost is asserted, not cited with numbers. MV-Actor "needed repair for consumer depth" is correctly grounded, but one repaired-depth datapoint does not establish that the whole deploy path collapses.

**Mean = 7.4. Min = 6.0.**

##### 2. Weakest link

**F2's claim that Sparse2Act has "executed and published H4's thesis."** The specific decoder-ablation numbers (82.0 / 75.0 / 74.7) that make this claim bite are **not in the vault** — the KH note carries only a qualitative encoder-vs-decoder statement and the 86.9% LIBERO-10 headline. To verify, one must pull the Sparse2Act PDF and confirm (i) the exact decoder-capacity table exists with those numbers, and critically (ii) whether the paper runs a *representation-identity* swap (sparse-3D-features vs 2D/RGB-features, decoder held fixed) or only a *decoder-capacity* sweep. If only the latter, H4's exact contribution survives and "pre-empted" should soften to "adjacent prior to cite." The entire "demote H4 to confirmatory ablation" recommendation hinges on this distinction, and it is currently resting on un-sourced numbers.

##### 3. Bias check

**The synthesis is dominated by the Practitioner–Economist axis (deploy-cost / capital-allocation), amplified by the Historian.** Three of the five findings (F3, F5, and the "hidden connection") and the entire actionable insight resolve to "run the cheapest, most deployable experiment." The "hidden cross-perspective connection" is presented as three *independent* incentive structures converging on H3 — but Practitioner (deploy gate), Economist (which side of the moat), and Historian (durable design rule) are not independent; they are three restatements of a single deploy-economics prior. That is corroboration-by-rephrasing dressed as triangulation, and it inflates confidence in H3 beyond what the *scientific* (Academic) case alone supports. The **Academic** voice — the one perspective that defends H4/H5 as legitimate open contests and flags the AnyBody-task-scope confound — is consistently the dissenter that gets out-voted 3-to-1 rather than weighed. The **Skeptic** is treated as near-authoritative (it anchors F2 and F4) largely *because* it cites the one uncited paper, but a single uncited counterexample should raise a literature-search flag, not settle two of five findings.

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The Methodologist / Experimental-statistician.** No perspective interrogates whether the proposed experiments can actually *resolve* their hypotheses at the effect sizes and sample sizes available. Concretely: (a) AnyBody (verified) is **reach + push only**, and ME-agents already hit **0% on extrapolation** there — so the H4 2x2 swap may land in a floor regime where representation and decoder both score ~0 and *nothing* is separable; the card admits AnyBody "narrows rather than closes the contact-precise side," but the briefing never asks whether the instrument has the dynamic range to read H4 at all. (b) LIBERO ID is saturated at ~97% across GeoVLA/AML-VLA/3DThinkVLA within 1% — H5's "ties on ID" prediction is untestable on a saturated ceiling (everything ties). (c) The H3 "knee" is a claim about curve *shape*; detecting a knee vs a smooth ramp needs enough density points and seeds to distinguish them statistically, and nobody priced the seed budget. A Methodologist would likely down-rank F3 and F4 on *measurability* grounds and would surface the blind spot the analyst flagged but no synthesis finding absorbed — the **annotation/matched-data cost of the OOD evaluation harness itself**. This perspective changes conclusions: it suggests the real first experiment is not "run H3" but "pilot whether any of these suites can separate the hypotheses before committing the bet."

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

A genuinely strong, honestly-scoped briefing: the H1/H2 demotion is correct, the H4 citation gap is a real catch (Sparse2Act verifiably in-KH-but-uncited), and the H4/H5 evidence on Evo/3DThinkVLA is verbatim-grounded. It loses the A-range on three things: (1) it over-weights deploy economics and presents three correlated perspectives as independent convergence (the "strong signal" in the hidden connection is partly an artifact of restating one prior thrice); (2) its second-most-prominent finding (F2) rests on decoder-ablation numbers absent from the cited vault note and conflates decoder-capacity with representation-identity; (3) it never asks whether the proposed instruments (AnyBody floor effects, saturated LIBERO ID) can measure what the hypotheses claim.

**Top fixes, in order:**
1. **Source the Sparse2Act decoder numbers and pin down its ablation type** before calling H4 "pre-empted." If it is decoder-capacity only, downgrade "demote to confirmatory ablation" to "cite as adjacent prior; H4's representation-identity swap remains open."
2. **Sharpen H5's split.** State explicitly that Evo/3DThinkVLA win on *appearance/viewpoint* OOD, which H5 *cedes*; H5's live claim is only the contact-rich geometry-bound split, and the falsification risk should be re-scored against that split, not LIBERO-Plus broadly.
3. **Add a measurability pre-flight.** Before the H3 density sweep, pilot that AnyBody (reach/push, 0% extrapolation floor) and the chosen OOD suite have the dynamic range and seed budget to separate knee-vs-ramp and representation-vs-decoder — otherwise the "cheapest experiment" returns an uninterpretable null.
4. **Discount the hidden connection.** Re-label Practitioner/Economist/Historian convergence as one deploy-economics prior viewed three ways, not three independent confirmations, and let the Academic case carry equal weight on whether H3's curve is scientifically (not just commercially) worth a paper.
