### A2 (Direction review)

Topic: A dense *explicit* voxel-occupancy forward model (beyond ACID's implicit field and 3D-Occ-MPC's single-object grid) holds geometric stability ≥10× longer than a pixel-WM baseline at RoboCasa scale, its Warp-DiT error bound survives to sub-cm resolution, and its forecast occupancy can condition A1's point head to close a fully-geometric perceive-imagine-act loop that beats present-frame-only conditioning on long-horizon SR.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
Core position: A dense sub-cm voxel grid over a manipulation workspace is a memory and latency monster, and the bottleneck for closed-loop control is not horizon length but per-step inference cost inside an MPC/MPPI sampling loop. The card's own Risk note concedes voxels are memory-heavy and occupancy GT is scarce in manipulation datasets; that is the whole ballgame for a deployable system.
Strongest evidence: [[2601.03782|PointWorld]] (Stanford/NVIDIA) gets *real-time* manipulation MPC at **0.1s/forward pass** precisely by going to a sparse point-flow substrate and weighting loss to the 1-5% of points that move, explicitly because dense prediction does not scale; [[2603.28887|OccSim]]'s 3,000-frame result is an offline driving *data generator*, never run in a control loop at all. [[2506.23135|RoboScape]] hits 91% Robomimic on 200 trajectories staying pixel-anchored.
Only-this-perspective: build the H1 occupancy-vs-pixel inner-loop comparison with the *clock running* — report frames-to-divergence per wall-clock-second of MPC budget, not per rollout step. A WM that diverges later but is 30× slower per step loses the closed-loop race; OccSim's horizon win may be irrelevant once you amortize it over a real sampling budget.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
Core position: The card is honest that occupancy-as-forward-model is settled ([[2203.06856|ACID]] RSS'22, [[2505.16249|3D-Occ-MPC]] RA-L'25), so the contribution narrows to a *conjunction* (dense-voxel × RoboCasa-scale × ≥10×-horizon × A1-conditioning). The literature already contains every individual ingredient — what is genuinely unrun is only the head-to-head measurement, which is a benchmark contribution, not a method contribution.
Strongest evidence: [[2505.05512|Occupancy World Model]] already ports the occupancy-WM driving→indoor scale move (+22.34 IoU next-state); [[2510.16729|IR-WM]] independently delivers a *second* anti-drift occupancy forecaster (residual + Feature Alignment, L2 0.53m); [[2508.17600|GWM]] already shows a geometry-native (3DGS) manipulation WM beats image WMs (+10.5% IL SR, 2× faster MBRL). The "geometry-beats-pixels for manipulation WMs" thesis is thus *already demonstrated* — just not with a voxel substrate.
Only-this-perspective: the publishable unit is H1+H5 framed as "occupancy vs pixel vs point vs Gaussian substrate horizon-to-divergence on a shared RoboCasa/[[2604.19092|RoboWM-Bench]] scorecard," i.e. a substrate-comparison study, and reviewers will demand PointWorld and GWM as baselines, not just a pixel WM.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
Core position: The whitespace is largely an artifact of substrate-pedantry — the card defends novelty by insisting on "dense *explicit voxel*" against ACID's implicit field, 3D-Occ-MPC's single-object grid, ParticleFormer's particles, GWM's Gaussians and PointWorld's points, but a reviewer will ask why dense voxels specifically win, and the card gives no first-principles reason occupancy must be a *grid* rather than a point set. If a sparser explicit-geometry substrate already gets the horizon and the closed loop, the "voxel" qualifier is a moat of one word.
Strongest evidence: [[2601.03782|PointWorld]] is an explicit-3D, action-conditioned, scaled (2M traj, scaling laws), MPC-rolled manipulation world model with chunked prediction *specifically to cut rollout drift* — it occupies the exact "explicit geometry WM rolled forward by a planner for manipulation at scale" slot the bet claims is open, and it does so with points, not voxels, because points are cheaper. The card's H4 (forecast occupancy conditions A1's point head) is conceptually downstream of PointWorld's whole premise (state and action unified as 3D point flow).
Only-this-perspective: the card ignored the strongest counter — that the field's verdict (PointWorld, ParticleFormer, GWM) is converging on *sparse* explicit geometry as the manipulation substrate, and dense voxel occupancy may be the loser of that race, not the unrun frontier. The honest H1 falsifier should be "dense voxel ≤ a sparse-explicit-geometry baseline (PointWorld/ParticleFormer), not just ≤ pixel."

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
Core position: "Port the matured driving occupancy stack to tabletop" is a low-cost narrative because it lets an academic lab reuse a heavily-funded AV codebase (OccSim, IR-WM, GaussianWorld) and ride a benchmark-saturation wave (RoboCasa/RoboWM-Bench) without collecting expensive real-robot data. The incentive structure rewards the *port* paper regardless of whether dense voxels are the right answer.
Strongest evidence: the AV occupancy ecosystem is industrially subsidized — [[2603.28887|OccSim]], [[2510.16729|IR-WM]], GaussianWorld all come from AV-adjacent labs/companies; meanwhile the manipulation-side incumbents are the well-resourced players ([[2601.03782|PointWorld]] = Fei-Fei/Fox/NVIDIA, [[2510.10125|CTRL-WORLD]] = Tsinghua/Stanford), who can out-compute any voxel study. The cheap, defensible move for a small lab is the *benchmark* (H1's substrate-comparison scorecard), which is reusable IP, not the method.
Only-this-perspective: do not try to out-scale PointWorld/CTRL-WORLD — you will lose on compute and data. Monetize the one thing they cannot cheaply produce: a standardized *geometric* horizon-to-divergence benchmark ([[2604.22152|dWorldEval]] only scores pixels) that forces everyone's WM onto one scorecard. The benchmark is the bet, the voxel WM is just the reference implementation.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
Core position: This is the pixel-vs-latent / explicit-vs-implicit world-model cycle replaying. Each wave (Dreamer's latent rollout, NeRF→3DGS explicit reconstruction, occupancy networks in AV) swings toward whichever representation is cheapest to forecast *stably*, and the historical pattern is that the explicit-geometry camp wins the horizon argument but loses the scaling argument to whatever substrate compresses best.
Strongest evidence: the card itself documents the cycle — implicit field ([[2203.06856|ACID]], 2022) → explicit single-object grid ([[2505.16249|3D-Occ-MPC]], 2025) → latent-4D ([[2604.26694|X-WAM]]) → Gaussian ([[2508.17600|GWM]]) → point ([[2601.03782|PointWorld]], 2026). Every substrate claimed "geometry beats pixels"; the survivors were the ones that found a *compact* explicit code (3DGS-VAE in GWM, point flow in PointWorld), echoing how latent Dreamer beat pixel-space MBRL on compute, not fidelity.
Only-this-perspective: H5 (occupancy wins horizon, loses per-frame Chamfer vs X-WAM latent) is the historically correct *complementarity* framing — but history says the winning paper is the one that makes the explicit substrate *compact* (the octree in the Risk note), not the one that proves dense voxels last longest. Bet on hierarchical/sparse occupancy from day one or you re-lose the same scaling fight that killed dense voxel grids in AV (which moved to sparse/BEV).

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Practitioner vs the card's H1*: card claims ≥10× horizon-to-divergence is the win; practitioner says horizon-per-rollout-step is the wrong axis — horizon-per-wall-clock-second inside MPC is what deploys, and a dense voxel grid may lose it (PointWorld 0.1s/step vs an unstated dense-voxel cost).
   - *Skeptic vs Academic*: skeptic says the "voxel" qualifier is a one-word moat and PointWorld already pre-empts the explicit-geometry-WM-for-manipulation slot; academic says the *conjunction* (dense-voxel × scale × ≥10× × A1-conditioning) is still individually unrun, so it survives as a benchmark contribution. They clash on whether "unrun conjunction" = "novel."
   - *Historian vs the card's bet*: card bets *dense* explicit voxels hold horizon ≥10×; historian says every prior wave that bet on the *dense* explicit code lost to the compact one, so the dense-voxel framing is on the wrong side of the cycle.

2. **Strongest vs weakest.**
   - Strongest: **Skeptic**, because it is anchored on a single concrete, recent, high-credibility paper ([[2601.03782|PointWorld]]) that directly instantiates "explicit-geometry manipulation WM rolled forward by a planner at scale with anti-drift chunking" — the exact slot the bet frames as open — and it correctly identifies that the card never justifies *voxel* over *point*.
   - Weakest: **Economist**, because while the incentive read is plausible, it is the least falsifiable and least grounded in a specific result; it advises a benchmark pivot without resolving whether dense voxels are technically right.

3. **Question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   On a shared RoboCasa/[[2604.19092|RoboWM-Bench]] closed-loop scorecard, does a dense voxel-occupancy forward model beat a *sparse explicit-geometry* baseline ([[2601.03782|PointWorld]]-style point flow / [[2506.23126|ParticleFormer]]), not merely a pixel-video WM, on frames-to-divergence **per unit MPC wall-clock**? This simultaneously settles Practitioner (latency axis), Skeptic (voxel-vs-point moat) and Historian (dense-vs-compact) — and the card's current H1 falsifier ("occupancy ≤ pixel") is too weak to trigger it.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).**
   Every perspective agrees that explicit metric geometry as the forward-model substrate beats pixel/video substrates for manipulation horizon stability — supported by [[2508.17600|GWM]] (+10.5% IL SR, 2× MBRL), [[2505.16249|3D-Occ-MPC]] (occupancy EMD beats patch/crop), [[2601.03782|PointWorld]] (zero-shot MPC), and [[2510.16729|IR-WM]]/[[2603.28887|OccSim]] (occupancy horizon stabilizers). The *geometry-beats-pixels* premise is safe; what is contested is whether the dense-voxel form of that geometry is the winning instance.

5. **Blind spot (none addressed).**
   None of the perspectives or the card addresses **where the future-occupancy supervision comes from in a manipulation dataset, and whether deriving it from depth + gripper URDF (the Risk note's escape hatch) introduces a self-consistency loophole** — i.e. an occupancy WM trained on depth-derived pseudo-GT may "win" horizon stability against a pixel WM simply because both its target and its metric live in the same derived geometry space, making the ≥10× comparison partly definitional rather than physical. No perspective interrogates the occupancy-GT generation pipeline as a confound.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** A2 bets that a *dense explicit voxel-occupancy* world model, ported from autonomous-driving (OccSim's Warp-DiT) to sub-cm tabletop, will out-horizon a pixel-video forward model by ≥10× at RoboCasa scale and close a fully-geometric perceive-imagine-act loop with A1's point head. The premise underneath it — explicit metric geometry beats pixels for manipulation world models — is now well-supported and effectively settled (GWM, 3D-Occ-MPC, PointWorld, IR-WM), so the contribution is *not* the premise but a specific four-way conjunction (dense-voxel substrate × RoboCasa scale × ≥10× horizon-to-divergence × A1-head conditioning) that no single paper has yet run end-to-end. The live danger is that the field's strongest recent move ([[2601.03782|PointWorld]], Stanford/NVIDIA, Jan 2026) already occupies the "explicit-geometry manipulation WM rolled forward by a planner at scale" slot using *sparse points* chosen explicitly because dense prediction does not scale — so A2's "voxel" qualifier is the entire moat, and it is unjustified in the card. This is a de-risk, not a clean commit: the publishable, defensible core is a *substrate-comparison benchmark* (geometric horizon-to-divergence on a shared closed-loop scorecard), with the voxel WM as reference implementation, not a bet that dense voxels are the right answer.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Geometry-beats-pixels for manipulation WMs is true and well-supported* — backed by GWM (+10.5% IL SR, 2× MBRL convergence), 3D-Occ-MPC (occupancy EMD 28.6e-3 beats patch/crop), IR-WM/OccSim horizon stabilizers. Supported by all five perspectives; challenged by none. (Highest reliability — but it is the premise, not the novelty.)
2. *The bet's novelty is a conjunction, not a mechanism* — occupancy-as-forward-model is conceded settled (ACID, 3D-Occ-MPC); only the dense-voxel × scale × ≥10× × A1-conditioning *combination* is unrun. Supported by Academic; challenged by Skeptic (who says "unrun conjunction" ≠ novel once PointWorld exists). (High reliability — the card states this itself.)
3. *PointWorld is the most threatening pre-emption* — an explicit-3D, scaled, MPC-rolled, drift-chunked manipulation WM occupying the bet's slot with sparse points. Supported by Skeptic, Historian, Economist; challenged by Academic (different substrate, no voxel/occupancy, no geometric-horizon benchmark). (High reliability — grounded in fetched paper content.)
4. *Latency, not horizon, is the deployable axis* — dense sub-cm voxels may win frames-to-divergence per step yet lose per MPC wall-clock-second vs PointWorld's 0.1s/step. Supported by Practitioner, Historian; challenged by none, but untested in the card. (Medium reliability — plausible, unmeasured.)
5. *Dense voxels are historically the wrong side of the explicit-substrate scaling cycle* — survivors found compact codes (GWM's 3DGS-VAE, PointWorld's point flow, AV's move to sparse/BEV). Supported by Historian, Skeptic; challenged by the card's appeal to OccSim's dense result (a data-gen, not control, regime). (Medium reliability — analogical.)

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** Practitioner (latency-per-MPC-step), Historian (compact-code survivors win) and Economist (don't out-scale the incumbents) are independently pointing at the *same* corrective: the Risk note's parenthetical octree/sparse-hierarchical occupancy is not a footnote, it is the actual thesis. The defensible version of A2 is "compact/sparse explicit occupancy at manipulation scale," and the card has buried its own winning move in a risk mitigation.

**Actionable insight (DE-RISK, do not commit as written).** Reframe before committing. (a) Demote the method, promote the benchmark: the unit reviewers will reward is a *geometric* horizon-to-divergence scorecard (dWorldEval only scores pixels) that forces occupancy, point ([[2601.03782|PointWorld]]/[[2506.23126|ParticleFormer]]), Gaussian (GWM) and pixel WMs onto one closed-loop RoboCasa/[[2604.19092|RoboWM-Bench]]/[[2510.18135|World-in-World]] protocol. (b) Strengthen H1's falsifier from "occupancy ≤ pixel" to "occupancy ≤ a sparse-explicit-geometry baseline per MPC wall-clock-second" — if it fails *that*, the voxel qualifier is dead and you pivot to the benchmark cleanly. (c) Move sparse/hierarchical occupancy from Risk to core hypothesis. Concrete next experiment: matched-backbone dense-voxel-occupancy vs PointWorld-style point-flow vs pixel-video WM on 3-5 geometry-bound RoboCasa tasks, scored on frames-to-geometric-divergence *and* downstream closed-loop SR *per unit MPC compute*, with occupancy GT-derivation (depth+URDF) audited for the self-consistency confound the blind-spot names. This single experiment resolves the Skeptic, Practitioner and Historian clashes at once and tells you commit-vs-drop.

**Frontier question.** Is there a *representation-invariant* law of explicit-geometry world models — does horizon-to-divergence depend only on how compactly and rigidly the substrate encodes metric geometry (voxel = point = Gaussian once matched on rigidity prior and compute), or is the voxel grid's collision-queryability a genuine planning advantage that survives the latency penalty? If the former, A2 collapses into PointWorld's lineage; if the latter, the dense-voxel bet is real and the whole direction is justified.

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. I read the A2 card (Spatial-4D.md L188-260), the prior five-perspective analyst part, the PointWorld KH note (2601.03782), and the two benchmark notes (RoboWM-Bench 2604.19092, World-in-World 2510.18135) before scoring. Two facts that the synthesis got materially wrong or under-stated are flagged inline.

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

1. **Geometry-beats-pixels for manipulation WMs — 9/10.** Multiply-anchored (GWM +10.5% IL SR / 2x MBRL, 3D-Occ-MPC EMD 28.6e-3, IR-WM, OccSim) and the analyst correctly demotes it to *premise, not novelty*. The one-point deduction: "effectively settled" is a touch strong — GWM/3D-Occ-MPC show geometry-native WMs beat pixel WMs in *specific* IL/MPC setups, not a universal law; the card's own H1 still treats the head-to-head as unrun, which is mildly in tension with "settled."

2. **Novelty is a conjunction, not a mechanism — 8/10.** This is the most defensible structural critique and the card concedes it in its own text (L204, L229). Correctly attributes support to Academic and the live challenge to Skeptic. Deduction: "conjunction = benchmark contribution, not method contribution" is asserted, not argued — a four-way unrun conjunction *can* be a method paper if the dense-voxel substrate requires non-trivial engineering to hold sub-cm (H2). The synthesis treats "unrun conjunction" as automatically benchmark-shaped; that is a reviewer-taste claim, not a fact.

3. **PointWorld is the most threatening pre-emption — 6/10.** Grounded in real fetched content and the threat is real: an explicit-3D, scaled, MPC-rolled manipulation WM does compress most of the "explicit geometry rolled by a planner at scale" slot. BUT the synthesis overstates "the card ignored it." PointWorld is cited **eight times** in this very document as the **C3 cluster anchor** (Spatial-4D.md L703-778), correctly filed there because it is point-flow/latent-4D, not occupancy. The A2 card's omission is a *cross-cluster siloing* problem (A2 never imports the C3 competitor as a substrate baseline), not an oversight of the paper's existence. That is a weaker, more surgical criticism than "the bet's slot is already occupied." Also, the prior-art logic ("dense prediction does not scale, so they chose points") is PointWorld's *design rationale*, not a proof that dense voxels lose — H2 is exactly the test of whether that rationale ports to sub-cm tabletop. The finding reads the competitor's motivation as a settled verdict.

4. **Latency, not horizon, is the deployable axis — 4/10.** The *axis* is correct and valuable (per-MPC-wall-clock-second is the deployable unit, and the card omits it). But the load-bearing number is **misread**: the KH note states PointWorld runs **~0.12s for a 10-step prediction at 1B params**, i.e. ~0.012s/step / 0.12s per 10-step chunk. The synthesis repeatedly cites "**0.1s/step**" (CEO summary, Finding 4, Contradiction Map), inflating PointWorld's per-step cost ~8x and making the "dense voxel may lose per wall-clock-second" claim look more grounded than it is. The comparator the whole latency argument hinges on is wrong by an order of magnitude, *and* the dense-voxel side has no measured number at all — so this is a hypothesis dressed as a finding. The card honestly leaves it untested; the synthesis should have too.

5. **Dense voxels are the wrong side of the scaling cycle — 5/10.** Honest self-labeling as "analogical / Medium." The Dreamer-latent / NeRF→3DGS / AV-BEV pattern is real and the "survivors found compact codes" reading is the synthesis's best historical insight. Deductions: (a) it is an analogy, not evidence about *this* substrate at *manipulation* scale; (b) it elides that AV moved to sparse occupancy/BEV *and still kept occupancy as the representation* — which arguably supports "sparse occupancy" (the Risk-note octree) rather than refuting occupancy wholesale; the card's hierarchical-occupancy escape hatch already absorbs most of this blow, weakening the finding's bite.

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 4 (latency axis).** Least reliable because its central comparator is numerically wrong (0.12s/10-step misread as 0.1s/step) and the dense-voxel side is entirely unmeasured — the claim "dense voxels may lose per wall-clock-second" rests on a phantom number on one side and an 8x-inflated number on the other. To verify: (i) re-read PointWorld's reported inference cost and normalize to per-step *and* per-MPC-rollout-budget; (ii) obtain or estimate a dense sub-cm voxel-occupancy forward pass cost on a RoboCasa workspace at the resolution H2 demands (the card never states the grid size, so the memory/latency claim cannot even be order-of-magnitude checked); (iii) only then is "per wall-clock-second" a finding rather than a conjecture. Until both numbers exist on one clock, this is an open experiment, which is exactly what the actionable insight (correctly) proposes.

##### 3. Bias check

**Skeptic over-dominated.** The analyst itself crowned Skeptic "strongest" and Economist "weakest" (Contradiction Map §2), and the synthesis inherits that ranking wholesale: the CEO summary, the "voxel qualifier is the entire moat," the de-risk verdict, and the frontier question all run on Skeptic's single anchor (PointWorld). The Practitioner is co-opted into the same conclusion via the (mis-stated) latency number, and the Hidden-connection paragraph fuses Practitioner+Historian+Economist into "Skeptic was right, pivot to benchmark." Academic — the only voice defending the conjunction as genuinely unrun — is repeatedly cast as the lone dissenter and given the last-challenged slot in three of five findings. The result is a synthesis that argues *against* committing A2 with more conviction than the evidence (one Jan-2026 paper, one misread latency figure, one analogy) actually licenses. A balanced read would have given Academic's "the head-to-head is genuinely unrun and that is publishable" equal standing with Skeptic's "points already won."

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The Controls / planning-theory perspective (collision-queryability as a first-class planning primitive).** Every voice here treats the substrate as a *forecasting-accuracy* question (which representation drifts slowest, cheapest). None asks the planner-side question the frontier paragraph gestures at but no perspective owns: **does an explicit occupancy grid give the MPC/MPPI sampler a constant-time collision and free-space query that a point set or Gaussian cloud cannot, independent of horizon?** If voxel occupancy's value is O(1) collision-checking and signed-distance queries inside the sampling loop (the reason AV kept occupancy even after going sparse), then "voxel = point = Gaussian once matched on compute" is false at the *planning* layer regardless of who forecasts farther — and the latency penalty might be *bought back* by cheaper per-sample feasibility checks, inverting Finding 4. This single perspective could flip the verdict from "de-risk into a benchmark" to "the dense-voxel bet has a real, untested planning-side moat," because it is the one advantage of a grid that the entire synthesis prices at zero.

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B.**

A genuinely strong, well-grounded adversarial synthesis: it correctly demotes the premise, names the real competitor, surfaces a non-obvious blind spot (the depth+URDF self-consistency confound, which the card *does* leave open and is the single most original contribution here), and proposes a decisive single experiment. It loses a full letter for three things a Stanford committee would catch: a misread load-bearing number, a one-perspective tilt, and a slightly over-confident "de-risk, do not commit" verdict that the evidence (one recent paper + one analogy) does not fully earn.

Top fixes, in priority order:
1. **Correct the PointWorld latency figure** (0.12s/10-step, not 0.1s/step) everywhere it appears, and label Finding 4 as a hypothesis until a dense-voxel number exists on the same clock. The latency argument is currently the synthesis's most-cited and least-true claim.
2. **Re-rate Finding 3 down and re-frame the PointWorld critique** as *cross-cluster siloing* (A2 fails to import its own C3 anchor as a substrate baseline) rather than "ignored the competitor" — the doc demonstrably engages PointWorld eight times. This is the honest, and still damning, version.
3. **Add the controls/collision-queryability perspective** before declaring "de-risk into a benchmark." If the grid's planning-side query advantage survives the latency penalty, the commit-vs-drop verdict flips, and the synthesis currently cannot rule that out.
4. **Promote the self-consistency confound** (depth+URDF pseudo-GT making the ≥10x partly definitional) from a buried blind-spot to a headline finding — it is the most damaging *original* observation in the whole review and is more decision-relevant than the historical analogy (Finding 5).

---

### A3 (Direction review)

Topic: depth-token side-channel into a frozen 2D-pretrained VLA, where the deliverable is the *measurement* nobody has produced, (i) a recovery-fraction-vs-added-params knee that recovers ≥80% of GeoVLA's full-3D-branch SR gain well below full-branch cost, and (ii) a frozen-backbone side-channel that perturbs a held-out VQA semantic-alignment probe measurably less than full-branch fusion. The bet is the *frontier + alignment-cost number*, not the existence claim PointVLA settled.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The bet is real but the framing undersells the actual deployment win, the thing a practitioner cares about is not "≥80% of GeoVLA's gain" but "no depth sensor at test time and no re-training of the checkpoint I already paid for." The recovery-fraction curve is a nice-to-have; the H5 train-time-only lane (Spatial Forcing 98.5% LIBERO, zero inference cost) is the operationally dominant answer and it half-kills the inference-depth thesis.
- *Strongest evidence*: Spatial Forcing [[2510.12276]] hits 98.5% LIBERO at 3.8× faster training / 5.9× more data-efficient with *no 3D at inference*; SwiftVLA [[2512.00903]] drops the whole 4D branch and still gets 94.7% LIBERO at 18× faster / 12× less memory on Jetson Orin; GeoAlign [[2606.03240]] reaches 99.0% LIBERO with the depth encoder *dropped at execution*. If the cheapest deployable form needs no depth at test, the depth-token side-channel A3 measures is the *more expensive* option.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Depth-token quality is hostage to the monocular depth estimator on the real robot, LIBERO's clean sim depth is not the deployment distribution; the entire H1 knee can be a sim artifact that collapses under real estimator noise. Report the curve *under estimator noise* or it is not a deployment claim.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The existence claim is thoroughly settled (the card concedes this), and the two surviving deliverables are genuinely unmeasured, but the literature is converging on "geometry as a frozen-backbone side-task" so fast that the contribution is a *measurement paper*, not a method paper, and must be sold as such. The card's own table is the strongest evidence *against* its novelty headline.
- *Strongest evidence*: PointVLA [[2503.07511]] states A3's mechanism + installed-base motivation verbatim (frozen action expert, skip-block injection, no retraining); Depth Helps [[2408.05107]] pre-answers H2 (quantized depth costs only 0.8% to drop); Lift3D [[2411.18623]] is a 2024 precedent for "missing channel, not wrong backbone" + 2D-prior-preserving distillation. GST [[2603.09079]] already beats the DepthVLA parallel stream with a *richer* token (+9.2pp precision-insertion) — so "a depth token helps" and even "a better depth token beats the parallel branch" are both done.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: H4 leans on SITE's 0.902 Pearson VQA→LIBERO-SR correlation as the bridge that makes a VQA-probe drift *predictive* of manipulation SR — that single borrowed correlation is the entire load-bearing assumption of the alignment-preservation deliverable, and it was measured on a different setup. If that correlation does not hold on A3's backbone, H4 measures nothing actionable.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The "nobody has drawn the frontier / measured the alignment cost" whitespace is narrower than the card claims and is being actively closed. A systematic injection-strategy comparison already exists, and the recovery-fraction story may be uninteresting because the field has already routed *around* inference-depth entirely.
- *Strongest evidence (the evidence the card under-weights)*: 3D-MIX [[2603.24393]] runs a 9-scheme fusion pilot (Early / Gated / 3D-Tokens / Spatial-Forcing / Middle-layer / Visual / AE / Concat / CrossAttn fusion), sweeps backbone scale 2B→8B across nine MLLM variants, ablates frozen-vs-trainable VGGT, corrupts 3D features to isolate the gain, and does a sparse-layer-fusion-depth memory trade-off — i.e. it *already* maps "where/how geometry is injected" and a coarse cost/memory frontier. It does not plot recovery-fraction vs a GeoVLA full-3D ceiling, nor a VQA-alignment probe, so A3's two specific numbers survive — but the "injection strategies have never been systematically compared" framing is now false.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: H3 (a second view closes the residual to full-3D) and H1 (the knee) may be in tension — if a second view closes the gap, the "cheap single-view knee" is not the real operating point, and if it does not, the bridge ceiling is intrinsic and the whole "≥80% recovery" target may be unreachable on the hardest tasks. The card never reconciles H1 and H3.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The narrative that wins funding and citations is "reuse the installed base, no re-training, no sensor" — which is exactly the train-time-distillation lane (Spatial Forcing / ROCKET / VEGA), not the inference-depth-token lane A3 centers. The compute-efficiency framing (ROCKET 98.5% at ~4% compute) is the citation magnet, and A3's measurement paper competes for attention against method papers that post a single SOTA number.
- *Strongest evidence*: ROCKET [[2602.17951]] sells 98.5% LIBERO at ~4% of prior-SOTA compute + 81.7% LIBERO-Plus robustness — a clean headline that a frontier-measurement paper cannot match. The proliferation of LIBERO-saturated numbers (97-99%: GeoVLA 97.7, VLA-JEPA 97.2, GeoAlign 99.0, Spatial Forcing/ROCKET 98.5) means LIBERO is benchmark-saturated and a recovery-fraction *read off LIBERO* will be measuring differences in the noise floor.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: A measurement/frontier paper has weak defensibility — the moment you publish the knee, the method-paper authors (QDepth-VLA, GeoVLA, GST) add one figure and absorb your contribution into their next revision. The economic moat is the *probe + protocol* (a reusable alignment-preservation benchmark), not the knee itself. Ship the benchmark, not the plot.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle and the modular-vs-end-to-end swing replayed on geometry injection. Every wave starts with an explicit external channel (here: sensed point clouds / depth tokens), then the field discovers it can be distilled into the backbone at zero inference cost (here: Spatial Forcing / VEGA / SwiftVLA), and the explicit channel survives only where the latent version provably fails.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card's own lane structure recapitulates the arc — explicit point branch (GeoVLA, PointAction) → in-loop / side-channel depth (QDepth-VLA, GST) → train-time-only distillation (Spatial Forcing, ROCKET, VEGA, SwiftVLA, WAM4D). Historically (Dreamer/latent-RL, DR/sim2real) the latent/implicit form wins on cost and the explicit form retreats to a diagnostic role.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The durable contribution from past waves was never the winning architecture (it gets superseded in ~12 months) — it was the *measurement methodology* that let the field decide which lane wins. A3's instinct to ship the *measurement* is historically the right bet; its error is anchoring that measurement to inference-depth (the lane history says retreats) rather than to the implicit-vs-explicit *crossover itself*.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Direct clashes.**
- *Practitioner & Historian vs the card's center of gravity*: both say the inference-depth-token channel is the lane that history/economics retreats from; the cheapest deployable form (H5) needs no depth at test (Spatial Forcing 98.5%, SwiftVLA 94.7% branch-dropped). The card makes H1/H4 (the inference channel) the "front-line" and demotes H5 — the contradiction is which hypothesis is actually load-bearing.
- *Skeptic vs Academic on whitespace size*: Skeptic says 3D-MIX [[2603.24393]] already systematically compared injection strategies (9 schemes × backbone scale × frozen/trainable × sparse-depth), shrinking the whitespace; Academic agrees the *two specific numbers* (recovery-fraction-vs-GeoVLA knee, VQA-alignment drift) survive. The clash: is the contribution "a frontier nobody drew" (still novel) or "two missing cells in a table 3D-MIX mostly filled" (incremental)?
- *Internal H1 vs H3 tension* (Skeptic): if H3's second view closes the residual to full-3D, the cheap single-view knee (H1) is not the deployable operating point.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest.**
- *Strongest*: SKEPTIC — it is the only perspective grounded in a *specific uncited paper* (3D-MIX) that directly contests the novelty framing, and it surfaces a real internal inconsistency (H1 vs H3). Evidence-bound and adversarial.
- *Weakest*: ECONOMIST — directionally right about defensibility and LIBERO saturation, but its claims are about *incentives*, not about whether the experiment succeeds; it cannot by itself kill or save the bet.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
On one common backbone, does an inference-time depth-token channel recover *materially more* of GeoVLA's full-3D SR gain than train-time-only distillation (Spatial Forcing / SwiftVLA) recovers at *zero* inference cost — on geometry-critical tasks under realistic depth-estimator noise? If no, the entire inference-depth lane (H1/H4) is dominated by H5 and the paper should pivot to "the implicit-vs-explicit crossover," resolving the Practitioner/Historian-vs-card clash. This is exactly A3's H5 falsifier, which means H5 — not H1 — is the real decision experiment.

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
The existence claim is dead: injecting geometry into a frozen 2D-pretrained VLA without re-training works, and the two unmeasured quantities A3 names (a recovery-fraction-vs-cost frontier against a full-3D ceiling, and a quantified semantic-alignment-preservation number) genuinely have not been published. The *measurement* is the only defensible contribution; "a depth bridge helps" is not a paper.

**(5) The blind spot (addressed by none).**
No perspective — and not the card — addresses *cross-embodiment / cross-backbone transfer of the frontier itself*. Every number is single-backbone LIBERO. 2606.14153 ("Encoder Winners Do Not Reliably Transfer Across VLA Backbone Scale") shows encoder rankings do *not* transfer from a 450M to a 3.3B VLA — strongly implying the recovery-fraction *knee location* is backbone-specific and a single-backbone knee may not generalize. If the knee moves with scale, the headline deliverable is not a frontier but a point estimate.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.**
A3 is a *measurement* bet, not a method bet, and on that narrow ground it is still defensible: nobody has published a recovery-fraction-vs-added-params knee against a full-3D point-cloud ceiling (GeoVLA) on a common backbone, nor a quantified semantic-alignment-preservation number for a frozen-backbone depth side-channel. But the whitespace is meaningfully narrower than the card's framing: 3D-MIX (2603.24393, uncited) has already run a 9-scheme injection-strategy comparison with a backbone-scale sweep and a memory/cost frontier, and the field's momentum (Spatial Forcing, ROCKET, SwiftVLA, GeoAlign — all 94-99% LIBERO at zero inference 3D) is toward the *train-time-distillation* lane that A3's own H5 admits may dominate the inference-depth lane it centers. The honest contribution is therefore the implicit-vs-explicit *crossover under realistic depth noise* plus a reusable alignment-preservation probe — not the single-view depth-token knee. LIBERO saturation (97-99% everywhere) means the knee must be read off a harder, noisier surface or it measures noise.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *The existence claim is settled; only the measurement is novel.* (Supported: all five perspectives, card concedes it. Challenged: none.) Most reliable — PointVLA/Depth Helps/Lift3D state mechanism + cost verbatim.
2. *The two specific numbers (recovery-fraction-vs-GeoVLA knee, VQA-alignment drift) are genuinely unpublished.* (Supported: Academic, Skeptic, alphaxiv search returned no paper plotting either. Challenged mildly: Skeptic notes 3D-MIX got close on injection-strategy comparison and corrupts-features ablation.) Reliable — the whitespace survives but is narrow.
3. *Train-time-only distillation (H5) likely dominates the inference-depth channel (H1/H4) on cost.* (Supported: Practitioner, Economist, Historian; Spatial Forcing 98.5% / SwiftVLA 94.7%-branch-dropped / GeoAlign 99.0%-dropped. Challenged: card makes H1/H4 the front-line.) Reliable enough to reorder the hypotheses.
4. *The knee is probably backbone-specific and may not transfer.* (Supported: Skeptic via 2606.14153 encoder-rankings-don't-transfer-across-scale. Challenged: none directly; nobody else raised it — it is the blind spot.) Moderately reliable, untested.
5. *LIBERO saturation makes a LIBERO-read knee measure the noise floor.* (Supported: Economist; 97-99% cluster. Challenged: card targets LIBERO-Spatial/long-horizon specifically, which are less saturated.) Plausible, partially mitigated by suite choice.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.**
The Historian's "the durable artifact is the measurement methodology, not the architecture" and the Economist's "the moat is the probe/protocol, not the knee" are the *same insight* arriving from opposite directions (history vs incentives). Both independently conclude A3 should ship a *reusable alignment-preservation benchmark + crossover protocol*, not a one-off plot — and the Academic's worry (the SITE 0.902 correlation is borrowed and unverified on A3's backbone) tells you the *first deliverable* of that benchmark: re-establish the VQA→SR correlation on the actual backbone before trusting any drift number.

**(4) Actionable insight: DE-RISK, then COMMIT to a reframed version.**
Do not COMMIT as written (the inference-depth knee is the lane history/economics retreats from, and 3D-MIX narrows the framing). Do not DROP (the two numbers are real whitespace). DE-RISK by reordering the hypotheses and running H5 *first* as the decision gate. Concrete next experiment: on ONE common backbone (e.g. open_pi_0, matching QDepth-VLA), train three variants — inference depth-token channel (QDepth-style), train-time-only distillation (Spatial-Forcing-style), and the GeoVLA full-3D branch ceiling — and plot recovery-fraction-vs-inference-cost for all three on LIBERO-Spatial + long-horizon *and* on a held-out geometry-critical task under injected depth-estimator noise. If train-time-only recovers comparable fraction at zero inference cost (H5 holds), the paper's headline becomes "the implicit-vs-explicit crossover + where the explicit channel still wins," which is both novel and aligned with where the field is going. Bundle the SITE-style VQA-alignment probe (after re-verifying the VQA→SR correlation on this backbone) as the reusable artifact — that is the defensible moat, not the knee.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.**
Does the recovery-fraction knee *and* the implicit-vs-explicit crossover point move with backbone scale and embodiment — i.e. is there a single transferable frontier at all, or only per-backbone point estimates (as 2606.14153's non-transferring encoder rankings imply)? If the frontier is universal, A3 becomes a foundational measurement paper; if it is backbone-specific, the entire "draw the frontier" premise collapses into "report one number for one model," and the field needs a scaling law for geometry-injection cost-efficiency instead.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded against `Mechanism/Spatial-4D.md` lines 261-345 (A3) and independent verification of 3D-MIX (2603.24393), GeoVLA (2508.09071), and the train-time-distillation cluster.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability of each of the 5 key findings)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---------|-------|------------------------|
| 1 | The existence claim is settled; only the measurement is novel | **9/10** | The card itself concedes this in three places (Thesis "PointVLA settled that", FP "settled false", H-preamble). PointVLA / Depth Helps / Lift3D state mechanism + drop-cost verbatim. The one deduction: "only the measurement is novel" is *itself* the contestable claim (see finding 2), so calling the existence-claim "settled" is safe but slightly self-serving as a framing. |
| 2 | The two specific numbers (recovery-fraction-vs-GeoVLA knee, VQA-alignment drift) are genuinely unpublished | **6/10** | I verified 3D-MIX directly: it runs 9 injection schemes across 6 MLLM series / 9 variants (2B-8B) on LIBERO+SIMPLER with a memory/cost frontier and a "sparse fusion ≈ full fusion at lower cost" result. It does **not** plot recovery-fraction vs a full-3D point-cloud ceiling, does **not** measure VQA/alignment drift, and does **not** inject depth-estimator noise. So both A3 numbers survive — but the *first* number's surrounding methodology (capacity/backbone sweep + cost frontier for 3D injection) is now substantially pre-occupied. The briefing's body grades this correctly ("narrow"); its CEO-summary phrasing inflates the overlap. Net: whitespace is real but thinner than a "6/10-on-novelty" reader would assume from the headline. |
| 3 | Train-time-only distillation (H5) likely dominates the inference-depth channel on cost | **7/10** | Strongly supported by the card's own evidence cluster (Spatial Forcing 98.5%, ROCKET 98.5% @ ~4% compute, SwiftVLA 94.7% branch-dropped, GeoAlign 99.0% dropped, VEGA, DynaFLIP, WAM4D). The reorder-hypotheses recommendation is sound. Deduction: "dominates on cost" is an *inference-cost* claim; none of these papers reports the recovery-fraction *under realistic depth noise on a held-out geometry-critical task*, which is exactly where the explicit channel is hypothesized to still win (H1/H3). So H5 dominating on clean LIBERO is near-certain; H5 dominating on the surface that actually decides A3's contribution is unproven. The briefing conflates these slightly. |
| 4 | The knee is probably backbone-specific and may not transfer | **5/10** | This is the right worry and the briefing is honest that it is "untested, the blind spot." But it rests on a single transplanted analogy (2606.14153's non-transferring encoder rankings), and 3D-MIX is mild counter-evidence the briefing did not weigh: 3D-MIX reports *consistent* +7.0% across all nine 2B-8B variants, which is weak evidence that *some* geometry-injection benefit does transfer across backbone scale even if the precise knee location does not. The finding is plausible and important but under-evidenced in both directions. |
| 5 | LIBERO saturation makes a LIBERO-read knee measure the noise floor | **6/10** | Correct and verified-worse-than-stated: the GeoVLA *ceiling* A3 measures recovery-fraction against is itself 97.7% (LIBERO-90 / overall avg), sitting in the same 97-99% cluster as the implicit methods. So both the numerator (bridge) and denominator (full-3D ceiling) live in the saturation band — a recovery-*fraction* of two near-identical near-ceiling numbers is a ratio of two noise-dominated quantities, which is worse than "the knee measures noise." The briefing names the saturation but mitigates it only on the bridge side (LIBERO-Spatial/long-horizon) and does not flag that the *ceiling* is saturated too. Partially mitigated, partially under-stated. |

**Mean = 6.6. Lowest = 5 (finding 4).**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 4 (the knee is backbone-specific and may not transfer) — confidence 5/10.** It is the load-bearing premise of the entire reframe: the briefing's Frontier Question (§5) explicitly says if the knee is backbone-specific the whole "draw the frontier" premise collapses into "report one number for one model." Yet the only evidence is a borrowed analogy (2606.14153 encoder-ranking non-transfer), and there is unweighed counter-evidence (3D-MIX's consistent gain across nine 2B-8B variants). **To verify, the needed information is a single concrete experiment the card already half-specifies but never runs: replicate the H1 capacity sweep on ≥2 backbones of materially different scale/family (e.g. open_pi_0 and a GR00T-style 2B and ~7B) and test whether the *knee location* (params at which ≥80% recovery is hit), not just the gain sign, is stable.** Until that exists, finding 4 is an assertion, and because §5 hangs the paper's significance on it, the whole "foundational vs one-off" framing is unfalsified.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Economist and Historian over-dominated; the synthesis is moat-pilled.** The "Hidden cross-perspective connection" (§3) explicitly fuses Historian + Economist into one conclusion ("ship a reusable probe/protocol, not a one-off plot"), and §4's actionable insight ("the moat is the probe, not the knee") and §5's framing all descend from that same incentive-flavored reasoning. The Practitioner shows up only as a co-signer of H5-dominance. The **Academic perspective is structurally subordinated**: its one distinct contribution (the SITE 0.902 VQA→SR correlation is borrowed and unverified on A3's backbone) is correctly surfaced but immediately *absorbed* into the Economist's deliverable ("names the first deliverable of that benchmark") rather than allowed to stand as an independent threat to H4's validity. That absorption is convenient — it converts a potential falsifier of H4 into a feature of the moat. The **Skeptic is the most under-weighted**: findings 4 and 5 (both Skeptic-flavored) carry the two lowest scores and are the only two with "Challenged: none," meaning the synthesis let the skeptical claims through *without adversarial pressure*, which is itself a bias (unchallenged ≠ verified). A genuinely balanced synthesis would have stress-tested finding 4 against 3D-MIX's transfer evidence rather than parking it as "the blind spot."

---

##### 4. Missing perspective (a 6th angle that would change the conclusions)

**The hardware/sensing-realist (a deployment-systems perspective distinct from the cost-Economist).** Every number in A3 — and every method in the table — derives its depth from one of two regimes: *sensed* depth (PointVLA's real point clouds, RGB-D codebooks) or *estimated* depth (QDepth's VQ tokens from a monocular/learned estimator, AugVLA/VGGT, Evo-Depth FiLM). A3's central bet is that quantization "buffers estimator noise" (H2, R2) — but the entire knee, recovery-fraction, and crossover are measured on benchmarks (LIBERO, SIMPLER) where depth is rendered/clean. The missing perspective asks: *on a real robot with a real depth sensor (RealSense noise, specular/transparent-object dropout, metric-scale drift), does the inference-depth channel's advantage survive at all, or does sensor reality erase the very gap that justifies paying inference cost?* This changes the conclusion because it could **invert** the §4 recommendation: if real-sensor noise collapses the explicit channel's edge, then H5 (train-time-only, zero inference depth, no sensor at test) does not merely "dominate on cost" — it dominates on *robustness*, and the explicit-channel lane A3 centers becomes a benchmark artifact. Conversely, if estimated depth is the realistic regime (no depth sensor at all), then "depth-token bridge" and "train-time distillation" are *both* really about monocular depth-estimator quality, and the honest axis is estimator-quality-vs-recovery, not params-vs-recovery. Neither the briefing nor the card distinguishes sensed-vs-estimated as a first-class variable, yet it gates which of the five hypotheses even has a deployable answer.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a careful, intellectually honest review that correctly identifies A3 as a measurement bet, correctly flags the train-time-distillation lane as the field's momentum, and correctly de-risks rather than rubber-stamps. It does real verification work (3D-MIX is a genuine find that the card missed). It loses the A-range on three counts: (i) the CEO-summary overstates 3D-MIX's overlap relative to the body, a calibration drift that a reader skimming only §1 would be misled by; (ii) the synthesis is moat-biased — Historian+Economist drive the conclusion and the Academic's H4-falsifier is absorbed rather than confronted; (iii) the two most decision-critical claims (findings 4 and 5) are the least evidenced *and* the least challenged, and the briefing treats "unchallenged" as a strength rather than a gap.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**

1. **Reconcile §1 with §2 on 3D-MIX.** State in the CEO summary exactly what 3D-MIX does *not* do (no full-3D ceiling, no alignment drift, no depth-noise), so the headline whitespace claim matches the verified one. As written, §1 implies 3D-MIX nearly closes H1's methodology; it does not.
2. **Promote the sensed-vs-estimated depth axis to a first-class variable** (the missing 6th perspective). It determines whether H2/H3/H5 have deployable answers and whether the explicit channel's edge is a real-robot artifact. Without it, "under realistic depth noise" (the briefing's own honest-contribution framing) is undefined.
3. **Make finding 4 an experiment, not a worry.** Run the H1 sweep on ≥2 backbones before claiming a "frontier." Per §5, the paper's significance is binary on this; it should be the *first* gate, alongside H5, not deferred to "the blind spot."
4. **Fix the saturated-ceiling problem, not just the saturated-benchmark problem.** GeoVLA's 97.7% ceiling is itself in the noise band; recovery-fraction is a ratio of two near-ceiling numbers. Either pick a non-saturated ceiling/task or report absolute SR-at-cost alongside the fraction so the knee is not a ratio of noise.
5. **Confront the H4 falsifier instead of absorbing it.** If the SITE 0.902 VQA→SR correlation does not replicate on A3's backbone, the alignment-preservation number is uninterpretable — that is a kill-switch for the second of A3's two headline numbers, and it deserves to be a standalone risk, not a line item inside the Economist's deliverable.

---

### B (Cluster review)

Topic: the coherence + central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster B — "3D-Grounded Cognition": *reasoning happens over explicit metric geometry, upstream of the action head; B1 grounds geometry in space (a scene-graph), B2 in time (4D consistency), and both supply the upstream metric structure Cluster A's action heads consume.* Shared bottleneck (Cluster-Overview row): "Reasoning isn't grounded in metric geometry: language CoT over RGB hallucinates spatial relations and dynamics." Is this carved at the right joint, and is the shared bet sound?

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily)**
- *Core position*: The cluster's value is real but its two halves live on opposite sides of the deploy line. B2 (implicit 4D-consistency) is deployable today — ConsisVLA-4D ([[2605.05126]]) runs at 0.110s/step (2.31× faster than OpenVLA) and hits 70.0% real bimanual vs OpenVLA's 28.5%, GAM ([[2606.17046]]) is 6.9 ms/pass. B1 (scene-graph CoT) is a perception-side reasoning layer that adds a two-step prompt and a graph-construction pass per decision, with no demonstrated closed-loop latency budget.
- *Strongest evidence*: B2's real-robot numbers are concrete and the cost frontier is measured (WAM-vs-VLA [[2603.22078]]: WAMs ≥4.8× slower than π0.5's 63ms/chunk). B1's strongest deployable artifact is Trust-Spatial's edge-precision lift (52.1%→78.3%, [[2601.11644]]) — but that is a confidence estimator, not a policy.
- *Only this perspective says*: The B1→B2→A handoff the cluster bets on (scene-graph coords feeding an action head, H4) has never been clocked end-to-end. A graph that is correct but arrives 400ms late is useless for contact-rich control; the cluster never states a reasoning-latency budget, and B1's per-decision graph construction may simply not fit a 10Hz control loop.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature)**
- *Core position*: The diagnostic half of the cluster is on firm ground — multiple independent probes converge that VLMs describe rather than reason about metric space (CausalSpatial [[2601.13304]] GPT-5 54.17% vs human 84.49%; Why Far Looks Up [[2605.30161]] 36.9pp shortcut gap; ReVSI [[2604.24300]] absent-object hallucination). The *prescriptive* half is where the card overclaims consensus: "structure+RL+OOD-generalizes" is well-supported, but "structure beats prose" already has a counterexample the card itself records.
- *Strongest evidence*: VSR Information Injection Analysis ([[2602.21619]]) shows naive *numerical/metric* text injection into a frozen model backfires (−9.2%), and verbalized beats numerical — which directly complicates B1's bet that *metric* content is the load-bearing component. The metric win must come from a *learned* graph, not metric prose, and the card acknowledges this but does not resolve it.
- *Only this perspective says*: The two directions are not symmetric in maturity. B2's first principle ("consistency ≠ rendering every frame") is stated *verbatim* by a prior paper (GeoAware [[2509.14117]]: "generalization across views is tied to the geometric acuity of the visual encoder") — so B2's novelty is narrowed to the *temporal* + *consistency-vs-perception isolation* slice, not the thesis itself.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved)**
- *Core position*: The cluster's grouping is sound but its shared bet may be *measuring an artifact*. The strongest counter-evidence is that when geometry injection into VLAs is isolated rigorously, it mostly does NOT help: Understanding GFMs on VLAs ([[2605.24642]], Amazon/MIT) finds Early/Late-Fusion/Spatial-Forcing of VGGT into GR00T-N1.5 yields *no statistically significant* sim gain (69.7% vs 71.7% baseline, p=0.399) and only marginal, non-significant real gains. If explicit geometry barely moves the needle once you control for confounds, the cluster's "geometry is the missing substrate" framing is on shakier ground than its tables suggest.
- *Strongest evidence*: The same paper's linear-probe quantifies the "geometric gap" (GR00T VLM RMSE 0.73m vs VGGT 0.41m) yet shows closing that probe-gap does NOT translate to manipulation success — a direct challenge to the cluster's load-bearing assumption that grounding reasoning in metric geometry is what unlocks downstream performance.
- *Only this perspective says*: The cluster conveniently reports OOD/SR *wins* but never the null results. B2's whole case rests on ConsisVLA-4D's single-source 2.31× cost claim (the card flags this as a risk but still builds the bet on it). The skeptic's read: the consistency-vs-perception isolation (B2-H1) may come back showing *perception* drives OOD as much as consistency — which is the card's own falsifier, and 2605.24642 is the leading indicator that it might.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives)**
- *Core position*: This cluster is benchmark-shaped. The targets are LIBERO ≥98.1%, CVBench ≥77.69%, 3DSRBench, RoboTwin 2.0 — suites that are saturating, which is precisely *why* the field is pivoting to OOD splits (LIBERO-Plus [[2510.13626]], LIBERO-X [[2602.06556]]) and metric-causal diagnostics (CausalSpatial). The incentive gradient rewards "new geometric module + new OOD benchmark" papers, and B1/B2 are well-positioned to ride it.
- *Strongest evidence*: The proliferation of near-identical results (ConsisVLA-4D 98.1%, OptimusVLA 98.6%, GTA-VLA 98.6%, VLA-4D 97.4% on LIBERO) is textbook saturation — the differentiator has migrated entirely to OOD retention (GAM's 12.1pp drop vs baselines' 16.1–60.9%). A bet framed as an *isolation/ablation* contribution (which property drives the gain) is grant-attractive precisely because it cannot be beaten by the next +0.5% LIBERO number.
- *Only this perspective says*: The danger is that "metric scene-graph" and "4D-consistency" are crowded narratives (Huawei Noah's Ark, Shanghai AI Lab, and HIT each ship a variant every cycle). The defensible economic moat is NOT another high-SR model — it's the *negative/isolation result* (H1, H5) that the SR-chasing labs have no incentive to run. The cluster's value is highest where the field's incentives are weakest.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves)**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle replayed in the VLA era. B2's "implicit consistency matches explicit generation at lower cost" is the Dreamer-style latent-imagination bet (latent rollout beats pixel rollout) ported to 4D; B1's "explicit symbolic graph upstream of a learned head" is the modular-vs-end-to-end pendulum swinging back toward structure.
- *Strongest evidence*: The card's own table encodes the cycle — explicit-frame-generation rows (STARRY [[2604.26848]], MV-VDP [[2604.03181]], EA-WM [[2605.06192]]) vs implicit-attention rows (ConsisVLA-4D, VLA-JEPA [[2602.10098]], LaST0 [[2601.05248]]) is exactly the pixel-vs-latent split, and H4 (horizon crossover) is the historically recurring finding that latent wins mid-horizon and explicit wins long-horizon (the Dreamer-vs-MPC boundary).
- *Only this perspective says*: Every prior structure-vs-scale swing resolved not by "structure wins" but by "scale absorbs structure." The historical risk for B1 is that a big-enough geometry-pretrained VLM (G²VLM [[2511.21688]] already unifies reconstruction + reasoning, surpassing GPT-4o by 18.48 on SPAR-Bench) makes the *explicit* scene-graph redundant — the graph becomes an implicit feature, and B1's "metric edges carry the gain" becomes unmeasurable inside the latent (the card flags this exact failure mode for Spa3R/GeoThinker).

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes**:
   - *Skeptic vs Practitioner/Academic on whether geometry helps at all*: Skeptic cites 2605.24642 (geometry injection → no significant sim gain, p=0.399); Practitioner cites ConsisVLA-4D's 70.0% real bimanual and GAM's 12.1pp OOD drop. The clash is real because both cite rigorous evidence — the difference is *injection-vs-baseline* (2605.24642 found null) vs *full-architecture-redesign* (ConsisVLA found wins), which is unresolved within the cluster.
   - *Academic vs the card's B1 bet on metric content*: VSR Injection ([[2602.21619]]) shows metric/numerical content *hurts* a frozen model (−9.2%); B1-H1 bets metric edges *carry the gain*. They are reconcilable only if the gain requires a *learned* graph — but that distinction is exactly what B1 has not yet isolated.
   - *Historian vs Economist on durability*: Historian says scale will absorb the explicit graph (G²VLM); Economist says the isolation result is the durable contribution precisely because SR-chasing labs won't run it. They clash on whether B1's artifact (a graph) or its finding (which property matters) is the asset.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective**: Strongest = **Skeptic**, because it is anchored to the one paper (2605.24642) that controls for confounds the entire cluster's win-tables do not, and it lands on the cluster's *own* stated falsifiers (B1-H1, B2-H1). Weakest = **Economist**, useful for framing but it argues about incentives, not about whether the bet is *true*; its claims don't constrain the experiment.

3. **Question that resolves the biggest contradiction**: When consistency (B2-H1) or metric-edges (B1-H1) are ablated with *perception held fixed and statistically tested* (McNemar-style, as in 2605.24642), does the consistency/metric arm collapse OOD SR *more* than the perceptual arm — or does the geometry arm wash out like it did for GFM-injection? This single isolation adjudicates Skeptic-vs-Practitioner *and* validates-or-kills both directions' central bet.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true)**: The *diagnostic* claim — current VLMs/VLAs reason about metric space lossily and hallucinate when ungrounded (CausalSpatial 54.17% vs 84.49%, Why Far Looks Up 36.9pp, ReVSI absent-object hallucination, GR00T probe-gap 0.73m vs 0.41m). The gap is real; the dispute is only over the *fix* and whether geometry-grounding is the lever that closes it.

5. **Blind spot (none addressed)**: Dynamic / multi-agent / deformable scenes. Every paper and both directions assume a quasi-static scene of rigid objects whose graph or 4D-trajectory is well-defined. None addresses scene-graph construction or 4D-consistency when objects are *non-rigid, articulated, or moved by other agents* — exactly the case where both "a graph over geometric entities" and "the same object on a coherent 4D trajectory" stop being well-posed. The cluster's first principles silently assume rigid-body, single-agent worlds.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** Cluster B carves the problem at a defensible joint: it separates the *spatial* (B1, scene-graph) and *temporal* (B2, 4D-consistency) halves of one "ground reasoning in metric geometry" thesis, and the diagnostic evidence that this gap exists is overwhelming and uncontested. The shared bet, however, is not "add geometry" (which a rigorous Amazon/MIT study, 2605.24642, shows often yields no significant gain) but the sharper, mostly-unrun *isolation*: which property — metric edges over topological structure (B1), the consistency constraint over raw perception (B2) — actually carries the OOD gain, and where construction (not reasoning) is the bottleneck. That isolation is genuine whitespace and is grant-durable precisely because SR-chasing labs have no incentive to run it; but it is double-edged, since the same isolation is the cluster's own falsifier, and the leading external evidence (geometry-injection null results) suggests it could come back showing perception, not consistency/metric-content, is the driver. Commit to B2's isolation as the lead bet, treat B1 as a coupled second experiment, and front-load the falsifier.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability:**
1. *The diagnostic gap is real and metric-shaped* (CausalSpatial, Why Far Looks Up, ReVSI, GR00T linear-probe). **Support**: all five perspectives. **Challenge**: none — this is the cluster's bedrock.
2. *Saturation has migrated the contest to OOD retention, making an isolation/ablation contribution the durable play* (LIBERO clustered at 97–98.6%; differentiator is now OOD drop: GAM 12.1pp vs 16.1–60.9%). **Support**: Economist, Academic, Historian. **Challenge**: Practitioner (warns OOD wins still must clear a deploy-latency bar the cluster never states).
3. *B2's consistency-vs-perception isolation (H1) is the cluster's single highest-value, least-pre-empted experiment.* **Support**: Skeptic, Academic, Economist. **Challenge**: Practitioner (it rests on ConsisVLA-4D's single-source cost claim); Historian (scale may absorb it).
4. *Geometry injection, isolated and statistically tested, often does NOT help* (2605.24642: 69.7% vs 71.7%, p=0.399). **Support**: Skeptic. **Challenge**: Practitioner/Academic (full-architecture methods like ConsisVLA-4D/GAM redesign rather than inject, and do show wins) — the contradiction is itself the reason to run the isolation.
5. *B1's "metric content carries the gain" is the most contestable sub-bet* — VSR Injection shows metric prose backfires (−9.2%); the win, if it exists, needs a *learned* graph and is not yet dissociated from topology. **Support**: Academic, Historian. **Challenge**: the card's H1 prediction (untested).

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Skeptic's null result (2605.24642), the Economist's saturation argument, and the Historian's scale-absorbs-structure pattern are the *same phenomenon* viewed three ways: as benchmarks saturate, naive geometry/structure injection stops moving SR, so the field's apparent "geometry helps" wins are increasingly confounded — which is *exactly why* the cluster's isolation experiments (H1, H5) gain value as everything else loses it. The cluster's weakness (its win-tables are confounded) and its opportunity (the unrun isolation) are the same fact.

**Actionable insight for the researcher — DE-RISK, then COMMIT to B2-led.** Do not commit to the cluster as a whole or to B1 as the lead. Commit conditionally to **B2's consistency-vs-perception isolation (H1)** as the spearhead, with B1's metric-vs-topological ablation (H1) as a coupled second arm, because both share one experimental machinery (ablate property X with perception held fixed; report ID-vs-OOD delta with statistical testing). Concrete next experiment: replicate the 2605.24642 *protocol* (matched architecture, McNemar significance, frozen-perception control) but apply it to ConsisVLA-4D's consistency attention — ablate consistency with perceptual features held fixed on LIBERO-Plus's isolated camera-viewpoint axis and RoboTwin-2.0-Plus, measuring whether the consistency-ablation OOD drop exceeds the strongest single-perceptual-feature-ablation drop. De-risk first by re-deriving ConsisVLA-4D's 2.31× cost claim independently (the single-source risk) before generalizing. Go/no-go: if perception-ablation hurts OOD as much as consistency-ablation (the card's own falsifier, and 2605.24642's leading indicator), DROP the consistency-as-driver framing and pivot to the construction-bottleneck thread (B1-H5 / Trust-Spatial), which survives even if the consistency bet dies.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Does explicit metric structure (a scene-graph, a 4D-consistency constraint) provide any *durable* advantage that a sufficiently geometry-pretrained monolithic VLM (G²VLM-class, which already unifies reconstruction + reasoning) cannot absorb into its latent — i.e., is the explicit/implicit boundary a real, persistent axis, or a transient pre-scale artifact? If scale absorbs it, the entire cluster's "explicit upstream of the head" framing is a temporary scaffold; if it doesn't, B1+B2's isolation defines a permanent design axis.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Independent reviewer: skeptical robotics professor. Scope: Cluster B (`Mechanism/Spatial-4D.md` L346-533, B1 scene-graph + B2 4D-consistency) and the briefing built on it.*

**Verdict up front.** The cluster carving is sound and the diagnostic gap is bedrock. But the briefing's single most load-bearing rhetorical move — that a "rigorous Amazon/MIT study (2605.24642) shows geometry injection often yields no significant gain (69.7% vs 71.7%, p=0.399)" — is **not supported by the vault's own source note for that paper, and contradicts the paper's headline conclusion**. This is a contamination inherited verbatim from the analyst part (005-B-1-analyst.md) and never re-checked. It poisons Finding 4, weakens the "hidden cross-perspective connection," and inflates the Skeptic's standing in the synthesis. Everything else is solid. Grade reflects one serious, fixable factual defect on an otherwise strong briefing.

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

1. **Diagnostic gap is real and metric-shaped** — **9/10.** Bedrock, and I verified the supporting metrics: CausalSpatial GPT-5 54.17% vs human 84.49% (card L360), ReVSI dummy-video hallucination, Why-Far-Looks-Up 36.9pp shortcut (L362, L395), the GR00T linear-probe 0.73m vs 0.41m depth RMSE (KH-confirmed in 2605.24642 itself, L37). Five papers, four mechanisms, uncontested. Docked 1 only because "metric-shaped" is the cluster's framing of the gap, not a neutral fact: the same evidence is consistent with a *perception* deficit (the card's own H3/falsifier concedes this).

2. **Saturation migrated the contest to OOD retention** — **8/10.** LIBERO clustering at 97-98.6% is real across the table (ConsisVLA 98.1%, OptimusVLA/GTA-VLA 98.6%, GAM 97.6% — all KH-confirmed); GAM's 12.1pp-vs-16.1-60.9% OOD differentiator is genuine (KH-confirmed). The inference is sound. The Practitioner's challenge (OOD wins must clear an unstated deploy-latency bar) is legitimate and *under-weighted*: the cluster sells implicit-4D on a 2.31x speedup but never states the latency budget OOD wins must survive in, so "OOD retention is the durable play" is true for publication, not yet for deployment.

3. **B2's consistency-vs-perception isolation (H1) is the highest-value, least-pre-empted experiment** — **7/10.** I credit this. The cluster table genuinely shows no row runs it: VLA-4D ablates 3D-presence (coordinate chaos), GeoAware ablates VGGT-layer choice, GAM/WAM4D report architecture wins, ROCKET/ST4VLA/GenStudy-2512.02902 isolate the *perception* arm only (L491-493). The whitespace is real. Docked because the experiment's *feasibility* rests on ConsisVLA-4D being ablatable with perception held fixed, which is a single-source assumption (the card itself flags the 2.31x cost claim as single-source, L529), and because Finding 4's contamination (below) is what supposedly motivates running it.

4. **Geometry injection, isolated and statistically tested, often does NOT help (2605.24642: 69.7% vs 71.7%, p=0.399)** — **2/10.** This is the briefing's weakest link and I am confident it is *wrong as stated*. The KH note for 2605.24642 reports baseline RoboCasa 71.7% and **mid-training Early Fusion 75.2%, described as "statistically better"** (KH L39) — a *win*, not a null. The single-camera setting shows **+21.5%** GFM gain (KH L46). The G1 real result is 27.78% vs 22.22% baseline — also a gain. There is **no 69.7% figure, no p=0.399, and no McNemar test anywhere in the source note.** The paper's stated takeaway is "larger-scale training unlocks the potential of geometric VLAs, yielding statistically significant improvements." The "69.7%/p=0.399/null" framing traces to 005-B-1-analyst.md L18 and was carried forward un-rechecked. Separately: this paper is cited in `Spatial-4D.md` only at L105 and L301 — **inside Cluster A, not Cluster B** — so the briefing imports its central external falsifier from a *different cluster's* card. The 2 points are for the directional intuition (naive prompt-level metric injection *can* backfire) being independently corroborated by VSR-Injection 2602.21619's −9.2% (KH-confirmed) — but that is a different paper and a different mechanism (frozen-model prose injection, not GFM-feature fusion).

5. **B1's "metric content carries the gain" is the most contestable sub-bet** — **7/10.** Correctly flagged as contestable, and the supporting evidence is real: VSR-Injection's −9.2% numerical-prose backfire is KH-confirmed (2602.21619), and the card's own table concedes no row dissociates metric-edge from topological-edge content (L373-375, L397). The honest "needs a learned graph, not yet dissociated from topology" framing is right. This finding is *more* reliable than Finding 4 and arguably should outrank it; the briefing's own ranking is slightly inverted.

**Mean = 6.6. Min = 2.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 4 (the 2605.24642 "null result").** It is the only finding that is *factually contradicted by the vault's own source*, and it is load-bearing three times over: it is the lead clause of the CEO summary ("not 'add geometry' ... which a rigorous study shows yields no significant gain"), the entire content of the "hidden cross-perspective connection," and the named protocol the concrete next experiment tells the researcher to replicate ("McNemar significance, frozen-perception control"). If 2605.24642 actually found geometry *helps* (which its KH note says), then the briefing's framing — "geometry-injection null results suggest the bet could come back showing perception is the driver" — loses its strongest external anchor, and the Skeptic's whole position weakens.

**Specific info needed to verify:** pull the actual 2605.24642 PDF (the paper has a frozen-VLM Early/Late/Spatial-Forcing comparison) and locate (a) the exact sim numbers the analyst rendered as "69.7% vs 71.7%", (b) whether a p=0.399 / McNemar test appears anywhere, and (c) whether *any* configuration is reported as a non-significant null. The KH note suggests the opposite (75.2% vs 71.7% "statistically better," +21.5% single-camera). Until that PDF check is done, treat the null-result claim as **unverified and probably inverted**, and demote it from the lead clause to, at most, "naive prompt-level metric injection can backfire (VSR-Injection 2602.21619, −9.2%)" — which *is* vault-supported.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Skeptic over-dominated**, and the analyst part says so explicitly (005-B-1-analyst.md L39: "Strongest = Skeptic"). The synthesis is structurally built around the Skeptic's null-result paper: Findings 1, 3, 4, the hidden connection, and the go/no-go pivot all route through "geometry injection mostly fails, so run the isolation." That would be defensible *if the null result were real* — but it is not vault-supported, so the dominant perspective is leaning on the briefing's one bad fact. The Economist (saturation) and Historian (scale-absorbs-structure) are folded *into* the Skeptic's frame by the "hidden connection," which reduces three independent perspectives to one argument and creates false consensus ("support: Economist, Academic, Historian" on Finding 2 is genuine; the merge on the hidden connection is the over-reach). The **Practitioner is under-weighted**: its two challenges (unstated deploy-latency bar on Finding 2; ConsisVLA single-source cost claim on Finding 3) are the most actionable caveats in the document and are correctly noted but never allowed to move the conclusion. Net: not balanced — Skeptic-dominant, Practitioner-suppressed.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective (6th angle that would change conclusions)

**The Builder / Reproducibility-engineer** — "can this isolation actually be run, and on whose code?" The entire actionable insight is "ablate ConsisVLA-4D's consistency attention with perception held fixed." That presupposes (a) ConsisVLA-4D's code is released and the consistency-attention module is *cleanly separable* from perceptual features, and (b) "perception held fixed" is even well-defined when consistency attention and perceptual encoding share parameters. None of the B2 table rows confirm ConsisVLA-4D is open-source; the card flags its 2.31x cost as single-source, which hints the artifact may not be independently runnable. A Builder would ask: if the spearhead experiment can't be run on ConsisVLA-4D, what is the fallback substrate? The card lists candidates (VLA-4D's coordinate-chaos harness, WAM4D's spatial-register-token drop, GenStudy 2512.02902's FTM/FLA at 4K params — which *already* isolates the perception arm at 99x fewer params, L492) that may be *more* ablation-friendly than ConsisVLA-4D. This perspective would likely **re-target the lead experiment off ConsisVLA-4D**, dissolving the single-source risk the briefing instead proposes to "de-risk by re-deriving the 2.31x claim" (which doesn't make the artifact ablatable, only the cost claim trustworthy). Adding it changes the concrete next step.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B−.**

A genuinely sharp briefing — the joint (spatial/temporal halves of one grounding thesis) is real, the whitespace (nobody runs the consistency-vs-perception isolation) is correctly identified and vault-verified, the falsifier is front-loaded, and the go/no-go pivot to the construction-bottleneck thread (B1-H5/Trust-Spatial) is exactly the kind of survives-if-the-bet-dies hedge a fundable proposal needs. The B2 and B1 method/benchmark numbers I spot-checked (ConsisVLA-4D, GAM, VSR-Injection, RoboTwin, GeoAware) are all genuine and correctly characterized. What pulls it from A− to B− is a single but serious defect: **the keystone external evidence is misreported against the vault's own source, and that error is replicated unexamined from the upstream analyst part into the CEO summary, the hidden connection, and the named experimental protocol.** A Stanford committee would catch the inverted citation in five minutes and it would cost the whole proposal credibility.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Verify or retract the 2605.24642 null result.** Pull the PDF; confirm whether 69.7%/p=0.399/McNemar exists. Per its KH note it does *not*, and the paper reports geometry *helping* (75.2% vs 71.7% "statistically better," +21.5% single-camera). If the PDF agrees with the note, rewrite the CEO summary, Finding 4, and the hidden connection: the honest claim is "*naive prompt-level* metric injection into a *frozen* model can backfire (VSR-Injection 2602.21619, −9.2%) — *full-architecture* GFM fusion and consistency redesign do show OOD wins," which makes the isolation experiment *more* interesting, not less, and stops the briefing from resting on a phantom null.
2. **Stop importing Cluster A's falsifier into Cluster B.** 2605.24642 is cited only in Cluster A (L105, L301). Either justify why a GFM-fusion result governs B2's consistency mechanism, or replace it with a B-resident anchor.
3. **State the deploy-latency budget** the OOD wins must clear (Practitioner's suppressed point) — Finding 2's "durable play" is publication-durable, not yet deployment-durable, without it.
4. **Re-target the lead experiment off the single-source artifact**, or name the fallback substrate (VLA-4D / WAM4D / 2512.02902) if ConsisVLA-4D's consistency attention turns out not to be cleanly ablatable — "re-derive the 2.31x cost" does not solve the ablatability risk.
5. **Consider swapping the rank of Findings 4 and 5** — Finding 5 (B1 metric-vs-topological, vault-supported) is more reliable than Finding 4 (contaminated) and should not sit below it.

---

### B1 (Direction review)

Topic: B1 bets that in scene-graph chain-of-thought spatial reasoning for VLMs, the *metric* edge content (distances, angles), not graph topology, carries the gain, and that graph-*construction* accuracy, not reasoning, is the failure bottleneck — verbatim: "the gain is carried by *metric* content, not graph topology, and bottlenecked by *construction*, not reasoning".

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys robot policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The bet is intellectually clean but lives one full layer above where deployed robots break.
