A metric scene-graph CoT is a reasoning-side intervention; the thing that kills real manipulation is whether the *upstream geometry estimate* (depth, object pose) is correct, and B1's own H4 quietly concedes "a residual only sensed geometry closes."
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2605.29074|Embodied3DBench]] shows 13 VLMs are *none robust on low-level metric 3D* and that this score *correlates with downstream LIBERO SR* — i.e. the metric-3D perception substrate, not the reasoning chain, gates the policy. [[2501.10074|SpatialCoT]]'s 82.57% manip / 61.83% nav is the only number on the card tied to an actual robot, and it is "never benchmarked vs a geometric action head" — so the practitioner has no evidence the scene-graph route beats just feeding a point-cloud head.
- *Only this perspective would say*: build the construction-quality predictor first and measure it *on your own robot's perception stack* — graph-construction accuracy from a clean synthetic 3DSG (RieMind-style) is a fantasy on a real RGB-D stream with occlusion and motion blur; the H5 "construction predicts answer" relationship you are betting on is exactly the part that degrades when you leave the benchmark.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The card's framing is unusually honest and the literature genuinely supports the *premises* (structure beats prose; RL-OOD beats SFT). But the two halves of the bet have very different evidentiary status: H1 (metric vs topological) is a real, unaddressed isolation, whereas H3/H5 (perception/construction is the bottleneck) is *already the field's leading hypothesis*, not a contrarian wedge.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2504.20024|SpatialReasoner]] explicitly "states perception (3D-property estimation) is the bottleneck, not computation" and shows the explicit-3D ablation 51.9%→58.3%; [[2603.15386|RieMind]] hits 89.5% VSI-Bench-static with a *ground-truth* 3DSG precisely to "isolate reasoning from perceptual error" — both already argue B1's H3/H5 direction. Conversely [[2507.13362|VLM Spatial Reasoning RL]] (77.69% CVBench, +19.5% Depth-OOD) and [[2511.07403|SpatialThinker]] (78.2% CV-Bench, but rewards *CIoU box-alignment* not metric edges) leave the metric-vs-topological question genuinely open — the H1 cell is real.
- *Only this perspective would say*: rewrite the contribution so the headline is H1 (the causal isolation nobody has run), not H5 (a re-confirmation). The peer reviewer who knows VSP 2407.01863 and SpatialReasoner will reject an H5-led paper as "known," but cannot dismiss a clean metric-vs-topological dissociation with FlipEval controls.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The dichotomy "metric content vs graph topology" may be a false binary — what the literature calls a "metric" win is often a *perceptual-grounding* win (better box localization), and once you control for grounding the metric *labels* may add little. And the construction-bottleneck claim is close to tautological: of course a wrong graph poisons the answer.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2602.21619|VSR Information Injection Analysis]] is the card's own self-foil — injecting *numerical/metric* text into a frozen model *backfires up to −9.2%*, and verbalized beats numerical. So "metric content helps" is not a safe prior; B1 must show the win comes from a *learned structured* metric graph, a much narrower claim. [[2511.07403|SpatialThinker]]'s gain rides on CIoU box-alignment (a grounding signal), not edge labels, hinting the real lever is perception, not metric arithmetic. [[2407.01863|VSP]] (newly surfaced) already swaps vision→text and shows perception is the dominant bottleneck — so the "bottleneck is construction" half is arguably pre-confirmed by 2024 work.
- *Only this perspective would say*: your H1 falsifier ("topological edges match metric edges → the win is structure, not metric") is the *likely* outcome on CVBench, where most questions are categorical. Pre-register a benchmark whose questions actually require metric arithmetic (3DSRBench metric slice / [[2601.11442|Map2Thought]]-style continuous queries), or you will falsify your own headline.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: Scene-graph-CoT spatial reasoning is a low-capex, benchmark-legible direction — 7B models, synthetic data, public CVBench/3DSRBench/VSI-Bench leaderboards — which is exactly why it is *crowded*: it is the cheapest way for an academic lab to post a SOTA number without a robot.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: the card lists ~40 papers on one axis, many from late 2025–2026 ([[2601.01984|Thinking with Blueprints]] 7B, [[2512.16909|MomaGraph]] 7B, [[2511.07403|SpatialThinker]] on only 7K synthetic samples, [[2605.18162|SAGE]] at <37% of data) — a stampede toward "structure+RL on a small synthetic set." The incentive is to chase the benchmark delta, which rewards H2 (already-settled RL-OOD) and punishes the un-glamorous H5 instrumentation work that produces no leaderboard line.
- *Only this perspective would say*: the defensible asset here is *not* another +X% on CVBench (saturating, GPT-4o-relative deltas erode as bases improve) — it is the H5 *construction-quality predictor* ([[2601.11644|Trust-Spatial]]-style), because a reusable verifier that says "trust this spatial answer" is what a robotics product actually needs and what survives benchmark saturation. Fund the verifier, not the leaderboard climb.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the symbolic-vs-connectionist pendulum replayed in the VLM era: B1 reintroduces an *explicit structured intermediate* (scene-graph) against the scaling-implicit orthodoxy — the same move neuro-symbolic VQA (NS-VQA, NS-CL) made against end-to-end VQA circa 2018–2019, and the same move classical SLAM's metric maps made against pixel methods.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: the card's "challenged assumption" — that a big-enough model reasons about space implicitly — echoes the pixel-vs-latent and modular-vs-end-to-end cycles; [[2603.15386|RieMind]] (LLM querying a GT 3D scene-graph via tools) is structurally a 2018 neuro-symbolic executor wearing a 2026 agentic-LLM coat. Historically the explicit-structure side *wins on interpretability and OOD* (B1's H2, +19.5% Depth-OOD) but *loses on construction robustness* — exactly the failure mode H5 names.
- *Only this perspective would say*: every prior structured-intermediate wave was eventually absorbed when the end-to-end model got big enough to learn the structure internally (see [[2604.26934|World2VLM]] distilling WM imagination *into* the VLM, [[2602.21186|Spa3R]]'s opaque latent field beating token-append). Time-box this: B1's edge is a 12–18 month window before a frontier model internalizes the metric graph and the explicit-graph advantage collapses to interpretability-only.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Skeptic vs Academic on H1's premise*: Skeptic cites [[2602.21619|VSR Information Injection Analysis]] (numerical/metric injection backfires −9.2%) to argue "metric content helps" is *not* a safe prior; Academic treats H1 as the strongest, cleanest contribution. They clash on whether the metric-edge win even exists once grounding is controlled.
   - *Practitioner vs Academic on H5's measurability*: Academic prizes H5 as construction-bound-is-known-good-science (RieMind, SpatialReasoner); Practitioner says the H5 "construction predicts answer" relationship is measured on *clean synthetic 3DSGs* and will not survive a real RGB-D perception stack — the predictor is benchmark-bound.
   - *Economist vs Historian on what to build*: Economist says build the durable verifier (Trust-Spatial), Historian says any explicit structure is on a 12–18 month clock before absorption — so even the verifier is a depreciating asset.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.** Strongest: **ACADEMIC** — it is the only one that splits the bet into its two limbs with differential evidence (H1 live, H3/H5 pre-empted) and maps each claim to a specific cited result, which is exactly the decision the researcher faces. Weakest: **HISTORIAN** — the symbolic-pendulum analogy is illuminating but non-falsifiable on this timescale and offers no concrete next experiment; its "12–18 month window" is an unverifiable guess.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.** On a benchmark whose questions *require metric arithmetic* (not categorical), does a scene-graph with numeric metric edge labels beat the *same* graph with only categorical labels, *after* equalizing perceptual grounding (same box localizer / CIoU)? This single controlled ablation resolves the Skeptic-vs-Academic clash: if metric wins with grounding held fixed, H1 is real and B1 has a paper; if not, the win was grounding all along and B1 collapses into the crowded perception-grounding lane.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).** Explicit *structure* beats free-form language CoT for VLM spatial reasoning, and RL grounding (GRPO) generalizes OOD better than SFT — i.e. B1's premises and H2 are settled. Every perspective treats this as established ([[2507.13362|VLM Spatial Reasoning RL]] +19.5% Depth-OOD, [[2601.01984|Thinking with Blueprints]] 79.7% SAT-test, [[2512.16909|MomaGraph]]). The corollary they all accept: H2 is *not* a contribution, it is a re-confirmation.

5. **The blind spot (none addressed).** None addressed *compute/latency-per-query and inference cost of the scene-graph-construction step at robot control rates*. B1 frames a two-step (construct-graph → reason) pipeline as if cost is free, but [[2505.12448|SSR]] (CoT 23.16s→0.32s) and [[2602.08236|AVIC]] (9× fewer tokens by gating *when* to imagine) show the field already treats reasoning-step latency as a first-class constraint. A metric scene-graph that must be re-constructed every frame for a 10–30 Hz policy may be unaffordable regardless of whether H1 is true — and no perspective costed it.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** B1 is a two-limb bet, and the limbs have opposite risk profiles. The premises (structure > prose; RL-OOD > SFT) are settled across ~40 cited papers, so they earn no novelty credit. Of the two genuinely-claimed limbs, **H1 (metric edge content, not topology, carries the gain) is real, unaddressed whitespace** — the card's own survey confirms no paper isolates metric-vs-topological edges holding structure fixed, and the closest siblings ([[2507.13362|VLM Spatial Reasoning RL]], [[2511.07403|SpatialThinker]]) reward box-grounding, not metric labels. But **H3/H5 (the bottleneck is perception/construction, not reasoning) is the field's leading hypothesis, not a contrarian wedge**: [[2504.20024|SpatialReasoner]] states it, [[2603.15386|RieMind]] is built to demonstrate it with GT graphs, and a newly-surfaced 2024 paper, VSP ([[arxiv:2407.01863]]), already proves perception is the dominant bottleneck by swapping vision for text. The serious internal threat is the card's own foil [[2602.21619|VSR Information Injection Analysis]]: naive metric injection *backfires*, so H1's win must come specifically from a *learned structured* metric graph and must be tested on metric-arithmetic questions, or B1 will falsify its own headline on categorical-heavy CVBench. Verdict: commit to a de-risked, H1-led version with a grounding-controlled ablation; demote H5 to instrumentation, not headline.

**5 key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Structure > prose and RL-OOD > SFT are settled (premises + H2 are re-confirmation, not contribution).* Supported by Practitioner, Academic, Economist, Historian; challenged by none. This is the most reliable finding and the most damaging to a naive framing — it means at least two of B1's six hypotheses earn zero novelty credit.
2. *H1 (metric-vs-topological isolation) is genuine, unaddressed whitespace.* Supported by Academic (clean cell, FlipEval controls available), reinforced by the card's own audit ("none isolate metric vs topological"); challenged by Skeptic (the binary may be false / win may be grounding). Reliable as *novel*, contested as *winnable*.
3. *H3/H5 (perception/construction is the bottleneck) is partially pre-empted.* Supported by Academic and Practitioner (it's the field consensus, [[2504.20024|SpatialReasoner]]/[[2603.15386|RieMind]]/VSP 2407.01863); challenged as a contribution by Economist (no leaderboard line) — reliable that it's *true*, unreliable that it's *novel*.
4. *The metric win may dissolve once perceptual grounding is controlled.* Supported by Skeptic ([[2602.21619|VSR Information Injection Analysis]] −9.2%, [[2511.07403|SpatialThinker]]'s CIoU lever); challenged by Academic (still worth running). Medium reliability — it is precisely the experiment that hasn't been run.
5. *The H5 construction-quality predictor is the only benchmark-saturation-proof asset, but is measured on clean synthetic graphs.* Supported by Economist (durable verifier) and Practitioner (but won't survive real RGB-D); challenged by Historian (depreciating on a 12–18 month absorption clock). Lower reliability — depends on transfer to a real perception stack nobody here has tested.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Skeptic's −9.2% "numerical injection backfires" finding and the Practitioner's "it breaks on the real perception stack" warning are the *same underlying failure* viewed from two ends: both say the metric *numbers* are only useful when they are *correctly grounded*, so an ungrounded or wrongly-constructed metric edge is worse than a categorical one. This unifies H1 and H5: H1's metric-edge advantage is *conditional on* H5's construction accuracy — the two hypotheses are not independent, they are a single conditional claim ("metric edges win *iff* construction is accurate"). The card treats them as separable; they are not.

**Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, then COMMIT to an H1-led, grounding-controlled paper.** Do not commit to the bet as written (H5-flavored, six hypotheses). Instead: (1) *Reframe the headline as H1*, the one uncontested cell. (2) *Run the resolving experiment first* as a kill-test: on the 3DSRBench metric slice and a metric-arithmetic benchmark ([[2601.11442|Map2Thought]]-style continuous queries) — NOT categorical-heavy CVBench — ablate metric numeric edge labels vs the same graph with categorical-only labels, *holding the box localizer / CIoU grounding fixed* so the win cannot be attributed to perception. (3) Pre-register the Skeptic's falsifier: if categorical matches metric under fixed grounding, stop — the contribution is dead and you have saved a year. (4) Fold H5 in as the *conditioning variable* (instrument construction accuracy and report the metric-edge advantage *stratified by* construction correctness), not as a separate headline, since H3/H5's direction is pre-confirmed by VSP/SpatialReasoner. Concrete next experiment: the 2×2 of {metric-edges, categorical-edges} × {high-construction-accuracy, low-construction-accuracy} on the 3DSRBench metric slice, grounding held fixed — the cell where metric+high-construction wins and the others don't *is* the paper.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Once a frontier VLM internalizes a metric scene-graph implicitly (the Historian's absorption event, foreshadowed by [[2604.26934|World2VLM]] and [[2602.21186|Spa3R]]), does an *explicit* metric graph still add anything beyond interpretability — or does the entire explicit-structure direction collapse to a verifier/inspection tool? If explicit metric structure provides zero accuracy lift over an internalized one, B1's window is interpretability-only and the durable contribution is the H5 verifier, not the graph.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. I read B1 (Spatial-4D.md L350-441) and spot-verified the load-bearing external claims. The briefing is unusually disciplined about novelty accounting, but it leans one critical recommendation (demote H5) on a misread external paper, and it under-prices the experimental cost of the one cell it calls "uncontested."*

---

##### 1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1-10 reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Structure>prose and RL-OOD>SFT are settled; premises + H2 earn zero novelty | **9/10** | Directly grounded in the card's own rows (VLM-Spatial-RL +19.5% Depth-OOD, Thinking-with-Blueprints 79.7% SAT-test, MomaGraph). H2's own text concedes "settled prior, re-confirm." Unassailable, and the most damaging fact to a naive framing. Docked 1 only because "settled" still leaves the *causal-OOD* slice genuinely un-run. |
| 2 | H1 (metric-vs-topological isolation) is genuine, unaddressed whitespace | **8/10** | Verified: SpatialThinker (2511.07403) rewards CIoU box-grounding and VLM-Spatial-RL (2507.13362) doesn't separate edge types — the card's "what's missing" columns are accurate, not spin. The whitespace is real. Docked 2 because the Skeptic's challenge (the metric/topological binary may be false, or the win may be grounding) is unresolved and material. |
| 3 | H3/H5 (perception/construction is the bottleneck) is partially pre-empted | **5/10** | The *direction* is field consensus (SpatialReasoner 2504.20024 states it; RieMind 2603.15386 is built on it) — that part holds. But the briefing's strongest pre-emption witness, **VSP 2407.01863, is misread** (see §2). It is a *dual-challenge* paper, not a "perception is dominant" proof. The pre-emption is real but weaker than claimed; the score reflects a true core wrapped around a wrong anchor. |
| 4 | The metric win may dissolve once grounding is controlled | **7/10** | The 2602.21619 foil is real and accurately quoted (-9.2% numerical, +9.6% single verbalized context, verbalized>numerical — I confirmed against the KH note). This is the correct experiment and the correct risk. Docked 3 because it is a *prediction of a possible null*, not an established result; the experiment has not been run, so it is honest hedging, not a finding. |
| 5 | H5 construction-quality predictor is the only saturation-proof asset but measured on clean synthetic graphs | **6/10** | Trust-Spatial (2601.11644) genuinely supplies the deployable predictor (52.1%→78.3% edge precision) the other H5 rows lack — accurate. But the "won't survive real RGB-D" caveat and the "12-18 month absorption clock" are asserted, not evidenced; no row here tests transfer to a real perception stack. Durability claim rests on speculation about the field, not data. |

**Mean = 7.0, Min = 5.0.**

---

##### 2. WEAKEST LINK

**Finding 3 is the weakest claim, and it is weak for a specific, verifiable reason that the briefing got wrong.** The synthesis says VSP (2407.01863) "already proves perception is the dominant bottleneck by swapping vision for text," and uses that to justify the headline recommendation to *demote H5 to instrumentation*.

I checked. VSP's title is literally **"Assessing the dual challenges of perception AND reasoning in spatial planning."** Its signature result is the opposite of the briefing's framing: even when models are handed an oracle/text state (perception removed), they **still fail at the planning/reasoning step** — perception and reasoning are *both* bottlenecks, with reasoning surviving the perception fix. The paper is a "both fail" result, not a "perception dominates" result.

This matters because it inverts the load on the recommendation:
- If VSP showed perception dominates → demote H5 is safe (the bottleneck is pre-confirmed, just instrument it).
- VSP actually shows reasoning *persists* after perception is fixed → H5's "construction is THE bottleneck" claim is *contested by the very paper cited to pre-empt it*, and H3 (the reasoning-vs-perception split) becomes *more* interesting, not less.

Second-order problem: **VSP (2407.01863) is not in the KnowledgeHub and appears nowhere in the vault except the sibling analyst part that fed this briefing.** Every other paper B1 leans on is in KH; this one external anchor was introduced mid-pipeline and never grounded. A claim that reverses a headline recommendation should not rest on the one un-vaulted, misquoted source.

**Info needed to verify / fix:** read VSP's Table on text-only (oracle-perception) planning accuracy. If text-only planning is still far below human/ceiling, H5 should NOT be demoted on VSP's authority — H3 and H5 both stay live. The cheap check is the VSP paper's "perception sub-task" vs "reasoning sub-task" decomposition.

---

##### 3. BIAS CHECK — dominant perspective

**The Skeptic over-dominated.** The entire actionable insight is built around the Skeptic's -9.2% falsifier: reframe to H1, run the kill-test first, pre-register the Skeptic's null, demote everything else. That is a single-perspective program wearing a five-perspective costume. Three tells:

1. The "hidden cross-perspective connection" claims to *unify* Skeptic + Practitioner, but it does so by collapsing both into the Skeptic's thesis (metric edges only help if grounded). That is not synthesis across perspectives; it is the Skeptic absorbing the Practitioner.
2. The Economist appears only to *deny* novelty credit (no leaderboard line) — used as a second skeptical voice, not as an independent value lens. A real Economist read would ask "what is the *expected* SOTA delta and citation return of the H1 paper if it wins?" — that question is absent.
3. The Academic (whose job is to defend the contribution) is repeatedly overruled ("challenged by Skeptic," "still worth running" is the weakest possible defense). The synthesis lets the prosecution write the verdict.

Net effect: the briefing is *risk-management excellent* and *upside-blind*. It tells you precisely how B1 dies; it never quantifies what B1 is worth if it lives.

---

##### 4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE — the 6th angle

**The Experimentalist / cost-and-feasibility engineer.** Every recommendation here assumes the 2×2 kill-test is cheap to run. It is not, and nobody costed it:

- The kill-test requires a **metric-arithmetic benchmark** ("Map2Thought-style continuous queries"). The card cites Map2Thought (2601.11442) and Video2Layout (2511.16160) as *maps*, but there is **no off-the-shelf metric-arithmetic QA set with held-fixed grounding** named in the card. Building one is a project in itself, and a hand-built eval is exactly where a contribution quietly dies.
- The 2×2 also needs a **{high-construction, low-construction} graph axis** with the box localizer / CIoU grounding held fixed. Controlling grounding while varying only edge-label semantics is non-trivial: you need a graph constructor whose topology is identical and whose only difference is numeric-vs-categorical edge labels. The card asserts this is ablatable on 2507.13362 but gives no evidence the released artifacts expose that knob.

An Experimentalist would change the conclusion from "run the kill-test first" to "**spend phase 0 proving the metric-arithmetic eval and the grounding-fixed constructor even exist**, because if either is infeasible the entire de-risked plan is unbuildable and you should fall back to the H5 verifier (Trust-Spatial) as the primary contribution from day one." That reorders the whole actionable.

---

##### 5. OVERALL GRADE

**B+.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest briefing. It refuses novelty credit it could have grabbed (premises + H2), it correctly identifies the single sharpest internal foil (2602.21619), and its de-risk-then-commit instinct is the right one for a high-variance bet. The numbers it quotes that I could check were accurate. Most strategy memos at this level are cheerleading; this one is a prosecution, which is more useful.

It is not an A because the one recommendation with the largest consequence — *demote H5* — rests on a **misread, un-vaulted external paper (VSP 2407.01863)**, and the synthesis is **single-perspective (Skeptic) wearing five hats**, leaving it blind to (a) the upside if B1 wins and (b) the feasibility cost of the kill-test it prescribes.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Re-read VSP 2407.01863 and correct Finding 3 / the demote-H5 call.** VSP is "dual challenge," not "perception dominates." H5 should stay a live hypothesis, not be demoted to instrumentation, until a paper actually shows reasoning-given-a-correct-graph is solved. Ground VSP in KH or drop it as an anchor.
2. **Cost the kill-test before committing to it.** Confirm a metric-arithmetic benchmark and a grounding-fixed, topology-matched graph constructor are obtainable. If not, the de-risk plan is unbuildable; pivot the primary contribution to the Trust-Spatial H5 verifier (the one durable, saturation-proof asset the briefing itself names).
3. **Add the upside half.** State the expected SOTA delta and the contribution's value *if* H1 wins under fixed grounding. A bet memo that only prices downside cannot tell you whether the de-risked paper is worth a year even if it succeeds.
4. **Stop letting the Skeptic write the Academic's lines.** The "unification" of H1 and H5 (metric edges win iff construction is accurate) is a genuine insight, but as stated it is a *reason H1 is conditional*, not a reason to merge the hypotheses — keep them separable so H1 can be falsified without H5 confounding it.

---

### B2 (Direction review)

Topic: the bet that it is the spatio-temporal *consistency constraint* (not raw 3D perception or viewpoint-geometry alone) that drives OOD robustness in VLA manipulation, shown over *full* space+time 4D via a consistency-vs-perception isolation ablation (perception held fixed) — that no paper has yet run.

The bet, verbatim from the card's First-principles framing: *"it is the consistency constraint, not raw 3D or viewpoint-geometry alone, that drives the OOD gain — and it must be shown over full space+time 4D, not the viewpoint-only slice GeoAware fenced. Specifically, ablating ConsisVLA-4D's consistency attention with perception held fixed collapses OOD SR (toward MolmoAct's 72.1% baseline) more than ablating any single perceptual feature, and more than it dents ID SR — the consistency-vs-perception isolation no paper has run."*

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
Core position: the implicit-vs-explicit cost contrast is real and the most deployable part of the bet, but the headline efficiency numbers rest on single-source, sim-favorable measurements that won't survive a real bimanual robot. ConsisVLA-4D's 2.31× speedup / 0.110 s latency and 70.0% real bimanual (vs OpenVLA 28.5%) are from one lab on RoboTwin-style setups; GAM at 6.9 ms/pass (~145 Hz, 1.4B params) is the latency number a deployer would actually chase, and it already gets 83.1% under camera perturbation without any new ablation.
Strongest supporting evidence: GAM 2606.17046 (12.1 pp LIBERO-Plus drop vs 16.1–60.9% baselines, 55× faster than diffusion); WAM4D 2606.14048 (525.43 ms / 9.71 GiB, geometry dropped at deploy) — these prove the cheap-implicit regime is buildable today.
The one thing only this perspective says: the bet's go/no-go isn't H1's clean ablation, it's whether the consistency mechanism survives real-robot data cost and latency budgets — and a 525 ms WAM4D vs 6.9 ms GAM already tells you the "implicit is cheap" story has a 75× internal spread the card papers over.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
Core position: the precise experiment B2 proposes — hold perception fixed, ablate only the consistency mechanism, measure OOD-SR over full 4D — genuinely has not been published; the cited papers each ablate an adjacent variable (GeoAware 2509.14117 = VGGT-layer selection, viewpoint-only; VLA-4D 2511.17199 = coordinate-chaos = 3D-presence; WAM4D = register-token placement). So the *causal attribution* claim is open whitespace.
Strongest supporting evidence: 2512.02902 (VLA Generalizability Study) pins viewpoint/lighting brittleness to *visual-representation misalignment* (FTM 48.5%→87.1% at 4K params; FLA 94.8% at 99× fewer params) — i.e. it already attributes a large share of OOD to the *perception* arm, which is the rival hypothesis B2 must beat, not assume away.
The one thing only this perspective says: the card's own evidence cuts against its bet — if a 4K-param affine fix to *perception* recovers viewpoint OOD (2512.02902), the burden is on B2 to show consistency-ablation hurts OOD *more* than perception-ablation, and the prior from the literature is that perception is at least co-equal.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
Core position: "consistency helps OOD" is conceded by the card itself to be consensus (GeoAware viewpoint, EvoScene-VLA/OptimusVLA time), so the only novelty is the *isolation ablation* — a negative-result-shaped contribution that risks confirming the boring answer (both matter, entangled) or being unfalsifiable because "consistency attention" and "perception" cannot be cleanly separated in ConsisVLA-4D's CV-Aligner/CO-Fuser/CS-Thinker stack.
Strongest supporting evidence: ConsisVLA-4D's CS-Thinker *learns implicit local object dynamics and global depth knowledge* in one attention block (KH 2605.05126) — depth-knowledge IS perception, so "hold perception fixed while removing consistency" may not be a well-defined cut; and 2606.01095 already found WAM future-features are "associative," not cleanly attributable.
The one thing only this perspective says: the experiment may be ill-posed — if the consistency mechanism and the perceptual representation are co-trained in a single attention block, an ablation that removes one necessarily perturbs the other, so the "isolation" is a confound, and a clean falsifier ("perception ablation hurts as much") is the *likely* outcome.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows incentives).**
Core position: this is a benchmark-saturation play — LIBERO is pinned at 97–98% across a dozen methods (ConsisVLA-4D 98.1%, GTA-VLA 98.6%, OptimusVLA 98.6%, LaMP 98.3%, ROCKET 98.5%, GAM 97.6%), so the field has *migrated the prize* to OOD suites (LIBERO-Plus, RoboTwin-2.0-Plus, SimplerEnv-OR), and "which mechanism drives OOD" is exactly the narrative that mints the next round of papers without new hardware.
Strongest supporting evidence: the proliferation of competing OOD substrates in the card — LIBERO-Plus 2510.13626 (7 axes), WAM-vs-VLA 2603.22078 (21 sub-dims), LIBERO-X 2602.06556 (5 graded levels), SimplerEnv-OR (CronusVLA 2506.19816) — is a tell that benchmark-creation is where the citations are.
The one thing only this perspective says: B2's defensibility is benchmark-political, not scientific — whoever owns the OOD substrate on which the isolation is run controls the result; the cheapest moat is to *co-release the isolation protocol as a benchmark* (a consistency-vs-perception diagnostic suite) rather than a single ablation that any lab can contest on a different OOD suite.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
Core position: this is the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle replaying inside the policy — explicit future-frame generation (Dreamer-lineage, here X-WAM/STARRY/MV-VDP) vs implicit/latent consistency (here ConsisVLA-4D/VLA-JEPA/LaST0) — and history says the latent camp wins on cost but the explicit camp wins on debuggability and the longest horizons, which is exactly what H2/H4 predict.
Strongest supporting evidence: the card's own H4 crossover hypothesis and the C4 "route to explicit persistent memory beyond the upper crossover" mirror the model-based-RL arc where latent imagination (Dreamer) plateaued and explicit/hybrid methods reclaimed long-horizon tasks; VLA-JEPA 2602.10098 (97.2% LIBERO, pure-opaque latent) vs EA-WM 2605.06192 (explicit geometry-preserving generation) is the live re-enactment.
The one thing only this perspective says: every prior "X, not Y, is the real driver" attribution war (e.g. is it depth or is it the encoder; is it DR or is it scale) resolved to "it's the interaction, measured on a harder benchmark" — so B2 should pre-commit to reporting the *interaction term*, because the pure-isolation framing is the framing that historically gets overturned.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Direct clashes.**
- ACADEMIC vs the CARD's bet: 2512.02902 attributes viewpoint/lighting OOD to *perception misalignment* (FLA 94.8%, 99× fewer params) — directly the rival to "consistency, not perception, drives OOD." The card cites it as the perception-arm-to-hold-fixed; the academic reading is it's evidence the *falsifier may fire*.
- SKEPTIC vs PRACTITIONER/ACADEMIC on well-posedness: skeptic says ConsisVLA-4D's CS-Thinker fuses depth-knowledge (perception) with dynamics (consistency) in one block, so "perception held fixed" is not a clean cut; academic/practitioner assume the ablation is operationalizable.
- PRACTITIONER vs CARD on the cost claim: card leans on ConsisVLA-4D's 2.31× single-source number; practitioner counters with the 525 ms (WAM4D) ↔ 6.9 ms (GAM) spread showing "implicit is cheap" is not one regime.
- HISTORIAN vs SKEPTIC on outcome value: historian says the interaction-term result is the durable contribution; skeptic says that exact "both matter, entangled" outcome is the *boring/negative* result that may not clear a bar.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest.**
Strongest: ACADEMIC — its claim is grounded in two real controlled studies already in the vault (2512.02902 perception-isolation; 2606.01095 representational diagnostics) that bracket B2's question from both sides, making its prediction about the falsifier the most evidence-bound. Weakest: ECONOMIST — directionally true about benchmark-saturation incentives but its claims are sociological, not testable against a metric, so it cannot move the COMMIT/DROP decision on its own.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
Is the consistency mechanism in ConsisVLA-4D (CS-Thinker's spatio-temporal consistency attention) *architecturally separable* from its perceptual depth-knowledge such that one can be ablated with the other provably frozen? If no, H1 is ill-posed (skeptic wins) and the bet must be reformulated as an interaction/attribution study (historian's framing); if yes, H1 is the clean novel experiment (academic agrees it's unpublished).

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
That implicit/latent consistency *helps* OOD and is *cheaper than explicit future-frame generation* — every perspective concedes this (the card calls it consensus; GAM, ConsisVLA-4D, GeoAware, EvoScene-VLA, OptimusVLA, LaMP all show OOD gains from implicit temporal-geometry at low cost). The contested part is only the *causal attribution* (consistency vs perception) and *whether the isolation is well-posed*.

**(5) The blind spot (addressed by none).**
Real-world *temporal* OOD with closed-loop drift: every cited OOD substrate perturbs spatial/visual factors (viewpoint, lighting, background, layout); CronusVLA's SimplerEnv-OR is the lone temporal-OOD suite and it is sim. No perspective addressed whether 4D-consistency's claimed advantage holds under *real-robot temporal distribution shift* (latency-induced action lag, dynamic distractors entering mid-episode, contact-event timing) — the regime where a "4D consistency" claim should matter most and where no benchmark exists.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** B2 bets that a spatio-temporal *consistency constraint* — not raw 3D perception — is the causal driver of OOD robustness in VLA policies, and that proving it requires a consistency-vs-perception isolation ablation over full space+time 4D that no paper has run. The *experiment* is genuinely unpublished and the *premise* (implicit consistency helps OOD, cheaply) is now consensus, so the novelty is narrow and attribution-shaped: the risk is not that someone scooped the ablation, but that the ablation is ill-posed (consistency and perception are co-trained inside ConsisVLA-4D's single attention block) and that two existing vault papers — 2512.02902 (perception-misalignment drives OOD) and the new 2606.01095 (associative, SAE-based WAM-vs-VLA diagnostics) — bracket the question tightly enough that the likely result is "both matter, entangled," a defensible-but-modest contribution rather than a clean "consistency wins."

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Implicit consistency helps OOD at lower cost than explicit generation* — HIGH. Supported by PRACTITIONER, HISTORIAN, ACADEMIC; challenged by none (only the *magnitude* via single-source ConsisVLA-4D number is challenged by PRACTITIONER). Evidence: GAM 83.1% camera-OOD @6.9 ms, ConsisVLA-4D 98.1%/2.31×, LaMP +9.7 pp LIBERO-Plus.
2. *The exact isolation ablation (perception-frozen, consistency-removed, full-4D OOD-SR) is unpublished* — HIGH. Supported by ACADEMIC; cited papers ablate adjacent variables only (GeoAware=VGGT-layer, VLA-4D=coordinate-chaos, WAM4D=register placement, GEM-4D=geometry-flow-branch). Challenged mildly by SKEPTIC (it may be unpublished because ill-posed).
3. *Perception, not consistency, is at least co-equal as the OOD driver* — MEDIUM-HIGH. Supported by ACADEMIC, SKEPTIC; challenged by the card's bet. Evidence: 2512.02902 FTM 48.5%→87.1% (4K params), FLA 94.8% (99× fewer params) on viewpoint/visual OOD — a perception-only fix recovers most viewpoint OOD.
4. *The isolation may be ill-posed (confounded cut)* — MEDIUM. Supported by SKEPTIC; ConsisVLA-4D's CS-Thinker fuses depth-knowledge + dynamics in one block; 2606.01095 found WAM future-features only "associative." Challenged by ACADEMIC/PRACTITIONER who assume operationalizability.
5. *LIBERO saturation has migrated the prize to OOD substrates, making the result benchmark-political* — MEDIUM. Supported by ECONOMIST, HISTORIAN; LIBERO pinned at 97–98% across ≥6 methods, ≥4 competing OOD suites. Challenged by none but it's sociological, not a metric.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** SKEPTIC's "the cut is confounded" and HISTORIAN's "attribution wars always resolve to the interaction term" are the same claim from two directions: because the consistency mechanism and perceptual representation are co-trained, the *pure* isolation is exactly the framing history says gets overturned — which means the ECONOMIST's advice (own the diagnostic *protocol*) is the de-risking move, because a protocol that reports the interaction term is robust to the result that the isolation alone is not.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not COMMIT to H1 as stated.** Reframe the bet from "consistency, not perception, drives OOD" (a winner-take-all attribution likely to falsify or be ill-posed) to "we deliver the first *consistency-vs-perception attribution protocol* over full space+time 4D, reporting both main effects *and their interaction*, on a real temporal-OOD axis nobody has." Concrete next experiment: take ConsisVLA-4D (2605.05126) as the testbed, define two *provably independent* knobs — (a) freeze the perceptual encoder / depth-knowledge path and ablate ONLY CS-Thinker's consistency attention; (b) the reciprocal: freeze consistency attention and degrade perception via 2512.02902's FTM/FLA dial — then run a 2×2 (consistency on/off × perception aligned/misaligned) on LIBERO-Plus's *isolated* factor axes plus CronusVLA's SimplerEnv-OR temporal-OOD, reporting the interaction term and the ID-vs-OOD delta. Pre-register the falsifier (perception ablation hurts OOD ≥ consistency ablation). Pair with GAM (2606.17046) as the cheap-implicit upper bound and WAM4D (2606.14048) as the train-only-distill control. This converts the fragile "consistency wins" bet into a robust diagnostic contribution that publishes regardless of which arm wins.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Does 4D-consistency's advantage survive *real-robot closed-loop temporal distribution shift* — latency-induced action lag, mid-episode dynamic distractors, contact-timing drift — i.e. the temporal OOD regime where a "4D consistency" claim should be decisive but for which no benchmark yet exists? If yes, B2 graduates from a sim-ablation to a deployment-relevant axis and the whitespace becomes a moat; if the advantage is a sim artifact, the entire implicit-4D narrative is a LIBERO-OOD curiosity.
