#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. I read the card (Spatial-4D.md L442–533) and spot-checked the load-bearing citations against the KnowledgeHub. The briefing is unusually self-aware — it pre-concedes the winner-take-all framing is fragile and pivots to a diagnostic-protocol play. That honesty is the briefing's strongest feature. The problem is that one of the two papers it leans on to justify the pivot does not exist in the vault, and that quietly inflates the confidence on findings #3 and #4.

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

1. **Implicit consistency helps OOD at lower cost than explicit generation — 8/10.** Best-supported claim. The card table is dense and the numbers verify: GAM 83.1% camera-OOD @ ~6.9 ms (KH 2606.17046), ConsisVLA-4D 98.1% LIBERO / 2.31× / 70.0% real bimanual vs OpenVLA 28.5% (KH 2605.05126), LaMP +9.7 pp LIBERO-Plus. The premise is genuinely consensus across ≥6 methods, so it is not at risk. The single deduction: every number is a single-source headline from a paper marketing its own method; there is no matched-backbone head-to-head, so the *magnitude* of "lower cost" is asserted, not measured (the card itself flags the 2.31× as single-source in its Risks block — good).

2. **The exact isolation ablation (perception-frozen, consistency-removed, full-4D OOD-SR) is unpublished — 8/10.** I credit this. The cited papers ablate genuinely adjacent variables (GeoAware = VGGT-layer; VLA-4D = coordinate-chaos / 3D-presence; WAM4D = register placement; GEM-4D = geometry-flow-branch), and I confirmed those framings in the card's own table. The one-point reservation is structural, not empirical: "unpublished" here may mean "ill-posed," which the briefing concedes (see #4). A clean negative-novelty claim is only as strong as the proof the experiment is well-defined.

3. **Perception, not consistency, is at least co-equal as the OOD driver — 6/10.** The 2512.02902 leg is solid and verified (FTM 48.5%→87.1% at 4K params; FLA 94.8% on Libero-V at 99× fewer params — confirmed in KH). But the synthesis ranks this MEDIUM-HIGH on the strength of *two* vault studies "bracketing the question," and the second one (**2606.01095**) is not in the KnowledgeHub — it appears nowhere in the vault except inside this review chain's own Prompt-1 analyst file. So half the evidentiary base for this finding is an unverifiable citation. On 2512.02902 alone the claim is real but weaker than "co-equal driver": that paper shows perception *misalignment* is the brittleness *site* and that cheap re-alignment restores robustness — it does not measure perception against a consistency constraint, so it cannot by itself establish co-equality. Marked down for the phantom citation and the overreach from "perception is the repair site" to "perception is a co-equal causal driver."

4. **The isolation may be ill-posed (confounded cut) — 5/10.** The mechanism-level half is real and I verified it: ConsisVLA-4D's CS-Thinker "learns implicit local object dynamics *and* global depth knowledge" in one Spatiotemporal Consistency Attention block (KH 2605.05126, Method). Depth-knowledge is perception, so "freeze perception, ablate only consistency" is, on the paper's own description, a cut through a single fused block — a legitimate confound worth flagging. But the finding's *second* support ("2606.01095 found WAM future-features only associative") rests entirely on the missing paper. Strip that out and this is a one-source structural worry, not a substantiated finding. The actionable insight then proposes two "provably independent knobs" to dissolve the confound, but provides no evidence the knobs *are* independent given that depth and dynamics share the attention block — the de-risking move is asserted to work, not shown to. 5/10: the worry is credible, the resolution is hand-waved, and one of two supports is fictional.

5. **LIBERO saturation has migrated the prize to OOD substrates, making the result benchmark-political — 6/10.** The descriptive core is well-grounded: LIBERO is pinned at 97–98% across the table (ConsisVLA-4D 98.1, OptimusVLA/GTA-VLA 98.6, LaMP 98.3, GAM 97.6, LaST0 98.1), and ≥4 OOD suites (LIBERO-Plus, LIBERO-X, SimplerEnv-OR, WAM-vs-VLA) compete for the new prize. That much is verifiable from the card. But "benchmark-political" is a sociological read, not a technical finding — it is unfalsifiable as stated and the briefing admits no perspective challenges it ("challenged by none but sociological"). It informs strategy (own the protocol) but does not bear on whether the *bet* is true. Scored as a sound observation that is doing less work than its prominence implies.

**Mean = 6.6, min = 5.**

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding #4 (ill-posed isolation), at 5/10, and the reason is shared with #3: the briefing's claim that **two** vault-controlled studies bracket the question is false. **2606.01095 ("associative, SAE-based WAM-vs-VLA diagnostics") is not in the KnowledgeHub and appears nowhere in the vault except this review's own analyst output (007-B2-1-analyst.md).** Its specific empirical claim — that WAM future-features are "only associative" and therefore not cleanly attributable — is the load-bearing premise for "the cut is confounded," yet it is unsourced.

To verify: (a) confirm 2606.01095 exists, ingest it to `_KnowledgeHub_/2606.01095.md`, and check it actually runs an SAE / representational-attribution diagnostic on WAM vs VLA features that supports the "associative not causal" reading — or strike it and re-grade #3/#4 on 2512.02902 alone. (b) Independently, the deeper weakest *technical* link is whether CS-Thinker's depth-knowledge and object-dynamics sub-pathways are separable at all: this needs a look at ConsisVLA-4D's released code (does the attention factor into addressable heads/sub-modules?) or a query to the authors. If they are inseparable, the proposed 2×2 knob (a) collapses and the whole "freeze perception, ablate consistency" arm is undefined on this testbed — which would force moving the experiment to a model with a literally separate consistency module, changing the COMMIT plan.

##### 3. Bias check

**SKEPTIC over-dominated, with HISTORIAN as its echo.** The briefing explicitly flags this in its "hidden cross-perspective connection": SKEPTIC's "the cut is confounded" and HISTORIAN's "attribution wars resolve to the interaction term" are named as "the same claim from two directions." When two of five perspectives are structurally the same argument, the synthesis is effectively a 4-voice room weighted toward one conclusion — "this will resolve to the interaction term, so report the interaction term." That conclusion is reasonable, but it is reached by a near-doubled vote, and the entire actionable insight (de-risk to a protocol) is the SKEPTIC/HISTORIAN position operationalized. PRACTITIONER and ACADEMIC are present mainly to be "challenged by" the SKEPTIC on every contested finding (#3, #4). The result reads less like five independent analysts converging and more like one strong prior (the bet is probably ill-posed) dressed in five hats. ECONOMIST is the only genuinely orthogonal voice and the briefing itself rates it weakest. So: not balanced — SKEPTIC-led.

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The ENGINEER / reproducibility-practitioner who asks "can this 2×2 actually be instantiated on the released ConsisVLA-4D checkpoint?"** Every perspective here debates whether the *result* will be clean; none checks whether the *experiment is runnable* on the proposed testbed. This matters because the entire de-risked plan is "take ConsisVLA-4D (2605.05126) as testbed and define two provably independent knobs." But (i) ConsisVLA-4D's consistency lives in one fused attention block (verified), so knob (a) may not exist as a clean toggle; (ii) the plan assumes you can "degrade perception via 2512.02902's FTM/FLA dial" on a *different* architecture (ConsisVLA-4D), but FTM/FLA were defined and measured on the 2512.02902 models — there is no evidence the dial transfers; (iii) is the ConsisVLA-4D checkpoint or training code even public? An engineer would surface that the protocol's feasibility, not its outcome, is the real go/no-go. This perspective could flip the conclusion: if the knobs cannot be cleanly instantiated, the recommended "robust diagnostic that publishes regardless of which arm wins" is itself not buildable, and the de-risk evaporates — you would need to *train* a model with a deliberately modular consistency head, which is a different (larger) project than "ablate an existing testbed."

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B / B-.**

A strong, intellectually honest briefing that correctly identifies the novelty as attribution-shaped and narrow, correctly pre-concedes the winner-take-all bet is fragile, and proposes a genuinely better framing (report main effects + interaction). It loses a full grade-step for one disqualifying-if-unfixed flaw and two soft spots:

- **Top fix (blocking): the phantom citation.** 2606.01095 is cited as an existing vault paper in the CEO summary and findings #3/#4 but is not in the KnowledgeHub. Either ingest and verify it, or remove it and re-derive the confidence on #3 (drops toward MEDIUM) and #4 (drops toward MEDIUM-LOW) using only 2512.02902. A briefing that asserts "two controlled studies already in the vault" when there is one is overstating its evidentiary base, and that is exactly the soft-claim inflation this review exists to catch.

- **Second fix: prove the knobs independent before calling them "provably independent."** The 2×2 rests on freezing perception while toggling consistency, but the card's own evidence (CS-Thinker fuses depth-knowledge + dynamics in one block) says the cut runs through a single fused module. Demote "provably independent knobs" to "candidate knobs pending a separability check on the released architecture," and make that check the first milestone, ahead of the ablation.

- **Third fix: de-duplicate SKEPTIC and HISTORIAN.** They are the same claim; collapsing them frees a slot for the missing ENGINEER/feasibility voice, which is where the real risk to the *plan* (vs the bet) lives.

Kept honest, this is a publishable diagnostic-protocol paper. As written, it would get a "revise — your second supporting study does not exist, and your independence assumption is unproven" from any careful committee.

---

### C (Cluster review)

Topic: the COHERENCE + central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster C — "Geometry-Native World Models & Memory" — which groups C1 (occupancy), C2 (4D-video pointmaps), C3 (latent-4D), C4 (persistent memory) along an "explicit-external ↔ latent-internal ↔ persistent decodability axis," with the shared bottleneck: *"World models imagine in pixels; geometry is recovered after the fact, isn't externally usable, isn't natively 4D, and isn't kept over long horizons."* The cluster intro asserts the unifying claim that *"the world model's representation is geometry, not appearance — a model-agnostic substrate any policy or model can use."* This review asks whether that grouping carves the problem at the right joint and whether the shared bet is sound.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's "geometry-as-substrate" thesis is operationally seductive but the four directions hide wildly different deployment maturity behind one banner. C3 is real today (X-WAM [[2604.26694]] runs 15 Hz, PointWorld [[2601.03782]] 0.12 s/10-step at 1B params), but C1's occupancy-vs-latent horizon win is unproven at tabletop sub-cm, and C4's "one memory layer over all three substrates" is engineering vapor until someone builds it.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: X-WAM's 4.5× action-latency speedup (4665→1033 ms, 5 steps) and 79.2% RoboCasa, plus GAM [[2606.17046]] at ~6.9 ms/pass (~145 Hz, 55× faster than diffusion), prove geometry-native CAN deploy. But the deployment killer the card under-weights: every direction's risk callout concedes geometry needs depth/3D GT *not present in most robot datasets* — C1 ("occupancy supervision scarcity"), C3 ("4D supervision needs depth/3D GT"), C4 ("reliable 3D lifting fails on texture-poor scenes," MosaicMem [[2603.17117]]). The substrate is only "free" in sim.
- *Only this perspective says*: The cluster's coherence is a representation taxonomy, not a deployment taxonomy. A practitioner picks a substrate by *where the supervision comes from*, not by decodability. The right joint for a builder is "occupancy-recoverable-in-sim (C1) vs depth-sensor-available (C3) vs neither (fall back to pixel)" — an axis the card never draws.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The cluster is unusually *honest* about its own pre-emption — C3 explicitly concedes X-WAM owns native-vs-recovered and PointWorld owns the broad transfer claim; C4 concedes 3D Persistent Embodied WM [[2505.05495]] owns the "geometric memory beats attention-only" headline. This honesty is the cluster's strongest feature and its biggest liability: once you subtract the conceded headlines, the *shared* bet shrinks to four narrow residual deltas (occupancy-vs-latent horizon, cross-view-consistency-for-tracker, 1–4-step distillation, substrate-agnostic-layer) that share a *motivation* but not a *mechanism*.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The literature already crosses the cluster's internal boundaries: GEM-4D [[2605.22882]] appears as a foil in C1, C2, *and* C3 — the same paper threatens three directions, which means the directions are slices of one continuous geometry-on-rollout problem, not four orthogonal bets. Phys4D [[2603.03485]] (fidelity) and WorldArena [[2602.08971]] (utility, r=0.825 perceptual vs r=0.360 action-planning) already supply the measurement layer C1/C3 claim is missing.
- *Only this perspective says*: The "decodability axis" (external→latent→persistent) is a real and defensible carving — but C4 is a *different kind* of thing (a persistence operator, orthogonal to the substrate) and doesn't belong on the same axis as C1/C2/C3 (which are substrate choices). The cluster conflates "what the geometry is" (C1/C2/C3) with "how long it lasts" (C4).

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The cluster's central premise — that the field "imagines in pixels and recovers geometry after the fact" — is *empirically false by the card's own admission*, repeatedly. C1's own first-principles row calls the old "field skips occupancy" framing "empirically false" (DSR-Net [[2011.01968]] did occupancy-manipulation in 2020; 3D-Occ-MPC [[2505.16249]] instantiates it). C3 concedes native-vs-recovered is "already settled." If the shared bottleneck is already addressed in three of four directions, the cluster thesis is a retrofit narrative around residual ablations.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: Kairos [[2606.16533]] (Jun 2026) is the existential threat: it delivers the *exact* cluster-level promise — a "native world model stack" that is persistent (proven-contractive GLA memory over hundreds of frames), cross-embodiment, deployment-ready (4-step distilled, 23.5 GB, SOTA LIBERO-Plus 90.8 / RoboTwin-2.0 96.1) — *without* geometry being the substrate. Kairos imagines in pixels/latent and still solves persistence + deployment + control-grounding, the very bottlenecks C names. So the cluster's load-bearing claim ("geometry, not appearance, must be the substrate") is contested by a working system.
- *Only this perspective says*: The whitespace isn't "is geometry a good substrate" (yes, conceded everywhere) — it's the *unproven causal claim* that explicit geometry is *necessary*. Kairos is the falsifier the cluster doesn't list: if a pixel/latent stack with structured memory matches geometry-native on horizon + transfer, the entire cluster motivation collapses to "geometry is *one* good substrate," not "the" substrate.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The cluster rides a 2026 industry wave (Tsinghua/Xiaomi X-WAM, Fei-Fei/NVIDIA PointWorld, the anonymous well-resourced "Kairos Team") where the prize is the *foundation-model substrate* for physical AI — whoever owns the substrate owns the stack. An academic betting on the *same* substrate framing is competing with labs that have orders-of-magnitude more compute and proprietary data (Kairos: 100k+ hrs human data, in-house crawl).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The benchmark-chasing tell: RoboCasa (X-WAM 79.2%, GeoPredict [[2512.16811]] +10.1pp, Pri4R [[2603.01549]] +13.2%) and LIBERO (≥98% across GeoSem-WAM, GaussianDream, VLA-JEPA) are saturating — the card itself flags "headline LIBERO SR" as uninformative and routes to RoboWM-Bench [[2604.19092]] / Phys4D / WorldArena. Saturation is why the cluster's *new metric* contributions (horizon-to-divergence, perception-functionality gap) are where the defensible value is, not the substrate.
- *Only this perspective says*: The academic's edge is NOT building a better substrate (industry wins on compute) — it's the *measurement and adjudication* layer. ContactWorld [[2606.13877]] (point-cloud 32.1% vs image 20.7% planning SR) and Phys4D are the kind of "referee" contributions a small lab can own and that industry needs but won't prioritize. Bet on the ruler, not the runner.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle replayed in geometry's costume. Dreamer's latent imagination beat pixel reconstruction not because latent was "more correct" but because it was *cheaper to roll out*; the field then rediscovered that explicit structure helps on structured tasks (the modular-vs-end-to-end swing). Cluster C is the "explicit structure helps" half-cycle — historically these win on the narrow structured tasks they target and lose the general race to whatever scales cheapest.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card already contains the counter-swing within itself: the train-time-auxiliary-then-drop family (Pri4R, GaussianDream [[2605.20752]], GeoSem-WAM [[2606.03188]], GigaWorld-Policy [[2603.17240]], WAM4D [[2606.14048]]) gets geometry's *benefit* while keeping deployment *pixel-light* — the "have your cake and eat it" pattern that historically wins (cf. privileged-information distillation in sim2real). If geometry-as-train-signal matches geometry-as-substrate at deploy, history says the substrate bet loses.
- *Only this perspective says*: Every "substrate war" (pixel vs latent vs token vs explicit-3D, per the World Models survey [[2510.16732]] taxonomy) has ended not with one winner but with *hybridization* — which is exactly C1's H3 and C4's hybrid-memory rows. The cluster's most durable contribution will be the *complementarity* findings (occupancy-for-horizon × latent-for-fidelity), not the substrate-purity claim.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Direct clashes.**
- *Skeptic vs the cluster's framing (and Academic)*: Skeptic says the "imagines-in-pixels" bottleneck is already dissolved (Kairos solves persistence+deploy+control without geometry; C1/C3 concede prior work). The cluster (backed partly by Academic's "honest residuals" reading) says the residual deltas — occupancy-vs-latent horizon, cross-view-for-tracker, 1–4-step distillation, substrate-agnostic layer — are genuine untaken whitespace. Specific clash: is "geometry-native substrate" a *necessary* mechanism (cluster) or merely *one sufficient option among several* (Skeptic, citing Kairos's pixel/latent success)?
- *Historian/Economist vs Practitioner/Cluster*: Historian+Economist say the train-time-auxiliary-then-drop family (Pri4R, GeoPredict) and pixel-light unified stacks (Kairos) get geometry's benefit without paying the deploy/supervision tax — so "keep geometry online as the substrate" (C1/C3 core) is the losing side of the swing. Practitioner partly agrees (supervision is the killer) but notes X-WAM/GAM *do* deploy online today, so online-geometry isn't dead.
- *Academic vs cluster carving*: Academic disputes that C4 belongs on the same axis as C1/C2/C3 — persistence is orthogonal to substrate, so the "single decodability axis" is two axes wearing one label.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
- *Strongest*: SKEPTIC. It is the only perspective that names a *concrete, dated, working falsifier* (Kairos) for the cluster's load-bearing necessity claim, and it grounds the attack in the cluster's own repeated concessions ("empirically false," "already settled"). It converts a vibe ("is this whitespace?") into a testable necessity question.
- *Weakest*: ECONOMIST. Its core insight (bet on the ruler not the runner) is genuinely actionable, but it is the least grounded in the *specific* mechanism dispute — it would apply to almost any substrate cluster and doesn't adjudicate whether geometry-native is right, only who should build it.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
Run the head-to-head the cluster keeps deferring but against the *right* opponent: **on a matched manipulation suite (RoboWM-Bench step+final SR + Phys4D 4D-Chamfer-over-horizon), does a geometry-native online substrate (X-WAM / occupancy) beat a structured-latent stack with persistent memory (Kairos-class GLA, or LaWM [[2605.08279]]'s Least-Action latent) on horizon-to-divergence AND downstream SR — at matched compute?** If geometry-native wins, the cluster's necessity claim survives; if the structured latent matches it, the cluster collapses to "geometry is one good substrate" (Historian/Skeptic win).

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
Explicit geometry *helps* on geometry-bound / contact-rich tasks, and the *measurement* layer (horizon-to-divergence, perception-vs-functionality gap, contact-rich planning SR) is real, under-served, and where defensible value sits. Every perspective — even the Skeptic — concedes geometry beats pixel-only on contact/spatial tasks (ContactWorld 32.1% vs 20.7%; X-WAM native CD 0.0049 vs recovered 0.0401). No one argues geometry is *useless*; they argue about *necessity vs sufficiency* and *online-substrate vs train-time-signal*.

**(5) The blind spot no perspective fully addressed.**
**Where the geometry comes from at deploy time on a real robot with no depth GT and no privileged sim.** Every direction's risk callout waves at "off-the-shelf depth estimators" or "end-effector-derived poses," but none of the five perspectives — nor the cluster — treats the *quality of the estimated geometry* as the actual independent variable. The whole cluster assumes geometry is available-and-clean; the real-world failure mode is that estimated occupancy/pointmaps on a texture-poor, cluttered, novel scene are *worse than no geometry*, and no direction tests the substrate under realistic geometry-estimation noise. (C4's MosaicMem risk gestures at it but only for memory, not the substrate.)

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.**
Cluster C is a well-organized, intellectually honest grouping built on a real and partly-proven insight — explicit geometry helps world models on contact-rich and spatially-bound tasks — but its *cluster-level* thesis ("geometry, not appearance, is THE model-agnostic substrate") is stronger than the evidence and is now actively contested by working pixel/latent systems (Kairos) that achieve the same persistence, deployment, and control-grounding goals without geometry as the substrate. The four directions share a motivation, not a mechanism: C1/C2/C3 are competing *substrate* choices while C4 is an orthogonal *persistence* operator, and three of the four already concede their headline result to prior work, leaving narrow-but-real residual deltas. The durable, defensible value is not the substrate claim itself but two things every lens converges on: the *complementarity* findings (geometry-for-horizon × latent-for-fidelity) and the *measurement/adjudication* layer (horizon-to-divergence, perception–functionality gap, contact-rich planning SR). Commit to the cluster as a *measurement-and-complementarity* program, not as a substrate-purity bet.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Explicit geometry beats pixel-only on geometry-bound/contact tasks* — HIGHEST reliability. Supported by all five perspectives + hard numbers (ContactWorld 32.1% vs 20.7%; X-WAM CD 0.0049 vs 0.0401, SR 63.0→67.8). Challenged by none. This is the cluster's bedrock.
2. *Three of four directions concede their headline to prior work; the live whitespace is residual deltas* — HIGH reliability. Supported by Academic + Skeptic, grounded in the card's own "already settled / empirically false" language (C1 vs DSR-Net/3D-Occ-MPC, C3 vs X-WAM/PointWorld, C4 vs 3D Persistent Embodied WM). Challenged only by the optimistic reading that residuals are still worth a paper each.
3. *The cluster-level "geometry is THE substrate" necessity claim is contested, not whitespace* — HIGH reliability. Supported by Skeptic + Historian, grounded in Kairos [[2606.16533]] (pixel/latent, persistent, deployed, SOTA) and the train-time-drop family (Pri4R, GeoPredict). Challenged by Practitioner (X-WAM/GAM deploy geometry online today) — so it's "geometry is sufficient and competitive," not "necessary."
4. *C4 is mis-filed on the substrate axis — persistence is orthogonal to representation* — MEDIUM-HIGH reliability. Supported by Academic; consistent with Historian's hybridization view. Challenged by the card's framing that the decodability axis is single and continuous. Carving fix, not a kill.
5. *The defensible academic value is the measurement/adjudication layer, not the substrate* — MEDIUM reliability (it's a strategic claim). Supported by Economist + Academic + Historian; the card half-agrees by routing to Phys4D/WorldArena/ContactWorld/RoboWM-Bench. Challenged by Practitioner (metrics don't deploy robots) — true, but they de-risk and they're ownable by a small lab.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.**
The Economist's "bet on the ruler not the runner" and the Historian's "substrate wars end in hybridization, the durable contribution is the complementarity finding" are the *same insight* from different angles: in a saturating, industry-dominated substrate race, the small-lab-defensible artifact is the thing that *adjudicates between substrates* (a ruler that scores horizon × fidelity × cost across occupancy/latent/pixel), which is *also* the only instrument that can establish the hybridization/complementarity claims (C1-H3, C4 hybrid memory). Measurement and complementarity are one program, and it is the program that survives both the compute asymmetry and the historical swing.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher — DE-RISK (re-frame and re-target), do not COMMIT as written or DROP.**
The cluster is too rich and too partly-proven to drop, but committing to it as a "geometry-is-the-substrate" bet walks straight into Kairos and the compute asymmetry. De-risk by:
- (a) Re-frame the cluster contribution from *substrate-purity* to *substrate-adjudication + complementarity*: the paper's headline becomes "when does explicit-geometry-online beat structured-latent-with-memory, and where do they compose," not "geometry wins."
- (b) Demote the necessity claim to a sufficiency-and-frontier claim; explicitly add Kairos-class structured-latent (and LaWM's Least-Action latent) as the *primary* baseline, not occupancy-vs-X-WAM in isolation.
- (c) Collapse the four directions' overlapping foils (GEM-4D, Phys4D, WorldArena recur across C1/C2/C3) into one shared evaluation spine.
- *Concrete next experiment*: the Prompt-2 Q3 head-to-head — geometry-native online (X-WAM / occupancy) vs structured-latent-with-persistent-memory (a Kairos/GLA-style or LaWM latent) on RoboWM-Bench step+final SR scored for horizon-to-divergence via Phys4D's 4D-Chamfer-over-horizon, *at matched compute and under realistic estimated-geometry noise* (addressing the blind spot). One result resolves findings #1, #3, and the central contradiction simultaneously, and whichever way it lands the *measurement instrument* is a publishable contribution.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.**
Is explicit geometry a *substrate* the world model should be built in, or a *training signal / evaluation oracle* the world model should be shaped by and judged against — i.e., should geometry live online at deploy, or has the field already shown (Pri4R, GeoPredict, GaussianDream, Kairos) that geometry's entire causal benefit can be distilled into a pixel/latent model and dropped at inference, leaving "geometry-native online" a deployment liability with no remaining accuracy advantage? If the answer is "training signal," the whole cluster reorganizes around C's train-time-drop rows and the substrate directions become legacy.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. I read Cluster C (Spatial-4D.md L534–869: C1 occupancy, C2 pointmaps-for-pose, C3 native-4D, C4 persistent memory) and spot-checked the load-bearing papers in `_KnowledgeHub_/`. Verdict up front: the briefing is unusually honest and the DE-RISK call is correct, but its single most important counterexample (Kairos) is asserted, not wired into the card it reviews, and two findings lean on a contradiction the cluster's own rows already half-resolve.*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

**Finding 1 — Explicit geometry beats pixel-only on geometry-bound/contact tasks. Score: 9/10.**
Verified directly. ContactWorld's point-cloud 32.1% vs wrist-view-image 20.7% (+tactile 36.1%) is in the card (L743) and the KH note (2606.13877) matches. X-WAM's CD 0.0049 vs 0.0401 and SR 63.0→67.8 appear 12× consistently and are a *self-reported single-system ablation*, which the card itself flags (C3-H1 L750). The one deduction: every datum is a within-paper ablation or a single benchmark, not a matched-backbone third-party replication, so "bedrock" is right for *existence* of the effect but the *magnitude* is not yet adversarially confirmed. The finding survives.

**Finding 2 — Three of four directions concede their headline to prior work; live whitespace is residual deltas. Score: 8/10.**
Well-grounded and the most defensible structural claim. C1 concedes to DSR-Net (2011.01968, IoU 0.72) and 3D-Occ-MPC (2505.16249); C3 concedes native-vs-recovered to X-WAM and broad transfer to PointWorld (the card's own C3 risk box L778 says exactly this); C4 concedes the headline to PEWM (2505.05495, SRC 81.7% vs 63.4%, L790). I confirmed all four anchors are in KH. Deduction: "residual deltas" is doing a lot of work, the card frames them as live (H2 step-distillation, H1 layer-reuse), but whether a delta is a *paper* or a *footnote* is a judgment the briefing asserts rather than sizes. Still, the concession structure is real and faithfully reported.

**Finding 3 — The "geometry is THE substrate" necessity claim is contested, not whitespace. Score: 6/10.**
The *insight* is correct and important, but the *evidence handling* is the weakest part of the whole package. I confirmed Kairos (2606.16533) is in KH and its note substantiates the briefing's read: a native DiT + hybrid-linear-temporal-attention WM that achieves long-horizon persistence ("geometrically damping error accumulation" via attention, not a geometric grid) and efficient deploy, WorldModelBench-robot 9.30, LIBERO-Plus 90.8. So the necessity claim *is* genuinely contested. **But Kairos appears nowhere in Cluster C** (grep: zero hits in the entire Spatial-4D.md). The briefing's central contesting witness is not a row in the card it is reviewing, it is imported by the reviewer. That is a finding *about a gap in the card*, not a finding *the card supports* — and the briefing blurs the two. Separately, the "Practitioner challenge" half (GAM ~145 Hz, PointWorld 0.12s real Franka — both verified) is in the card and genuinely shows geometry-online deploys *today*, which means "necessity vs sufficiency" is the right frame but "deployment liability" (frontier question) is overstated against the cluster's own rows.

**Finding 4 — C4 is mis-filed on the substrate axis; persistence is orthogonal. Score: 8/10.**
Correct and the card half-admits it: C4's own first-principles line calls memory "substrate-orthogonal" (L795) and H1 is literally "one layer transfers across C1/C2/C3." So C4 is an operator over a substrate, not a competing substrate. The taxonomic critique is sound. Deduction: this is a framing/carving fix with low stakes for the research itself (the experiments don't change), so its *conclusion-altering* weight is modest — it tidies the cluster thesis rather than redirecting work.

**Finding 5 — Defensible value is the measurement/adjudication layer, not the substrate. Score: 6/10.**
The most interesting strategic claim and the one I'd most push back on. The supporting instruments are real and in KH (Phys4D 2603.03485 4D-Chamfer-over-horizon; WorldArena 2602.08971 with the explicit r=0.825-perceptual-vs-r=0.360-action-planning *perception–functionality gap*; ContactWorld; RoboWM-Bench 2604.19092). The "bet on the ruler not the runner" logic is genuinely attractive for a small lab under compute asymmetry. But two soft spots: (a) the briefing under-weights that Phys4D/WorldArena/ContactWorld *already exist and are recent* — the whitespace is "compose them into one spine + add estimated-geometry noise," which is an integration contribution, not a new instrument, and integration benchmarks are notoriously hard to get adopted; (b) the Practitioner objection ("metrics don't deploy robots") is dismissed too fast — a measurement program that resolves findings 1+3 is publishable, but "publishable" and "the durable defensible artifact for *this* lab" are not the same claim, and the briefing slides between them.

**Mean confidence: 7.4. Lowest: 6.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

The least-confident claim is **Finding 3's load-bearing use of Kairos as the thing that makes "geometry is THE substrate" *contested rather than whitespace*** — because the briefing presents Kairos as adjudicating evidence while the card under review never cites it. What would verify (or correct) it:
- **Wire Kairos into the card and re-score the contest.** Specifically: is Kairos a *latent* substrate with structured persistence (in which case it competes with C4's persistence layer and C3's latent corner, sharpening rather than killing the cluster), or does it genuinely subsume the geometry rows on the *contact/geometry-bound* tasks where Finding 1 is bedrock? The KH note shows LIBERO-Plus and WorldModelBench-robot numbers — **neither is a contact-rich/geometry-bound suite** (LIBERO-Plus is robustness-perturbation; WorldModelBench-robot is physics-adherence/instruction-following). So the one place Finding 1 is strongest (ContactWorld 32.1 vs 20.7) is *exactly where Kairos has not been measured*. Until a Kairos-class latent is run on ContactWorld / Phys4D-4D-Chamfer-over-horizon, "necessity is contested" is a plausible hypothesis, not an established result — and the briefing's own proposed next experiment is the right instrument to settle it.

---

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The Economist + Historian over-dominated; the Practitioner was systematically under-weighted.** The synthesis converges, a little too cleanly, on "bet on the ruler, substrate wars end in hybridization, build the measurement-and-complementarity program." That is the Economist's compute-asymmetry frame and the Historian's swing-toward-hybrid frame, and the briefing itself flags they are "the same insight" — which is presented as a strength (hidden connection) but is also a *tell* that two of five lenses agreed so hard they collapsed into one and pulled the conclusion with them. The Practitioner consistently appears only as the dissent that gets noted-and-overridden (findings 3 and 5 both list "Practitioner challenges" and then proceed past it). Yet the Practitioner has the cluster's strongest *positive* evidence on its side: GAM (~145 Hz) and PointWorld (real-time zero-shot Franka) are not hypotheses, they are deployed geometry-native systems in the card. A more balanced synthesis would treat "geometry-online already deploys and competes" as a co-equal finding, not a challenge to route around. The Skeptic is well-represented (it drives findings 2 and 3). The Academic is the connective tissue. Net: **not balanced — Economist/Historian-weighted, Practitioner-discounted.**

---

##### 4. Missing perspective (6th angle that would change conclusions)

**The Data/Supervision-economist (the "where does the geometry GT come from" lens).** Every one of C1–C4 carries a quiet supervision tax that the five perspectives never price: occupancy GT in manipulation (C1's own risk L614), cross-view pointmap supervision (C2), depth/3D GT for native-4D (C3's risk L776), reliable 3D lifting for memory (C4's risk L867). The whole "geometry vs latent" contest may be *decided by annotation availability*, not by horizon-to-divergence: Kairos and VLA-JEPA win partly *because* they need no geometric supervision and can drink open-world video, while the geometry substrates are gated to "where occupancy/depth GT is recoverable." If that lens is added, the conclusion shifts: the durable small-lab artifact may not be "the ruler" but **the cheapest geometry-supervision pipeline that makes the substrate competitive at all** — and the central experiment must run *under realistic estimated-geometry noise* (which, to its credit, the briefing's next-experiment already half-includes as "the blind spot," but never elevates to a perspective). A second candidate sixth lens is the **Hardware/sensor realist** (does the robot have depth/multi-view at deploy?), which would similarly re-rank C2/C3 by sensor assumptions rather than by mechanism.

---

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: A-.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest briefing — it refuses to rubber-stamp its own cluster, correctly identifies that three of four directions have been scooped to residual deltas, names the right strategic pivot (adjudication + complementarity over substrate-purity), and proposes a single experiment that genuinely resolves multiple findings. That is graduate-seminar-A work. It falls short of an A/A+ for three repairable reasons.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Wire the central counterexample into the card.** Kairos (and the train-time-drop family Pri4R/GeoPredict/GaussianDream — all in KH, all in C3's table *except Kairos*) carries Finding 3 and the frontier question, yet Kairos is absent from Cluster C. Either add it as a row (latent-with-structured-persistence, the "necessity falsifier") or the necessity-is-contested claim remains reviewer-imported, not card-supported. This is the gap between "a good review" and "a good direction doc."
2. **Run the contest where Finding 1 is strongest, not where it is silent.** Demote "necessity is contested" to a hypothesis until a Kairos-class latent is scored on ContactWorld / Phys4D-4D-Chamfer-over-horizon — Kairos's reported wins are on LIBERO-Plus/WorldModelBench, the *appearance/robustness* suites, not the contact/geometry-bound suites where geometry's edge is bedrock. The two findings (1 and 3) are not yet in contact with each other on the same axis.
3. **Re-balance toward the Practitioner and price the supervision tax.** Treat "GAM/PointWorld deploy geometry-online today" as a co-equal positive finding, soften the frontier question's "deployment liability" framing (the cluster's own rows refute it), and add the missing Data/Supervision lens so the next experiment's "estimated-geometry noise" condition is elevated from a footnote to a load-bearing variable. Whichever way the experiment lands, the supervision cost — not just horizon-to-divergence — may be what actually decides the substrate question.

---

### C1 (Direction review)

Topic: the bet that an explicit voxel-semantic occupancy world model beats a latent-4D baseline (sibling C3, X-WAM) by ≥1 order of magnitude on *horizon-to-divergence* at tabletop manipulation scale (the head-to-head 3D-Occ-MPC and DSR-Net never ran), while *losing* on per-frame Chamfer (latent-4D's 0.0049), making the two complementary not competitive, and its Warp-DiT rigid-transform error bound survives to sub-cm.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The card's win condition (frames-to-geometric-divergence) is a simulator metric, not a deployment metric; what kills manipulation WMs in the lab is not 50-vs-3,000-frame drift but whether you can get a clean occupancy grid in real-time off real sensors at sub-cm and feed it to a planner inside the control loop. Occupancy supervision in manipulation is the actual wall, not horizon length.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: 2604.19092 (RoboWM-Bench) is the practitioner's smoking gun — it shows perceptually-plausible video WMs fail on *executability*, and that human-hand videos out-execute robot videos because of pretraining bias; visual fidelity is "insufficient." 2604.26694 (X-WAM) only hits 15 Hz / 1033 ms action latency *with* 5-step async denoising; a 64³ occupancy grid (2509.05314 ManipDreamer3D) plus A*+CHOMP adds planner latency the card never budgets. 2505.16249 (3D-Occ-MPC) needs *multi-view* RGB to infer occupancy under self-occlusion — a multi-camera rig most real cells don't have.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Decide the occupancy-supervision story before any horizon experiment — the Risks section flags it but treats sim-derived occupancy as "free." On real robots, depth+gripper-geometry occupancy is noisy precisely at the sub-cm scale H4 must defend; if you can't get clean GT occupancy, H1 is unfalsifiable on hardware and you're left with a sim-only paper.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the literature).**
- *Core position*: The decomposition into H1–H5 is unusually rigorous and the "substrate choice bounds horizon, not the same as per-frame fidelity" thesis is genuinely under-tested. But the literature already contains the *adjacent* controlled substrate study (2605.06388, "Reconstruction or Semantics?"), which establishes the meta-claim "latent-space choice changes WM utility beyond pixel fidelity" — so the card's novelty must be pinned narrowly to *explicit-occupancy-vs-latent on geometric horizon*, not to "substrate choice matters."
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: 2605.06388 runs a fixed-everything-but-encoder LDM study on Bridge V2 and finds semantic latents beat reconstruction latents on action-recoverability/policy-success *even at equal pixel fidelity* — exactly the card's "fidelity ≠ utility" logic, but it never includes an occupancy substrate or a frames-to-divergence metric. 2011.01968 (DSR-Net) already showed voxel state beats SE3-Net (IoU 0.72 vs 0.31) in 2020, and 2505.05512 (Occupancy World Model) ports occupancy WMs driving→indoor (+22.34 IoU) — so "occupancy works in manipulation" is settled; the open question is strictly the *horizon comparison vs latent*.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The card's own table contains its toughest falsifier and names it (2605.08279 LaWM): a physics-structured latent (Least-Action transition rule) that *already bounds long-horizon geometric drift* (acceleration PIS 0.6568→0.8964) in a latent substrate. If LaWM closes the horizon gap, the driving-to-tabletop win was the *structure-preserving transition rule*, not the explicit grid — meaning the contribution is "structure," achievable in either substrate, and the occupancy framing is incidental. This must be the lead baseline, not a footnote.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is an artifact of an over-specified metric. "Nobody ran the occupancy-vs-latent horizon head-to-head at tabletop scale" is true only because the field has tacitly judged the comparison uninteresting — explicit occupancy and latent WMs are evaluated on the metrics that matter for *control* (executability, planning SR), where 2605.06388 and 2604.19092 already adjudicate substrate utility, and on those metrics occupancy has no demonstrated edge.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: 2603.28887 (OccSim) — the entire empirical engine of the bet — is a *driving* result (4 km, multi-kilometer maps); every quantitative claim (3,000 frames, 80×, +22.1% mIoU, 67% zero-shot) is at meter-scale street geometry. The card concedes H4 (sub-cm Warp-DiT) is the go/no-go and that occupancy "validated at meter-scale." Rigid-transform constraints are most powerful when scenes are dominated by rigid static structure (roads/buildings); tabletop manipulation is contact-rich, deformable (the elasto-plastic objects 2505.16249 targets), and articulated — exactly where a rigid-transform error bound is weakest. The 80× horizon gain may simply not exist once geometry stops being rigid.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: The complementarity escape hatch (H3, "C1 is not a better X-WAM, it's the substrate X-WAM isn't") is unfalsifiable-by-design — if occupancy loses on horizon you call it complementary, if it wins you call it superior. Pre-register H1's falsifier (occupancy ≤ latent horizon ⇒ scale-specific) and commit to dropping the direction on that outcome, or the bet can never lose.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows incentives).**
- *Core position*: The occupancy-WM narrative is subsidized by the autonomous-driving industry (nuScenes/occupancy-prediction tooling, OccSim, IR-WM 2510.16729 all driving), and the manipulation-WM benchmark economy (RoboCasa, RoboWM-Bench) rewards papers that plug a new substrate into an existing suite. C1 is cheap to *start* (sim occupancy is free) but the incentive gradient pushes toward a sim-only horizon-plot paper that benchmark-chases rather than the expensive real-robot sub-cm validation that would actually de-risk deployment.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The supply side is moving fast and is well-capitalized — alphaxiv surfaces 2601.03782 (PointWorld, *Stanford + NVIDIA*, "scaling 3D world models for in-the-wild manipulation"), 2508.17600 (GWM, Tsinghua/NTU/BIGAI Gaussian WM), 2606.13672 (WEAVER, "better/faster/longer" manipulation WM). Explicit-geometry manipulation WMs are a contested, industrially-funded land-grab, not a quiet whitespace. The card's anchor targets are all borrowed metrics (X-WAM's 79.2% RoboCasa, OccSim's 22.1%), i.e. benchmark-chasing infrastructure already exists, which lowers the cost of a fast-follower scooping the exact comparison.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Time-to-scoop is the real risk, not novelty. With PointWorld/GWM/WEAVER shipping monthly and a clear shared bench (RoboWM-Bench), the occupancy-vs-latent horizon plot is a 3-month experiment any of those labs can bolt on. Either move first with a sharp sim H1 result and a falsifier, or differentiate on the part nobody else is funded to do (real sub-cm H4 + planner-readable H2), which is also the part with defensible moat.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle replayed in 3D. Dreamer/PlaNet (latent, compact, drift-prone but trainable) vs explicit/reconstructive models recurs every few years; the field oscillates because each substrate wins a different metric and neither dominates both — exactly the complementarity C1 predicts (H3). The honest historical read is that "complementary, not competitive" is usually where these debates *end*, which makes C1's complementarity claim plausible but also makes the "≥1 order of magnitude" framing the part likely to not survive.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card itself encodes the cycle: 2510.10125 (CTRL-WORLD, controllable pixel), 2604.26694 (X-WAM, latent-4D, per-frame-excellent / horizon-weak), 2603.28887 (OccSim, explicit, horizon-strong / per-frame-untested at manipulation), 2504.20995 (TesserAct, depth-as-channel midpoint), 2605.08279 (LaWM, structure-in-the-latent). That is the full pixel→latent→channel→structured→explicit ladder — historically the resolution is hybridization (H3), not a substrate knockout (H1). The sim2real-history rhyme is exact: occupancy was a driving abstraction (like DR was a sim2real trick) that the field is now trying to transplant; transplants succeed when the *invariant* transfers, fail when the source-domain assumption (rigid scenes) doesn't.
- *The one thing only this perspective says*: Bet on H3 (hybrid) as the durable contribution and treat H1's order-of-magnitude as the attention-grabbing-but-likely-to-soften headline. Every prior substrate war ended in a hybrid; positioning C1 as "the occupancy-latent handoff recipe" ages better than "occupancy wins."

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Direct clashes.**
- *Skeptic vs Card-as-written (H1 magnitude)*: Skeptic says rigid-transform bounds are weakest exactly where manipulation lives (contact-rich/deformable/articulated), so the 80× driving gain likely evaporates; the card asserts occupancy beats latent by ≥1 order of magnitude on horizon. Concrete: does Warp-DiT's per-step bound hold on 2505.16249's elasto-plastic objects, or only on rigid static scenes like OccSim's roads?
- *Academic vs Skeptic on what's "solved"*: Academic says the *specific* occupancy-vs-latent horizon comparison is genuinely unrun (DSR-Net/3D-Occ-MPC/ManipDreamer3D all skip it); Skeptic says it's unrun because it's uninteresting given 2605.06388 already adjudicates substrate utility on control metrics. Clash: is "frames-to-geometric-divergence" a real missing axis or a metric chosen to manufacture novelty?
- *Practitioner vs Economist on where to spend*: Practitioner says solve real occupancy supervision / planner latency first (deployment reality); Economist says move fast on the cheap sim H1 plot before PointWorld/GWM/WEAVER scoop it. Clash: real-robot rigor (slow, defensible) vs sim-first speed (fast, scoopable).
- *Historian vs Card on framing*: Historian says the durable result is H3 (hybrid handoff), the card leads with H1 (occupancy wins). Clash: headline = "occupancy beats latent" vs "occupancy-latent complementarity recipe."

**(2) Strongest vs weakest.**
- *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — it is the only perspective that both grants the genuine gap *and* names the exact paper (2605.08279 LaWM) that could dissolve it (structure-not-substrate), and it is corroborated by the alphaxiv-surfaced 2605.06388 which independently validates "substrate choice matters beyond fidelity." It cuts closest to whether the contribution is occupancy *per se* or structure-preserving transitions.
- *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — directionally right (substrate wars end in hybrids) but least falsifiable and least grounded in a specific 2026 metric; "it'll probably hybridize" is a prior, not evidence, and could be wrong if rigid-transform bounds genuinely transfer.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
Run occupancy vs LaWM's physics-structured latent (2605.08279) on matched RoboWM-Bench (2604.19092) long-horizon tasks: does the explicit occupancy grid still beat a *drift-bounded* latent on frames-to-geometric-divergence? If LaWM matches it, the win is the structure-preserving transition rule (achievable in latent), not the explicit grid — collapsing the Academic-vs-card and Skeptic-vs-card clashes at once. This is exactly H1's named falsifier, and it should be the *first* experiment, not the last.

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
Per-frame fidelity (Chamfer/PSNR) is the wrong target for long-horizon manipulation WM utility — executability/planning-SR and horizon-stability are what matter. RoboWM-Bench (2604.19092), 2605.06388, X-WAM's horizon-weakness, and OccSim's horizon-strength all point the same way. The "fidelity ≠ control utility" premise underneath C1 is robust even if the occupancy-specific bet fails.

**(5) The blind spot (none addressed).**
Dynamic-agent / multi-object interaction and partial observability. OccSim's stability comes from a *decoupled* static-scene rollout plus separately-injected dynamic agents (DiT-S layouts); manipulation is fundamentally about the moving object the gripper acts on, under heavy self-occlusion (2505.16249's core problem). None of the five perspectives, nor the hypotheses H1–H5, isolate whether the rigid-transform horizon bound applies to the *manipulated dynamic object* (the part that matters) versus the *static background* (the part the bound is built for) — the bound may hold beautifully for the table and shelves and not at all for the cube being pushed.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** C1 bets that an explicit occupancy world model beats a latent-4D one by an order of magnitude on long-horizon geometric stability at tabletop scale, while conceding latent wins per-frame fidelity, so the two are complementary. The bet's *premise* (substrate choice bounds horizon, fidelity ≠ utility) is well-supported and the specific occupancy-vs-latent frames-to-divergence head-to-head genuinely has not been run — but the whitespace is narrower and more contested than the card implies: a controlled latent-vs-latent substrate study (2605.06388) already owns the meta-claim, the entire empirical engine (OccSim) is a *driving* result whose rigid-transform bound is weakest exactly where manipulation is hardest (contact/deformable/articulated/occluded dynamic objects), and well-funded labs (PointWorld=Stanford+NVIDIA, GWM, WEAVER) are land-grabbing explicit-geometry manipulation WMs monthly. The single load-bearing risk is that the driving gain is *structure*, not the explicit grid: LaWM (2605.08279) already bounds long-horizon geometric drift inside a latent.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Fidelity ≠ long-horizon control utility; the right axis is horizon/executability.* Highest reliability — supported by all five perspectives, RoboWM-Bench (2604.19092), 2605.06388, X-WAM. Challenged by none. C1's foundation is safe even if the occupancy bet isn't.
2. *The specific occupancy-vs-latent frames-to-divergence head-to-head at tabletop scale is genuinely unrun.* High — Academic confirms (DSR-Net 2011.01968, 3D-Occ-MPC 2505.16249, ManipDreamer3D 2509.05314 all skip it), alphaxiv found no preempting paper. Challenged by Skeptic (unrun because uninteresting) and partially by 2605.06388 owning the adjacent meta-claim.
3. *The win may be structure (Least-Action transition), not the explicit grid.* High and decisive — Academic; the card itself names LaWM (2605.08279) as the toughest falsifier. If true, the occupancy framing is incidental. Unchallenged as a risk; this is the experiment that resolves the bet.
4. *Rigid-transform bounds transfer poorly to contact-rich/deformable/occluded manipulation (the H4 sub-cm cliff).* Medium-high — Skeptic + Practitioner, grounded in OccSim being meter-scale-driving and 2505.16249's self-occlusion problem. Challenged by the card's optimism that the bound "degrades gracefully." This is the go/no-go.
5. *The space is a fast, well-funded land-grab — scoop risk dominates novelty risk.* Medium — Economist, grounded in PointWorld (2601.03782), GWM (2508.17600), WEAVER (2606.13672) and a shared bench. Challenged by Historian's view that the durable hybrid result is harder to scoop than the headline.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Academic's LaWM concern and the Skeptic's "rigid-transform bounds fail on non-rigid manipulation" are the *same mechanism* viewed from two sides: occupancy's horizon advantage rests entirely on a *structure-preserving constraint* (rigid transforms) holding over the rollout. The Skeptic doubts the constraint holds on deformable/contact scenes; the Academic notes a latent (LaWM) can carry an equivalent structure-preserving constraint (Least-Action) without the grid. Together they imply the real research object is not "occupancy vs latent" but "which structure-preserving constraint best bounds geometric drift in *manipulation* dynamics, and in which substrate is it cheapest to enforce" — which is also exactly the blind spot (does the constraint apply to the moving object or only the static background).

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not yet COMMIT.** The premise is sound and the gap is real but narrow and contested. Concrete next experiment (and it must be *first*, not last): the three-way matched-task horizon comparison on RoboWM-Bench (2604.19092) long-horizon tasks — explicit occupancy (OccSim-Warp-DiT port) vs X-WAM-class latent (2604.26694) vs LaWM physics-structured latent (2605.08279) — measuring frames-to-geometric-divergence (dWorldEval 2604.22152 round-trip LPIPS as the vehicle), *with the static-background vs manipulated-object divergence reported separately* (resolving the blind spot). Two pre-registered kill criteria: (a) if LaWM matches occupancy on horizon, the contribution is structure-not-substrate → pivot to "structure-preserving transitions, substrate-agnostic"; (b) if the Warp-DiT bound collapses on the manipulated dynamic object below meter-scale (H4), → drop the occupancy framing and salvage H3 (the occupancy-latent handoff recipe) as the durable hybrid contribution. Only COMMIT if occupancy beats *both* latents on object-level horizon at sub-cm. Differentiate from the scoop threat (PointWorld/GWM/WEAVER) by owning the part they won't: real-robot sub-cm + planner-readable collision-checking (H2).

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Is long-horizon geometric stability a property of the *representation* (explicit grid vs latent) at all, or purely of the *transition operator's structure* (rigid-transform / Least-Action / energy constraint) regardless of where the state lives? If it's the operator, the entire explicit-vs-latent substrate debate — C1, C3, and half of Cluster C — collapses into "design the drift-bounding transition rule," and occupancy becomes one cheap-to-collision-check decoder of it rather than the thing that buys the horizon.

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Grounded in `Mechanism/Spatial-4D.md` lines 538-615 (C1) and KH spot-checks of the load-bearing IDs.*

**Verification done first.** All briefing-cited IDs resolve to KH notes (2605.06388, 2605.08279, 2603.28887, 2604.26694, 2604.19092, 2604.22152, 2601.03782, 2508.17600, 2606.13672). Three facts the briefing leans on do *not* survive the read clean:

- **2605.06388 is mis-scoped as the meta-claim owner.** Its KH title is "Reconstruction or Semantics? What Makes a Latent Space Useful for Robotic World Models" — a *latent-internals* study (reconstruction-aligned vs semantic-aligned latent), i.e. it owns the fidelity≠utility axis (finding #1), **not** the explicit-occupancy-vs-latent substrate axis (finding #2). The briefing's "already owns the adjacent meta-claim" is half-right and the wording over-threatens C1's actual whitespace: it preempts the *premise*, not the *head-to-head*.
- **WEAVER (2606.13672) is mis-bucketed.** Its KH note is a multi-view *pixel/video* manipulation WM ("better, faster, longer," beats Ctrl-World) — the opposite corner from C1's explicit occupancy. Listing it among labs "land-grabbing explicit-geometry manipulation WMs" inflates the scoop count by one. It also appears nowhere in `Spatial-4D.md`.
- **GWM (2508.17600) is 3DGS, not occupancy.** The doc's own A2 row (line 216) concedes GWM is "a rendering-Gaussian substrate, not a planner-collision-queryable occupancy grid." It is geometry-native but does not occupy C1's exact corner (external, collision-checkable). PointWorld (2601.03782) is the one genuine scoop threat to C1's *external-explicit* thesis — and even it is point-flow, not occupancy, and lives in C3 (line 716), not C1.

Net: the scoop-risk finding (#5) is real but the briefing over-counts by conflating "geometry-native manipulation WM" with "explicit-occupancy planner-readable WM." Only PointWorld is on-corner, and it is point-flow.

**1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1-10, reliability).**

1. *Fidelity ≠ long-horizon control utility; the axis is horizon/executability.* **9** — Supported convergently (RoboWM-Bench's discriminative-power finding, 2605.06388's OOD gain, X-WAM's per-frame/horizon split). This is the safe foundation and survives even if the occupancy bet dies. Docked one point only because "executability" is asserted as the right axis but never operationalized against a control-loop SR in the card itself (H5 proposes it, doesn't show it).
2. *The specific occupancy-vs-latent frames-to-divergence head-to-head at tabletop scale is unrun.* **7** — Likely true (DSR-Net, 3D-Occ-MPC, ManipDreamer3D all skip it per the card; no preempting paper found). Docked because the briefing's own cited preemptor (2605.06388) is mis-scoped, and the Skeptic's "unrun because uninteresting" is not retired — an unrun comparison is not automatically a *valuable* one.
3. *The win may be structure (Least-Action), not the explicit grid; LaWM is the falsifier.* **8** — Decisive and honestly surfaced; the card itself names LaWM as the toughest comparator (lines 567, 587-589) and writes the kill criterion. KH confirms LaWM is robotics+embodied, "Long-Horizon Physical Consistency," so the falsifier is correctly characterized. High confidence *that this is the bet's pivot*; the open empirical question is exactly why it isn't a 9.
4. *Rigid-transform bounds transfer poorly to contact/deformable/occluded; H4 sub-cm cliff is go/no-go.* **7** — Well-grounded: OccSim's KH title is literally "Multi-kilometer Simulation," so the bound is a driving result, and the card's H4 risk concedes the meter→sub-cm gap. Docked because "degrades gracefully vs collapses" is currently a coin-flip with no quantitative prior either way — the briefing correctly flags this as the go/no-go but cannot rate which way it falls.
5. *Fast, well-funded land-grab; scoop risk dominates novelty risk.* **5** — The single weakest link. Two of three named scoopers (WEAVER, GWM) are off-corner per my KH check; the "shared bench" is the vault's own internal scorecard (RoboWM-Bench / World-in-World), not a community land-grab venue. The Historian's rebuttal (the durable hybrid is hard to scoop) is the more defensible read. Risk is real but over-stated.

Mean = 7.2. Min = 5.

**2. WEAKEST LINK.** Finding #5 (land-grab / scoop dominance), score 5. To verify: a corner-matched competitor census — papers that (a) keep an *explicit, externally-decodable* geometric state (occupancy or point-flow), (b) at *manipulation/tabletop sub-cm* scale, (c) *closing a planner-readable rollout loop*. My read says only PointWorld (2601.03782) clears all three, and it is point-flow not occupancy. The verification is a one-table audit against those three predicates; if the count is 1-2 on-corner rather than "monthly," DE-RISK is justified on novelty grounds and the urgency framing softens. Secondary weak link: finding #2's "unrun" claim rests on a mis-scoped preemptor — re-confirm 2605.06388 does *not* run an explicit-substrate arm (KH says it does not: it is latent-only).

**3. BIAS CHECK.** The **Skeptic + Academic over-dominated**; the synthesis is two-thirds a debate between "the bound won't transfer" (Skeptic, finding #4) and "structure not substrate" (Academic, finding #3), with the hidden-connection (§3) explicitly fusing those two into the central research object. That is intellectually the right fusion, but it crowds out the Economist, whose one contribution (finding #5) is the least-verified and partly wrong. The Practitioner appears only as a co-signer on #4, never independently (e.g., no word on occupancy supervision scarcity in real manipulation data, which is the card's own third risk at line 614 and arguably the true blocker). So: **Skeptic/Academic-dominant, Practitioner under-weighted, Economist over-trusted relative to evidence.**

**4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE (6th angle).** A **systems/compute-cost perspective**. The entire bet is "explicit grid buys horizon," but a voxel-semantic occupancy grid at *sub-cm tabletop* resolution is a cubic memory/compute blow-up exactly in the regime H4 demands — and the card never costs it. If sub-cm occupancy is 10-100× the memory/latency of an X-WAM latent or LaWM's integrator, then even a *win* on frames-to-divergence may be Pareto-dominated once you put both on a fixed compute budget (the real deployment axis). This flips the conclusion: the honest comparison is not "occupancy vs latent on horizon" but "horizon-per-FLOP," and on that axis a structure-bounded latent (LaWM) may win without ever losing the head-to-head the briefing wants to run. This 6th angle also reframes H3 (hybrid handoff) from a hedge into the *likely* primary contribution.

**5. OVERALL GRADE: B+.**

A genuinely strong card: the first principle (substrate bounds horizon), the falsifier discipline (LaWM named as the toughest comparator with a pre-registered kill criterion), and the DE-RISK-not-COMMIT verdict are all correct and well above field median. The reason it is not an A: the briefing's empirical scaffolding around the *threat landscape* is loose — it over-threatens with a mis-scoped preemptor (2605.06388), inflates the scoop count with two off-corner papers (WEAVER, GWM), and never costs the compute blow-up that could dominate the bet. The science is honest; the competitive intelligence is not yet audited.

Top fixes, in priority order:
1. **Run finding #2's audit before citing #5 as urgent** — confirm 2605.06388 is latent-only (it is) and re-census on-corner scoopers (likely just PointWorld). Demote WEAVER/GWM from the scoop list.
2. **Add a horizon-per-FLOP axis to H1.** The matched-task comparison must fix a compute budget, else a frames-to-divergence "win" is uninterpretable for deployment. This is the missing 6th perspective and it is cheap to add to the pre-registration.
3. **Make H4 the literal first experiment, not H1.** If the Warp-DiT bound collapses below meter-scale on the *manipulated object* (not the static background — the §3 blind spot), the whole occupancy framing dies and the cheaper LaWM/H3 pivot is the real paper. Resolve the cliff before paying for the three-way horizon study.
