### C2 (Direction review)

Topic restatement: the bet that the discriminator surviving the whole video-for-action field is *cross-view pointmap consistency read by an off-the-shelf geometric tracker* (not single-view RGB-D, not a learned estimator, not RGB-only Gen6D), where jointly predicting RGB + cross-view-consistent pointmaps yields tracker-readable 6-DoF trajectories at ~0.64 avg task SR vs ~0.12 for RGB-plus-estimator baselines (~5x), and the gain tracks cross-view consistency (mIoU) more than RGB quality (FVD) — the H1 isolation no neighbor has run.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The cross-view-pointmap idea is real but the deployment math is the whole story. Predicting multi-view RGB *and* aligned pointmaps with an SVD-class diffusion model is heavy; the open question for me is not whether consistency helps offline but whether the predict-then-track loop runs at control rate and survives noisy predicted depth on a real arm.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own honest baselines already give the practitioner answer — RIGVid ([[2507.00990|RIGVid]]) gets 85% on xArm7 with RGB-only Kling video + FoundationPose, and explicitly names *monocular depth* as the bottleneck; PEWM ([[2508.20840|Primitive Embodied WM]]) hits 12 FPS / 16-of-20 real with RGB-only Gen6D and 75x faster inference. These are the deployed systems, and they work today without the cross-view machinery.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The anchor ([[2507.01099|Geometry-aware 4D Robot Video]]) reports 0.64 SR on **three simulation tasks** with NO reported real-robot trajectory and no latency number. Before committing, measure end-to-end wall-clock of one predict→pointmap→track→retarget cycle on real hardware — if it is slower than RIGVid's RGB+FoundationPose loop, the "geometry is tracker-readable" win is offline-only and the H2 predicted-vs-sensed-depth gap is the real product question.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The mechanistic claim is well-posed and the H1 ablation (consistency-loss vs RGB-FVD) is genuinely unrun by the named neighbors. But the literature already shows that *internalizing* geometric consistency into features can beat output-space prediction on the same SR target, so the externalization premise is contested, not assumed.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: GEM-4D ([[2605.22882|GEM-4D]]) distills 4D correspondence into a Video-DiT's *intermediate features* and lifts TesserAct from 58→75% (Droid-AUTOLab), 65→83% (CLVR), 59→87% (RAIL), with ablations showing the geometry-flow branch beats explicit-depth-supervision variants — i.e. feature-level consistency, read by a learned gated tracker, already delivers large action gains. MVISTA-4D ([[2602.09878|MVISTA-4D]]) reaches cross-view-consistent geometry (72.6% RLBench) but routes it to an end-to-end head. ConsisVLA-4D ([[2605.05126|ConsisVLA-4D]]) hits 98.1% LIBERO with geometry that *never leaves the model*.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The card's H1 has two legs (ablate cross-view loss; swap to single-view-depth+learned-estimator) but the literature's strongest counter is a *third* arm the card under-tests: GEM-4D's internalized-distillation-plus-gated-tracker. The contribution must be framed as "externalized output-space pointmaps beat internalized feature-distillation *on debuggability/reuse*" (this is H4), because on raw SR the internalized route is already winning the headline numbers.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is a framing artifact. Every component exists: predicting video+pointmaps (anchor, FantasyWorld 2509.21657), reading 6-DoF off generated geometry with an off-the-shelf tracker (RIGVid+FoundationPose, NovaPlan+TAPIP3D+Kabsch), cross-view consistency (MVISTA-4D). The only thing genuinely "untaken" is one specific ablation, which is a paper-sized contribution, not a substrate-sized one.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: NovaPlan ([[2602.20119|NovaPlan]]) already reads dense-flow+Kabsch 6-DoF object pose off generated video with a real tracker and an occlusion hand-flow fallback (70% four-layer stacking, 90% insertion) — the card concedes this is "monocular-depth single-view," but it is the cross-view claim's whole burden of proof. And the anchor's entire 5x headline rests on a single three-task sim eval; the Risks box itself flags "single-anchor direction" and "three-task SR is a narrow base."
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The card conveniently treats GEM-4D's *learned gated tracker* as a disqualifier ("not an off-the-shelf tracker"). But "off-the-shelf tracker" is a self-imposed purity constraint, not a performance axis — if a learned/gated tracker over imperfect rollouts wins (GEM-4D's AIDS exists precisely because rollouts are "still imperfect"), then insisting on off-the-shelf trackers is choosing a *weaker* readout for ideological cleanliness. The bet may be defending the wrong variable.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: This direction is cheap to *start* (sim, three tasks, off-the-shelf trackers) and that is exactly why it is crowded — low capital cost plus a clean falsifiable ablation is catnip for a fast publication, which is why 15+ near-neighbors landed in ~12 months. The moat is thin because the inputs are commodity (SVD/Wan backbones, FoundationPose, RoboWM-Bench).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The dependency stack is all free/borrowed: Pi3 ([[2507.13347|Pi3]]) supplies the pointmap machinery, RoboWM-Bench ([[2604.19092|RoboWM-Bench]]) supplies the discriminative SR substrate, FoundationPose supplies the tracker. Industrial labs are already here — FantasyWorld is Alibaba/AMAP, the anchor is Stanford+TRI, GEM-4D is MIT-affiliated. When TRI and Alibaba both ship in your lane, the academic edge has to be a *mechanism nobody else will bother to isolate*, not a system.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: The benchmark choice IS the bet. The 5x headline (0.64 vs 0.12) lives on the anchor's bespoke three-task eval; the moment you move to a saturating benchmark (LIBERO, where GVF-TAPE is 83% and ConsisVLA-4D 98.1%), the geometry margin compresses. Commit only if RoboWM-Bench's perceptual-vs-executability split stays *un-saturated* — if it saturates within a year (the LIBERO/RoboTwin pattern), the discriminative substrate the whole claim scales onto evaporates.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle replayed in the geometry register. Dreamer's lineage taught that latent/internal dynamics often beat explicit reconstruction on control; the explicit-geometry camp (TesserAct, occupancy WMs) is the modular pendulum swinging back, and C2 is its sharpest form: externalize the geometry so a classical tracker (FoundationPose, the SLAM-era artifact) can read it.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own axis table is a fossil record of the swing — internalized (ConsisVLA-4D 98.1%, X-WAM latent-4D, GEM-4D feature-distill) vs externalized (anchor, RIGVid, NovaPlan). Historically the modular/explicit side wins on *debuggability and transfer* and loses on *raw asymptotic SR* once the end-to-end side gets enough data — exactly the ConsisVLA-4D-vs-C2 tension H4 names.
- *Only-this-perspective tells the researcher*: Every prior modular-vs-end-to-end swing was ultimately decided not by peak SR but by which side composed better with the next capability (memory, long-horizon, multi-object). C2's durable value is that externalized pointmaps are *reusable* (a planner/tracker/memory reads them) — so frame and evaluate it as the composable substrate (the C4-memory and multi-tracker story), because as a pure SR play history says the latent camp eventually catches up.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Externalize vs internalize (Skeptic + Academic vs the card)*: The card asserts externalized output-space cross-view pointmaps read by an *off-the-shelf* tracker is the discriminator. GEM-4D ([[2605.22882|GEM-4D]]) shows internalized feature-distillation + a *learned gated* tracker already delivers 58→75 / 59→87% gains. Claim vs claim: "off-the-shelf tracker over consistent points" (C2) vs "learned tracker over imperfect rollouts is what you actually need because rollouts are never clean enough" (GEM-4D's AIDS rationale).
   - *Is the gain real or benchmark-bound (Economist vs anchor)*: 5x (0.64 vs 0.12) on three sim tasks vs 98.1% LIBERO for an internalized head ([[2605.05126|ConsisVLA-4D]]). The headline magnitude depends entirely on which benchmark; on saturating ones the geometry edge compresses.
   - *Deploy-today vs predict-geometry (Practitioner vs the bet)*: RIGVid (85%) and PEWM (12 FPS, 16/20 real) ship RGB-only *now*; the card's H2 itself admits predicted pointmaps may lose a "large margin" to sensed depth, which would gate C2 to depth-sensor-less settings only.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — it lands the one survivable framing (the contribution is externalized-vs-internalized *on debuggability/reuse*, H4, not on raw SR) and is grounded in the exact rival numbers (GEM-4D, MVISTA-4D, ConsisVLA-4D).
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — directionally right about the pendulum but offers no number that changes the commit decision; its insight (compose, don't peak-chase) is a reframing, not a test.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
   On a *single matched task set*, does 6-DoF pose read by an **off-the-shelf** tracker from **output-space cross-view pointmaps** beat 6-DoF read by a **learned gated tracker** from **internalized/feature-level** geometry (the GEM-4D arm) — at equal RGB fidelity? If off-the-shelf-over-external loses, the bet's purity constraint is choosing the weaker readout and the whitespace collapses into GEM-4D's lane.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
   Geometric consistency (cross-view or 4D-correspondence), in *some* form, drives downstream action success more than RGB visual fidelity does — the perceptual-functionality gap is real. Every camp (anchor's mIoU>FVD, GEM-4D's geometry-flow ablation, RoboWM-Bench's executability split, WorldArena's gap) corroborates it. The contested part is only *where* the consistency should live (output pointmaps vs features) and *who* reads it (off-the-shelf vs learned tracker).

5. **The blind spot (addressed by no perspective).**
   Dynamic, non-rigid, and contact-rich pose: a "6-DoF pose is just the rigid transform between matching 3D points" premise assumes rigid objects. None of the perspectives interrogate deformables, articulated objects, in-hand occlusion-during-contact, or multi-object scenes where rigid-Kabsch readout breaks — yet RoboWM-Bench explicitly spans deformable/articulated, and NovaPlan needed a hand-flow fallback precisely because rigid object-pose readout fails under contact. The whole bet is framed on the rigid case it is most defensible on.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

(1) **CEO summary.** C2 bets that the field's video-for-action methods are bottlenecked by reading 6-DoF pose post-hoc from RGB, and that jointly predicting cross-view-consistent pointmaps lets an off-the-shelf geometric tracker read pose straight off — with consistency, not RGB fidelity, carrying the action gain (the unrun H1 ablation). The mechanism is real and the H1 isolation is genuinely untaken by the named neighbors, but the direction is not whitespace as a *substrate*: it is whitespace as a *clean ablation*. The strongest rival, GEM-4D, already shows that *internalized* feature-level geometry plus a *learned* tracker delivers the same class of SR gains, and deployed RGB-only systems (RIGVid 85%, PEWM 12 FPS) work today — so the survivable contribution is not "externalized geometry wins SR" but "externalized output-space pointmaps win on *debuggability, reuse, and composability* (H4), and consistency provably outdrives fidelity (H1)," validated as a mechanism on a deliberately narrow base, not overclaimed as a new substrate.

(2) **Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
   1. *Consistency > RGB fidelity for action* is the load-bearing, well-corroborated claim. SUPPORTED by Practitioner, Academic, Economist (perceptual-functionality gap), grounded in anchor mIoU>FVD, GEM-4D geometry-flow ablation, RoboWM-Bench. CHALLENGED by none directly — but its *form* (output vs feature) is contested.
   2. *The H1 ablation (cross-view-loss vs RGB-FVD vs single-view-depth+learned-estimator) is genuinely unrun by named neighbors* — a real, paper-sized novel contribution. SUPPORTED by Academic, Skeptic (concedes this one slice). CHALLENGED by Skeptic (it is paper-sized, not substrate-sized).
   3. *The "off-the-shelf tracker" purity constraint may be defending the wrong variable.* SUPPORTED by Skeptic, Academic. CHALLENGED by Historian (off-the-shelf = composable/debuggable, which is the long-run value).
   4. *The 5x headline is benchmark-fragile* (three sim tasks; compresses on LIBERO-class saturating benchmarks). SUPPORTED by Economist, Practitioner, the card's own Risks box. CHALLENGED by Academic (RoboWM-Bench is designed to stay discriminative).
   5. *Externalized geometry's durable edge is reuse/debuggability/composability, not peak SR.* SUPPORTED by Historian, Academic (H4). CHALLENGED by Economist (reuse is hard to monetize as a single-paper headline).

(3) **Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's "predicted pointmaps may be too noisy" (H2) and the Skeptic's "GEM-4D needs a *learned gated* tracker because rollouts are imperfect" are the *same finding* seen from two ends: predicted geometry is never clean enough for a naive off-the-shelf tracker, which is *why* GEM-4D built AIDS and NovaPlan built a hand-flow fallback. The bet's purity (off-the-shelf tracker on consistent points) and its biggest deployment risk (H2 noise) are the same coin — the cleaner the bet stays, the more fragile it is to real prediction noise.

(4) **Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not commit-as-substrate.** The mechanism (H1) is worth a paper but the "substrate" framing dies to GEM-4D. Concretely: (a) run the *decisive* experiment first — on ONE matched task set, three readouts at equal RGB fidelity: off-the-shelf tracker over output cross-view pointmaps (C2) vs learned gated tracker over internalized features (GEM-4D arm) vs single-view-depth+learned-estimator (GVF-TAPE arm); the C2 arm must win on a *debuggability/reuse* split (multi-tracker agreement, failure-localization) even if it ties on raw SR. (b) Re-position the thesis from "externalized geometry is the substrate" to "consistency provably outdrives fidelity (H1), and output-space pointmaps buy debuggability/reuse the internalized route cannot (H4)." (c) Pre-register the H2 predicted-vs-sensed-depth gap; if large, scope the claim to no-depth-sensor / novel-viewpoint settings up front. (d) Stress the rigid-pose blind spot by including at least one articulated/deformable task from RoboWM-Bench. DROP only if the decisive experiment shows the off-the-shelf-over-external arm loses on *both* SR and debuggability.

(5) **Frontier question that would change everything.** Is "read pose off geometry" the right abstraction at all, or is the field converging on *learned* readouts over *imperfect* predicted geometry (GEM-4D's AIDS, NovaPlan's gated fallback) — i.e., will the winning system be the one that *gates and corrects* noisy rollouts rather than the one that predicts geometry clean enough for a classical tracker? If gated-learned-readout-over-imperfect-geometry is the attractor, C2's entire off-the-shelf-tracker premise is a transitional artifact, and the durable bet is the *gating/confidence* mechanism, not the cross-view-consistency loss.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. Verdict up front: the briefing is unusually honest about its own weakness (it pre-concedes "whitespace as a clean ablation, not as a substrate"), and that self-demotion is the right call. But the honesty is doing work that masks two soft spots: the GEM-4D "same class of SR gains" claim is a cross-benchmark inference dressed as a measured fact, and the decisive-experiment prescription quietly assumes a debuggability metric exists and is winnable. Grade and fixes below.

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

**Finding 1 — Consistency > RGB fidelity for action is the load-bearing, well-corroborated claim. Score: 7/10.**
Well-supported in *direction* by three independent legs: the anchor's mIoU-improves-with-SR story, RoboWM-Bench's executable-vs-plausible split, and WorldArena's perception-functionality gap. But the briefing overstates corroboration: 2507.01099's KH note shows mIoU *and* FVD both improve together (line 40) — the paper never isolates consistency from fidelity. So the evidence is "consistency correlates with SR," not "consistency outdrives fidelity." That decoupling is exactly H1, which is *unrun*. The claim is plausible and well-motivated, not corroborated in the strong form the briefing's verb "outdrives" implies. Docked for the gap between correlation (have) and isolation (want).

**Finding 2 — The H1 ablation is genuinely unrun by named neighbors; a real paper-sized contribution. Score: 8/10.**
I verified this against the two most-load-bearing notes. Neither 2507.01099 nor GEM-4D ablates cross-view-loss vs RGB-FVD vs single-view-depth+learned-estimator as a three-way isolation. This is the strongest finding in the briefing: a concrete, falsifiable, unclaimed experiment. Docked one point only because "unrun by *named* neighbors" is not "unrun by the field" — the briefing did not verify the negative beyond its own citation set, and a three-way readout ablation is the kind of thing a contemporaneous arxiv preprint could already hold.

**Finding 3 — The "off-the-shelf tracker" purity constraint may be defending the wrong variable. Score: 7/10.**
This is the sharpest critical insight and it is well-grounded: GEM-4D's KH note explicitly says the AIDS tracker exists precisely because rollouts are "geometrically consistent, yet still imperfect" (line 52), and NovaPlan built a hand-flow fallback for the same reason. Two independent systems converged on *gated/corrected* readout, which is real evidence the off-the-shelf premise is fragile. Not a 9 because it is a forward-looking bet about where the field converges, not a settled result — the off-the-shelf route could still win on the consistency axis if the pointmaps are clean enough, which is empirically open.

**Finding 4 — The 5x headline is benchmark-fragile. Score: 9/10.**
Strongly supported and the card's own Risks box concedes it (three-task base, single anchor). The compression argument is concrete: GVF-TAPE 83% and ConsisVLA-4D 98.1% on LIBERO-class benchmarks leave no room for a 5x multiplier. The 0.64-vs-0.12 gap is real but lives on a deliberately hard three-task base where baselines floor out. The Academic's RoboWM-Bench rebuttal is fair but does not rescue the *headline number* — it only promises a discriminative substrate to re-measure on. Highest-confidence finding.

**Finding 5 — Externalized geometry's durable edge is reuse/debuggability/composability, not peak SR. Score: 5/10.**
This is the weakest link (see below). It is the load-bearing premise of the re-positioning advice (H4), yet it is the *least* evidenced claim in the briefing. No cited paper measures debuggability, multi-tracker agreement, or failure-localization. The H4 falsifier ("implicit head matches externalization everywhere") has never been tested by anyone, and the briefing's own decisive experiment requires inventing the metric. Worse, the Economist's challenge (reuse does not monetize as a single-paper headline) is not answered, only noted. This is a hopeful thesis, not a finding.

Mean: (7+8+7+9+5)/5 = **7.2**. Min: **5**.

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 5 (durable edge = debuggability/composability, score 5).** The entire re-positioning advice ("retreat from substrate to H1+H4") rests on H4 being winnable, but H4 is the one hypothesis with *zero* supporting measurement in the card. The briefing asks the author to run a decisive experiment where "the C2 arm must win on a debuggability/reuse split (multi-tracker agreement, failure-localization) even if it ties on raw SR" — but that split is not a standard metric, no benchmark in the table reports it, and there is no prior result showing output-space pointmaps are *measurably* more debuggable than GEM-4D's intermediate features (which a probe could also read out). 

To verify, I would need: (a) an operationalized debuggability metric with a baseline number — e.g. cross-tracker pose-agreement variance on identical rollouts, output-pointmaps vs a linear probe on GEM-4D features; (b) evidence that externalization is not trivially recoverable from internalized features by a cheap decoder (if it is, H4 collapses to "buy nothing," GEM-4D's own falsifier wins). Until (a) and (b) exist, "durable edge = debuggability" is a faith statement, and the whole "survivable contribution" pivots on faith.

---

##### 3. Bias check — dominant perspective

**The Skeptic over-dominated, with the Academic as enabler.** The synthesis is organized around the Skeptic's verdict ("paper-sized not substrate-sized," "GEM-4D kills the substrate framing") — that frame drives the CEO summary, the actionable insight, and the frontier question. Findings 2, 3, and the entire de-risk prescription are Skeptic-led. The Academic supplies the rescue rails (RoboWM-Bench, H4) that keep the direction alive after the Skeptic guts it, so the two together set the agenda.

Under-weighted: the **Practitioner** appears mainly as the H2-noise concern (folded into the Skeptic's GEM-4D point via the "two ends of one coin" move, which is elegant but effectively *absorbs* the Practitioner into the Skeptic rather than letting it speak independently). The **Economist** is cited but its strongest punch — reuse does not monetize — is logged and then ignored in the final recommendation, which still leans on reuse/composability as the survivable edge. The **Historian** is the only voice defending the off-the-shelf premise (composable/debuggable = long-run value) and is outvoted 4-to-1, which is exactly why Finding 3 reads as nearly settled when it is genuinely open.

Net: not balanced. The Skeptic+Academic axis manufactured a clean "demote to mechanism, survive on H4" narrative that is internally tidy but rests on the least-evidenced finding (5).

---

##### 4. Missing perspective — a 6th angle that would change conclusions

**The Systems/Latency engineer (deployment-cost realist).** Every survivability argument here is about *epistemics* (which variable carries the gain) and none about *throughput*. The competing deployed systems quote real numbers: PEWM 12 FPS, RIGVid runs FoundationPose in a closed loop, X-WAM hits 15 Hz via async denoising. C2's pipeline is "predict cross-view-consistent multi-view pointmaps with an SVD-class diffusion model, then run an off-the-shelf geometric tracker" — that is plausibly the *slowest* readout in the table, because multi-view pointmap diffusion plus external tracking is two heavy stages in series. 

A latency engineer would ask the question the briefing never asks: does the off-the-shelf-tracker purity that buys debuggability also buy a control-rate penalty that disqualifies C2 from the closed-loop regime where GEM-4D (single forward pass + gated tracker) and PEWM (12 FPS) already operate? If externalized output-space pointmaps cost 5-10x the inference of internalized features, then H4's "debuggability edge" is paid for in deployability, and the durable bet inverts: internalized-and-fast wins deployment, externalized-and-slow wins only offline analysis. This angle would push the recommendation from "re-position to H1+H4" toward "first prove C2 can hit a real control rate at all" — a go/no-go the current de-risk plan omits entirely.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest briefing — it correctly identifies the real contribution (H1 isolation), correctly demotes the overclaim (substrate → mechanism), and the "two ends of one coin" H2/GEM-4D synthesis is genuinely insightful. It loses the A because the survivable-contribution thesis it lands on (H4 debuggability) is the single least-evidenced claim in the whole document, and it never stress-tests its own deployment cost. A Stanford committee would accept the H1 paper and reject the H4 framing as currently unargued.

Top fixes, in priority order:
1. **Stop asserting GEM-4D delivers "the same class of SR gains."** Verified: GEM-4D's 58→75 / 65→83 / 59→87 are deltas over *TesserAct* on *Droid-AUTOLab / CLVR / RAIL* — never measured head-to-head against the C2 anchor's 0.64 on its three tasks. "Same class" is a cross-benchmark inference. Either run the matched comparison (the decisive experiment already prescribes it — good) or downgrade the prose to "GEM-4D demonstrates internalized geometry *can* lift SR on its own benchmarks," not "delivers the same gains."
2. **Operationalize H4 or drop it from the load-bearing position.** Define the debuggability metric (cross-tracker agreement variance, failure-localization recall) and add the kill-test: can a cheap decoder recover the same external pointmaps from GEM-4D's intermediate features? If yes, H4 buys nothing and the survivable contribution shrinks to H1 alone — which the briefing should be willing to accept.
3. **Add the latency go/no-go before the H1 ablation.** Measure C2's end-to-end control rate (multi-view pointmap diffusion + off-the-shelf tracker) against the 12 FPS / 15 Hz incumbents. If C2 cannot reach a deployable rate, the off-the-shelf purity is an offline-only virtue and the framing must say so up front.
4. **Let the Economist and Historian finish their sentences.** The recommendation leans on reuse/composability while the Economist's "reuse does not monetize" sits unanswered. Resolve it: either name the venue/audience where debuggability *is* the headline, or concede that H1 (not H4) is the publishable core.

---

### C3 (Direction review)

Topic: Natively-4D geometry (RGB + depth + 3D over time) as a deployable world-representation substrate. The bet is the two pillars no paper has charted: (i) generalizing X-WAM's Asynchronous Noise Sampling, the action schedule step-distills to **1-4 steps** without degrading the read-out geometry, and (ii) a native-4D-*RGB-D-video* substrate transfers WM→policy *with its appearance channel intact* as well as PointWorld's point-flow does.

Anchors read: [[2604.26694|X-WAM]], [[2601.03782|PointWorld]], [[2606.10040|Efficient-WAM]], [[2606.17046|GAM]], [[2603.12639|RoboStereo]]. alphaxiv `discover_papers` worked (2 queries) and surfaced two un-carded threats: [[2606.05254|Flash-WAM]] and [[2603.16195|S-VAM]]; `get_paper_content` confirmed both.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
Core position: the deployable win here is not "native 4D" as an idea but the latency-fidelity *frontier* at 1-4 action steps; everything below ~500 ms/chunk is the only thing that changes whether a WAM is a planner or a controller. Academics are pricing X-WAM's 15 Hz as the achievement when the field already crossed the real-time line on *pixel* substrates and at much lower latency.
Strongest evidence: GAM ([[2606.17046]]) runs **~6.9 ms/pass (~145 Hz)** single-pass on a GFM backbone, an order of magnitude past X-WAM's 1033 ms / 15 Hz; Efficient-WAM ([[2606.10040]]) hits **98 ms/action-chunk (32x over Motus 3215 ms)** and explicitly states "photorealistic future *not* necessary, geometric/motion/contact cues suffice." Flash-WAM ([[2606.05254]]) already distills a joint video-action WAM to **1v/1a (348 ms, 23x)**.
The one thing only this perspective says: the 4D *RGB-D appearance channel* the bet wants to keep "intact" is a deployment *liability*, not an asset, because every paper that wins on latency (GAM, Efficient-WAM, S-VAM) does so by *throwing away* photoreal pixels and keeping only geometry/motion cues. Carrying RGB-D at deploy is paying tokens for a channel the controller does not read.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
Core position: the card's own honesty has already eaten most of the novelty: it concedes X-WAM owns native-vs-recovered (Chamfer **0.0049 vs 0.0401**, SR **63.0→67.8**) and PointWorld owns the broad transfer claim. What survives is two narrow falsifiable extensions (H2 step-distillation holding *geometry*; H5 RGB-D-vs-point-flow transfer), and the literature is closing on both faster than the card.
Strongest evidence: Flash-WAM gives a *formal* account (Proposition 1: any consistency fn with b'(0)=0 vanishes as O(σ²) in the low-noise regime the action stream lives in) of why naive few-step async distillation collapses, and fixes it with modality-aware consistency functions, recovering **95.7% LIBERO at 1v/2a**. That is H2's mechanism, proven. S-VAM ([[2603.16195]]) self-distills *geometric* (DPAv3) + semantic foresight into a **single forward pass** at 25 Hz.
The one thing only this perspective says: H2 as written ("without degrading the **read-out geometry**") is *not yet measured* by anyone — Flash-WAM reports task SR + qualitative video, S-VAM reports SR, neither plots Chamfer/4D-fidelity vs step count. The genuinely open quantity is the geometry-vs-steps curve on [[2603.03485|Phys4D]], not the speedup.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it is wrong or already solved).**
Core position: both pillars are load-bearing on a distinction-without-a-difference. Pillar (i) is the WAM-distillation arms race (Flash-WAM, S-VAM, Efficient-WAM, GigaWorld-Policy) and the only thing un-run is a *measurement* (geometry-vs-steps), not a method. Pillar (ii) asks whether RGB-D-video transfers "as well as" point-flow — but the card's own table shows the field is converging on the answer that the *appearance channel is the part you drop* (GaussianDream 98.4% LIBERO drops geometry at deploy; WAM4D 91.8% drops registers; GeoSem-WAM 98.55% drops aux).
Strongest evidence the card under-weights: the train-time-auxiliary cluster (Pri4R **+13.2% RoboCasa** head dropped, GEM-4D **59→87% RAIL** geometry discarded at inference, GaussianDream, GeoPredict **+10.1pp RoboCasa**) is a coherent counter-thesis — "keep geometry as a train-time signal, run unchanged at deploy" — that lands *on the card's home benchmark* and beats some online-4D numbers. If geometry-as-supervision suffices, the entire "keep 4D online" premise is unnecessary.
The one thing only this perspective says: H5's falsifier ("RGB-D transfers no better than point-flow → adds nothing") is a coin-flip the card is structurally likely to lose, because point-flow and particle substrates (PointWorld, [[2511.01177|Scaling Cross-Embodiment WMs]], μ0) already demonstrate cross-embodiment transfer and RGB-D's extra appearance channel has no shown transfer benefit.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
Core position: this is an industry-lab land-grab on a saturating benchmark, and the academic bet is downstream of decisions made by Tsinghua/Xiaomi (X-WAM), Fei-Fei's lab + NVIDIA (PointWorld), Huawei (S-VAM), and EmbodyX (Flash-WAM). The "real-time 4D" narrative sells because it justifies the video-foundation-model compute these labs already own.
Strongest evidence: every real-time result is a well-resourced lab repurposing a *pretrained* large backbone (X-WAM's DiT, PointWorld's 1B PointTransformerV3, GAM's 1.4B GFM, Efficient-WAM's 1B MoT distilled from a foundation model). RoboCasa (79.2% X-WAM, +12.1pp over Cosmos) and LIBERO (97-98% across GAM/GeoSem-WAM/GaussianDream/Flash-WAM) are *already saturated* — the marginal pp is benchmark-chasing.
The one thing only this perspective says: an academic with academic compute cannot win the latency race (1v/1a distillation, 1B+ backbones, GFM repurposing are capex-gated), so the *only* defensible academic contribution is the **measurement protocol** (geometry-vs-steps on Phys4D, RGB-D-vs-point-flow transfer on a fixed head) — a referee tool, not a system. Committing to build a faster system is committing to lose to a lab.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen the prior waves).**
Core position: this is the pixel-vs-latent world-model cycle replaying at 4D resolution. Dreamer→DreamerV3 showed latent imagination beats pixel reconstruction for control; the field is now re-litigating whether *geometry* must be explicit-online or can be a latent/train-time prior — and history says the train-time-prior camp usually wins on deployment.
Strongest evidence: the card's own table is the cycle in miniature — explicit-online (X-WAM, PointWorld, RoboStereo, GAM) vs latent (VLA-JEPA 97.2%, DINO-WM, DexWorldModel 94%) vs train-time-dropped (GaussianDream, WAM4D, Pri4R, GEM-4D, GeoSem-WAM). Just as DR/sim2real eventually folded explicit physics into learned priors, "drop the expensive channel at deploy, keep its gradient" (Fast-WAM, GigaWorld-Policy 9x) is the historically favored endpoint.
The one thing only this perspective says: the bet that "appearance-carrying native-4D transfers better" echoes the pixel-reconstruction camp's losing argument from 2019-2021 (RGB reconstruction helps representation) — which lost to latent/contrastive once control was the metric. Carrying RGB-D for transfer is on the wrong side of a cycle that has already run once.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Practitioner vs the bet's H5*: Practitioner says the RGB-D appearance channel is a deploy liability dropped by every latency winner; H5 says it is the *asset* that should transfer better than point-flow. Specific clash: GAM/Efficient-WAM/S-VAM keep geometry-only cues and win latency, while H5 wants the color channel kept "intact."
   - *Academic vs Skeptic*: Academic says H2-as-geometry-measurement is genuinely open (no one plotted Chamfer-vs-steps); Skeptic says it is a measurement not a method, so it is a workshop-paper, not a publication bet. Both agree Flash-WAM ran the *mechanism*.
   - *Historian/Skeptic vs the whole premise*: train-time-auxiliary cluster (Pri4R, GEM-4D, GeoPredict on RoboCasa) says "keep 4D online" is unnecessary; the card concedes this only as "H1's falsifier side," but it threatens the *entire C3 premise*, not just H1.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.** Strongest: **Academic** — it is the most evidence-bound, correctly isolates the one un-run quantity (geometry-vs-steps), and is corroborated by Flash-WAM's own omission. Weakest: **Practitioner's** claim that RGB-D is *always* a liability overshoots — RoboStereo's dual-tower RGB+pointmap wins **16 WorldArena physics/3D metrics** and 30→65% real, evidence the appearance channel can help; the Practitioner under-weights it.

3. **The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.** Does read-out 4D geometric fidelity (Phys4D 4D-Chamfer + Worldline-L2) degrade *monotonically and significantly* as the action schedule drops from 5→1 steps, holding the backbone fixed? If geometry survives 1-2 steps, H2 is real and Flash-WAM merely didn't measure it; if geometry collapses (as task SR partially does in Flash-WAM 1v/1a real: 66.7→50%), the speedup trades away the substrate and the bet dies.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true).** Real-time 4D-aware control is *solved as a capability* (X-WAM 15 Hz, GAM 145 Hz, PointWorld 0.12s, Flash-WAM 348ms) — the open question is no longer "is online 4D possible" but "is keeping the full RGB-D channel online worth its cost versus dropping geometry to a train-time prior or distilling to one step." Everyone agrees the headline LIBERO/RoboCasa numbers are saturated and the contribution must move to *fidelity-under-latency* or *transfer*, not SR.

5. **The blind spot none addressed.** **Closed-loop error accumulation and the sim-to-real fidelity of the depth/geometry supervision itself.** Every metric cited is open-loop fidelity (Chamfer, AbsRel, PSNR) or single-rollout SR; none of the perspectives or the card interrogates whether off-the-shelf depth estimators (the card's own mitigation for "no depth GT in robot data") inject geometric error that *compounds* over minute-scale closed-loop horizons — the exact regime C4 (memory) exists for. The substrate's geometry is only as good as the depth pseudo-labels, and no cited result measures degradation of *that* under real-robot deployment.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**CEO summary.** C3's broad thesis — native online 4D beats lift-after-pixel — has been *substantially pre-empted*: the card honestly concedes X-WAM owns the native-vs-recovered Chamfer ablation and PointWorld owns cross-embodiment transfer, and since the card was written, Flash-WAM (2606.05254) has executed the *mechanism* of pillar (i) (modality-aware step-distillation of a joint video-action WAM to 1-2 action steps, with a formal proof of why it works) and S-VAM (2603.16195) has distilled geometric foresight into a single pass. What genuinely survives is narrow but real: nobody has *measured* whether read-out 4D **geometric fidelity** (not task SR) holds as the schedule drops to 1-4 steps, and nobody has run the head-to-head of an RGB-D-video substrate vs a point-flow substrate transferring into the same policy head. Those are a measurement protocol and a controlled comparison, not a new system — defensible as a rigorous empirical paper, fragile as a "we built the first X" bet, especially against industry labs who own the compute to win any latency race.

**Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Real-time 4D-aware control is already solved as a capability.* (Supported: Practitioner, Academic, Economist, Historian — GAM 145 Hz, X-WAM 15 Hz, PointWorld 0.12s, Flash-WAM 348ms. Challenged by: none.) Highest reliability.
2. *Pillar (i)'s mechanism is pre-empted by Flash-WAM; only its geometry-vs-steps measurement is open.* (Supported: Academic, Skeptic, Economist. Challenged by: a charitable reading that native-4D substrate changes the distillation behavior — untested.) High reliability.
3. *The headline benchmarks (LIBERO/RoboCasa) are saturated, forcing the contribution onto fidelity-under-latency or transfer.* (Supported: Economist, Academic, Historian. Challenged by: none.) High.
4. *The appearance/RGB-D channel is more likely a deploy liability than a transfer asset (H5 is structurally disfavored).* (Supported: Practitioner, Skeptic, Historian; RoboStereo is the counter-evidence. Challenged by: the bet itself, partially by RoboStereo.) Medium.
5. *Geometry-as-train-time-signal may make "keep 4D online" unnecessary, threatening the whole premise not just H1.* (Supported: Skeptic, Historian; Pri4R/GEM-4D/GeoPredict on RoboCasa. Challenged by: ContactWorld 2606.13877, which finds explicit point-cloud 32.1% > image 20.7% planning SR — explicit structure *does* help on contact-rich.) Medium, genuinely contested.

**Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Economist's "academics can't win the capex-gated latency race" and the Academic's "the only un-run thing is a measurement" converge on the *same* recommendation from opposite premises: the defensible C3 contribution is a **referee/measurement artifact** (a standardized geometry-vs-latency-vs-transfer protocol on Phys4D + WorldArena), not a system. The Historian supplies the reason it would be cited: every wave of this cycle was *settled by a benchmark* (Atari for Dreamer, sim2real suites for DR), not by the first system.

**Actionable insight for the researcher (DE-RISK, leaning toward re-scope).** Do NOT commit to "build the first 1-4-step native-4D RGB-D controller" — Flash-WAM and GAM have effectively taken the system framing. DO commit to the *measurement* that all five perspectives agree is un-run. Concrete next experiment: take a single fixed native-4D backbone (X-WAM-class, or reproduce via depth-branch-in-DiT), sweep action denoising steps 5→4→3→2→1 using Flash-WAM's modality-aware consistency functions as the distillation method (cite, don't reinvent), and plot **Phys4D 4D-Chamfer + Worldline-L2 vs steps** — the geometry-vs-steps curve nobody has. In the *same* harness, swap the substrate (RGB-D-video vs PointWorld-style point-flow) into one frozen action head and report WorldArena action-planner + RoboWM-Bench closed-loop SR. If geometry survives ≤2 steps AND RGB-D beats point-flow transfer, you have a real result; if not, you have a clean negative that reframes the field. Either is publishable as an *evaluation* paper; neither requires winning a compute race. Go/no-go gate per the card's own risk callout: H2 latency-fidelity frontier + H5 RGB-D transfer.

**Frontier question that would change everything.** Is there a single denoising step at which a native-4D substrate's *read-out geometry* (not its task success) crosses below the fidelity a downstream contact-mode/insertion policy needs — i.e., is there a hard floor on steps imposed by *geometry* that is independent of (and higher than) the floor imposed by action accuracy? If yes, geometry-bound tasks have a fundamentally different latency budget than appearance-bound ones, and that floor — not any system — is the citable law C3 should be hunting.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. The briefing is unusually honest about its own pre-emption, which is good. But honesty about being scooped is not the same as having a defensible residual, and the briefing's "what survives" claim leans on a measurement-vs-mechanism distinction that needs harder scrutiny.*

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

1. **Real-time 4D-aware control is already solved as a capability — 9/10.** Four independent data points (GAM ~145 Hz, X-WAM 15 Hz, PointWorld 0.12 s, Flash-WAM 348 ms), all verified in-KH, all converging. The one caveat is definitional: "real-time *4D-aware*" elides that Flash-WAM (2606.05254) is a *pixel/video* substrate, not native-4D, and reports task SR not read-out geometry. The capability of "fast WAM control" is solved; "fast *native-4D-fidelity* control" is the part still being argued. Docking one point for that conflation, but the headline is robust.

2. **Pillar (i)'s mechanism is pre-empted by Flash-WAM; only its geometry-vs-steps measurement is open — 7/10.** I verified Flash-WAM: it does exactly what the briefing says (modality-aware consistency functions, linear gradient scaling b(σ)=-σ for the action stream, formal b'(0)≠0 proof, 23x/348 ms). So the *distillation mechanism* claim is correct. But two corrections pull this down. First, the card *already* contained Efficient-WAM (2606.10040, line 741), which the card itself calls "the dedicated generalization of X-WAM's Asynchronous Noise Sampling H2 calls for" — so the briefing presents Flash-WAM as fresh pre-emption when the card had already conceded the step-distillation mechanism was generalized; Flash-WAM is a *second* instance, not a new blow. Second, and more importantly, both Flash-WAM and Efficient-WAM distill on a *pixel/video* substrate. H2 as written (line 754-758) asks whether geometry survives on a *native-4D* substrate — a question neither Flash-WAM nor Efficient-WAM touches, because neither carries a native-4D read-out to degrade. So "only the measurement is open" is slightly too pessimistic: the *mechanism transplanted onto a native-4D substrate* is itself untested, and that is a hair more than pure measurement. The briefing's own "charitable reading" hedge gestures at this but underweights it.

3. **Headline benchmarks saturated, forcing contribution onto fidelity-under-latency or transfer — 8/10.** The saturation is real and citation-backed in-card (GeoSem-WAM 98.55% LIBERO, GaussianDream 98.4%, VLA-JEPA 97.2%, GAM 97.6%). The logical move — "therefore the contribution must be fidelity-under-latency or transfer" — is sound *given* the card's framing. The half-point I withhold: RoboCasa (79.2%) is *not* saturated, and the card's own key target leans on it; "saturated" is true for LIBERO, weaker for the contact-rich suites the bet actually cares about. So the premise is right but the word "saturated" over-generalizes from LIBERO.

4. **RGB-D appearance channel is more likely a deploy liability than a transfer asset; H5 structurally disfavored — 5/10.** This is the briefing's most speculative finding and it is honestly flagged Medium. The structural argument (appearance is the sim-to-real gap; point-flow/geometry abstracts it away) is a reasonable prior, but it is a *prior*, not evidence. The single piece of direct counter-evidence — RoboStereo's 30→65% real + 16 WorldArena metrics on an RGB-video + 3D-pointmap dual tower — is a real existence proof that the appearance channel does not kill transfer. iMaC (2606.09813) even reports "internal RGB-D beats external depth for contact consistency," a second data point the briefing omits that cuts against the liability thesis. The finding is plausible but the evidence base is one prior plus one contrary result; calling RGB-D "structurally disfavored" is stronger than the evidence licenses.

5. **Geometry-as-train-time-signal may make "keep 4D online" unnecessary, threatening the whole premise — 6/10.** Genuinely the most interesting finding and correctly tagged "genuinely contested." The train-time-drop camp is deep and cited (Pri4R +13.2%, GEM-4D 59→87% RAIL, GeoPredict +10.1 pp, GigaWorld-Policy, GeoSem-WAM, GaussianDream, WAM4D, Fast-WAM). The counter — ContactWorld (2606.13877), verified: point-cloud 32.1% vs image 20.7% planning SR, +tactile 36.1% — is the one paper that adjudicates *contact-rich* and finds explicit-online structure wins. The honest reading is that the train-time-drop results are mostly on *appearance-bound or general* benchmarks while the online-geometry win is on *contact-rich planning* — which is precisely the task split the card's own Risk callout demands. So this finding does not "threaten the whole premise" uniformly; it threatens it on appearance-bound tasks and leaves it standing on contact-bound ones. Tagging it as a blanket premise-threat slightly overstates; the truth is task-conditional. Medium is fair.

##### 2. Weakest link

Finding 4 (RGB-D = deploy liability, H5 disfavored). The claim rests on a sim-to-real prior, not measured transfer, and the only direct evidence is *against* it (RoboStereo, plus the uncited iMaC contact result). **What would verify it:** the head-to-head the briefing itself proposes — freeze one policy head, transfer an RGB-D-video substrate vs a point-flow substrate (PointWorld-class) into it, and report closed-loop real SR on the *same* tasks. Until that controlled swap exists, "RGB-D is structurally disfavored" is an untested hypothesis dressed as a finding. The briefing is internally inconsistent here: Finding 4 says RGB-D probably loses, yet the actionable insight bets the paper on "if RGB-D beats point-flow transfer, you have a real result." You cannot both pre-judge H5 as disfavored *and* stake the contribution on it winning.

##### 3. Bias check

The **Economist over-dominated.** The single most consequential move in the briefing — "academics can't win a capex-gated latency race against Tsinghua/Xiaomi, Fei-Fei+NVIDIA, Huawei, EmbodyX, so re-scope to a measurement artifact" — is an Economist's industrial-organization argument, and it drives the entire DE-RISK recommendation. The "hidden cross-perspective connection" is explicitly framed as Economist + Academic converging, and the actionable insight is an Economist's hedge (don't race on compute). The **Practitioner is underweighted**: nobody asks whether a 1-4-step geometry-fidelity sweep is even *runnable* on an academic GPU budget, or whether Phys4D + WorldArena + RoboWM-Bench compose into one harness (the briefing assumes they do). The **Skeptic is used selectively** — deployed to demolish H1/H5 but not turned on the Economist's own claim that "a measurement paper can't be scooped" (it absolutely can: whoever has the compute can run the geometry-vs-steps sweep faster and publish the curve first, which guts the entire re-scope rationale).

##### 4. Missing perspective

**The hardware/sensing realist (the systems engineer who has to source the depth).** Every finding presumes a clean native-4D RGB-D stream exists to distill, measure, and transfer. But H5's whole premise — "appearance channel intact" — collides with the fact that real RGB-D is noisy, has holes at exactly the specular/transparent/thin-structure surfaces contact tasks care about, and X-WAM itself leans on *end-effector-derived* poses + off-the-shelf depth estimators (the card's own Risk row, line 776) precisely because real depth GT is absent in most robot data. A sixth perspective focused on *where the geometry signal physically comes from* would change the conclusion: the citable "geometry floor on denoising steps" (the frontier question) may be dominated not by the denoiser's step count but by sensor/estimator noise on the input geometry — in which case the elegant "hard floor on steps imposed by geometry" is confounded, and the proposed measurement measures the depth estimator as much as the substrate. That reframes the frontier question from a clean law into a sensor-conditional curve.

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.** This is a well-evidenced, refreshingly self-critical briefing that correctly identifies the direction has been substantially pre-empted and proposes a defensible (if modest) re-scope. It loses the A band on three counts: (a) an internal inconsistency between Finding 4 (RGB-D disfavored) and the actionable bet (RGB-D beating point-flow is the win condition); (b) over-crediting Flash-WAM as fresh pre-emption when the card already held Efficient-WAM as the same-mechanism concession, and under-noting that both are *pixel*-substrate so neither answers the native-4D version of H2; (c) an Economist-dominated re-scope whose load-bearing assumption ("a measurement artifact can't be lost to compute") is never stressed-tested and is probably false.

**Top fixes, in order.**
1. Resolve the H5 contradiction: either downgrade Finding 4 to "open, prior leans against" (and stop calling it structurally disfavored), or drop the RGB-D-beats-point-flow win condition from the actionable insight. Right now the paper's success criterion contradicts its own finding.
2. Sharpen Finding 2: state explicitly that the surviving residual is "step-distillation *on a native-4D read-out substrate*," and that Flash-WAM + Efficient-WAM establish the mechanism *only on pixel substrates* — that is the real gap, not "just a measurement."
3. Stress-test the Economist re-scope: name why a standardized geometry-vs-latency protocol on Phys4D + WorldArena cannot simply be run faster by the same capex-rich labs. If it can, the "measurement artifact is safe from the compute race" thesis collapses and the de-risk is illusory.
4. Add a feasibility line: confirm the proposed three-benchmark harness (Phys4D + WorldArena action-planner + RoboWM-Bench closed-loop) actually composes and is runnable on an academic budget before declaring the negative result "clean."

### C4 (Direction review)

Topic restatement: C4 bets that (i) a single world-frame geometric memory works as a *substrate-agnostic persistence layer* dropped over C1 occupancy / C2 pointmaps / C3 latent-4D, raising minute-scale coherence above each bare substrate by a margin tracking [[2505.05495|3D Persistent Embodied WM]]'s SRC 81.7% vs 63.4%; and (ii) geometric memory ([[2603.17117|MosaicMem]]) + episodic-event memory ([[2603.24576|Chameleon]]) *compound* (combined > max either) on the 68.9%-history-required subtasks of [[2605.10921|RoboMemArena]], because they fix different failure modes (where vs which-event). The headline "geometric memory beats attention-only for an action-conditioned consumer" is conceded *settled* by PEWM; the front-line is layer-reuse + compound.

Verbatim bet (First-principles): "(i) a single world-frame memory layer, dropped over each of C1/C2/C3, raises minute-scale coherence above the bare substrate by a margin tracking 3D Persistent Embodied WM's SRC 81.7% vs 63.4% no-memory gap — i.e. the layer is substrate-agnostic, not substrate-bespoke; and (ii) geometric (MosaicMem) + episodic (Chameleon) memory compound on RoboMemArena (combined > max(either) on the 68.9% history-required subtasks), because they address different failure modes — neither demonstrated by any single paper."

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The substrate-agnostic-layer claim is an engineering abstraction that will not survive contact with three real substrates that have different latency, coordinate, and update-rate profiles; the part worth building is the action-conditioned compound, because real long-horizon tasks (kitchen, retrieval) genuinely fail without history. A memory that helps in a video-WM benchmark may add deploy latency a real 1–2 Hz policy cannot absorb.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2510.20328|MemER]] runs at ~1–2 Hz real closed-loop and hits 59/60 retrievals — the practical bar; [[2602.15010|BPP]] gets +70% SR on real bimanual tasks with *latency-masked* keyframe selection, i.e. the practitioner's actual solution is selective abstraction, not dense world-frame geometry. [[2603.17117|MosaicMem]] runs 16 FPS for *camera* video, not a manipulation control loop; [[2606.09828|Mirage]] needed 10.57x speedup / 55x lower 3D-cache memory *just to make latent-3D affordable*, and still filters dynamic objects out (the moved-object permanence C4 cares about most).
- *Only-this-perspective*: The H5 footprint test is not a tiebreaker, it is the gate — if the shared layer must keep dense world-frame geometry to be substrate-agnostic, it likely loses to selective keyframe memory ([[2602.15010|BPP]]/[[2510.20328|MemER]]) on the real-robot Pareto, and the whole reuse argument becomes academic. Build H2 on a real arm first; treat H1/H5 as deferred.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The "geometric × another-memory-type compounds on action-conditioned tasks" structure is already demonstrated by multiple action-grounded ablations, so the *novel* contribution must be specifically geometric × *episodic-event* (not geometric × semantic) AND a *world-frame* persistence (not implicit). The card is honest that PEWM settles the headline, but it under-weights how crowded the compound flank already is.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2509.22548|JanusVLN]] is a clean action-grounded dual-memory ablation (semantic vs spatial-geometric) showing both essential — removing spatial-geometric drops SPL 8.3 pts (49.2→40.9) on R2R-CE; [[2606.09827|MemoryVLA++]] fuses perceptual + cognitive memory in a VLA (+26% real memory-dependent over CogACT); [[2506.05284|Long-Term Spatial Memory WM]] already combines geometric + episodic in *one* WM (view-recall PSNR 19.10 vs 11.71). The card itself flags Long-Term Spatial Memory WM as "pre-empts the compound (H2)." So the compound is established as a *pattern*; the residue is the specific pairing + the action-conditioned RoboMemArena run nobody did.
- *Only-this-perspective*: H2 as written (Chameleon-events × MosaicMem-geometry) risks being read as "yet another two-memory-types-compound paper" unless the experiment isolates *why episodic-event disambiguation beats semantic/perceptual* memory specifically on perceptual-aliasing subtasks — that is H3, and H3 is the actually-novel knife, not H2.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks it's wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The substrate-agnostic layer (H1) is the load-bearing novelty and it is the least supported and most likely to fail — coherence lift will vary wildly across occupancy/pointmaps/latent because each substrate's drift mode differs, which the card's own falsifier admits collapses the claim. And the action-conditioned-geometric-memory frontier the card calls open is being filled in real time by 2026 papers it doesn't cite.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: alphaxiv surfaces **[[Mem-World|2606.18960 Mem-World]]** (Jun 2026): a 4D-surfel geometry-aware + temporally-aware action-conditioned manipulation memory built on Ctrl-World (the card's own no-memory baseline), with action-grounded retrieval, r=0.97 sim-real correlation and policy training 58%→72% — exactly the "action-conditioned geometric memory as a layer over a WM" the card frames as open. And **2603.03596 MEM** (Physical Intelligence) compounds dense-visual + compressed-semantic memory on π0.6 manipulation with both-essential ablations across counting/find-object/window-cleaning — the compound pattern, on a real generalist VLA, at scale. The card's "nobody has run the compound on action-conditioned tasks" is eroding monthly.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The card never proves the *same* mechanism transfers; it asserts substrate-orthogonality as a first principle. If occupancy memory needs voxel-fusion and latent-4D memory needs token-retrieval, "one layer" is a slogan — the honest result may be three adapters sharing an interface, which is engineering, not a finding (the H5 falsifier: "reuse buys no efficiency, only engineering convenience").

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: This direction is benchmark-creation-shaped, and the field is mid-gold-rush minting memory benchmarks (RoboMemArena, RoboMME, RMBench, LIBERO-Mem, MemoryBench) — the citation incentive rewards a *new compound benchmark axis* far more than a marginal method. The substrate-agnostic-layer narrative is attractive precisely because it lets one paper claim relevance to three clusters (C1/C2/C3) at once, which is grant/positioning value, not necessarily science value.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card's own anchor set is benchmark-heavy ([[2605.10921|RoboMemArena]], [[2606.09803|Echo-Memory]], [[2603.04639|RoboMME]]) and three competing 2026 memory benchmarks (RoboMME, RMBench 2603.01229, LIBERO-Mem 2511.11478) already exist; [[2603.04639|RoboMME]]'s headline "no single memory representation dominates" is *itself* the finding the field is converging on, which both motivates and undercuts a "which-representation-wins" study. Big labs (Physical Intelligence's MEM, NVIDIA's [[2509.20297|mindmap]], Figure AI on RoboMME) own the compute to run the action-conditioned scale version first.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The defensible economic moat is *not* the method but the **geometric×episodic compound benchmark protocol on RoboMemArena** — own the axis, not the model. A new method on someone else's benchmark gets cited as a row; a new *measurement* of when geometric vs episodic memory wins gets cited as the reference.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the explicit-vs-implicit memory cycle replaying the pixel-vs-latent world-model and SLAM-vs-end-to-end swings — "explicit world-frame geometry beats implicit attention" is exactly the claim DR/SLAM made before learned implicit memory ate it, and the field oscillates. The "substrate-agnostic layer" dream echoes the universal-representation dream (one feature map for all tasks) that historically fragments into task-specific variants.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The card already contains the cycle in microcosm: explicit ([[2603.17117|MosaicMem]] RotErr 0.51°) beats implicit ([[2503.14489|SEVA]] 1.42°) beats static-explicit ([[2506.03141|CaM]] 4.65°) — but [[2509.22548|JanusVLN]]'s *implicit* dual memory then beats explicit-memory methods by up to 10.8% SR on navigation, the pendulum swinging back. [[2606.09803|Echo-Memory]]'s controlled study (State-Space recurrence 69.00 > Spatial-memory 17.12 on semantic identity) shows the "winner" depends entirely on what you measure — the classic signature of a cycle, not a resolution.
- *Only-this-perspective*: Whoever frames the result as "explicit geometry *always* wins" will be wrong within two cycles; the durable contribution is the *conditional* — the H3 statement of *which subtask regime* makes explicit world-frame geometry necessary vs where implicit/episodic suffices. History rewards the boundary-drawer, not the champion-of-one-side.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *Practitioner vs Skeptic on what to build*: Practitioner says drop H1/H5, build H2 on a real arm (the compound is the deployable win); Skeptic says H1 *is* the novelty and H2 is nearly pre-empted (MEM, Long-Term Spatial Memory WM) — so cutting H1 guts the paper. Concrete: does the contribution live in "one layer across substrates" (H1) or "geometric×episodic on a real task" (H2)?
   - *Academic vs Skeptic on whether the compound is novel*: Academic says the compound is novel *only if* it isolates episodic-event vs semantic memory (H3); Skeptic says MEM (visual+semantic) and JanusVLN (geometric+semantic) already show two-types-compound on action tasks, so the pairing is incremental. Specific claim in tension: is "events vs semantic" a real distinction with a different failure-mode profile, or relabeling?
   - *Historian vs the card's framing*: card says explicit geometric memory's superiority is "settled" (PEWM); Historian says Echo-Memory + JanusVLN show implicit can win depending on metric — "settled" is cycle-blind.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: **Skeptic** — it is the only one bearing a 2026 paper (Mem-World 2606.18960) the card does not cite that directly occupies the framed-open action-conditioned-geometric-memory ground, plus MEM at scale. Evidence-bound and falsifier-aligned with the card's own H1/H5 falsifiers.
   - *Weakest*: **Historian** — insightful on cyclicality but supplies no falsifiable test; "pendulum swings back" is unfalsifiable mood music unless converted into H3's conditional, at which point it collapses into the Academic's point.

3. **Question that resolves the biggest contradiction (Practitioner vs Skeptic, where does novelty live):** *On RoboMemArena's 68.9%-history-required subtasks, does geometric × episodic-event memory beat the strongest already-published compound (MEM-style visual+semantic, or JanusVLN-style geometric+semantic) — and does the margin concentrate on perceptual-aliasing/occlusion subtasks (H3)?* If yes, the novelty is real and lives in H2/H3 regardless of H1; if the gap is uniform or negative, the bet is pre-empted and H1's layer-reuse is the only refuge — which the Skeptic argues is weakest.

4. **What every perspective agrees on (likely true):** Action-conditioned long-horizon manipulation genuinely needs memory and reactive policies fail it (RoboMemArena 68.9% history-required; MemER, BPP, MEM, Mem-World all corroborate independently). The *demand* is real and measured; the dispute is only about which memory architecture wins and whether C4's specific pairing/layer is novel.

5. **Blind spot (nobody addressed):** **Where the memory's contents come from under error — write-side correctness.** Every perspective debates retrieval/representation/compound; none asks whether a persistent world-frame memory accumulates *wrong* geometry when 3D lifting fails (the card's own Risk-1) or when the policy acts on a hallucinated state, and how that error propagates across minute-scale horizons. Mem-World feeds *predicted* observations back into its surfel memory — a documented error-accumulation path — and no hypothesis measures memory *poisoning* / drift-of-the-memory-itself, only drift of generated output.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** C4's headline is honestly conceded as settled (PEWM: action-conditioned geometric memory beats attention-only, SRC 81.7% vs 63.4%), so the bet correctly retreats to two genuinely-unrun claims: a *substrate-agnostic* persistence layer over C1/C2/C3, and a *geometric × episodic-event compound* on action-conditioned RoboMemArena. The compound half is the stronger, more deployable, more defensible bet, but it sits in a fast-filling neighborhood: 2026 papers the card does not cite — Mem-World (geometry-aware action-conditioned manipulation memory on Ctrl-World) and Physical Intelligence's MEM (dense-visual × compressed-semantic compound at π0.6 scale) — are converging on the same ground, so the window is months, not years. The substrate-agnostic-layer half (H1) is the more original framing but the least evidenced and the one most likely to degrade into "three adapters with a shared interface," which the card's own falsifier calls mere engineering convenience. Net: a real opening, but it must be claimed via the *measurement* (when does geometric vs episodic memory win) rather than via the method, and fast.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
- *F1 (most reliable)*: The memory *demand* is real and action-conditioned — reactive policies fail long-horizon manipulation. Supported by all five perspectives + RoboMemArena 68.9%, MemER 59/60, BPP +70%, MEM kitchen tasks. Challenged by none.
- *F2*: The two-complementary-memory-types-compound *pattern* is already published on action-conditioned tasks, so C4's compound is incremental unless it isolates episodic-event vs semantic specifically. Supported by Academic + Skeptic (JanusVLN −8.3 SPL ablation, MemoryVLA++ +26%, Long-Term Spatial Memory WM PSNR 19.10, MEM both-essential). Challenged by the card's claim that "neither demonstrated by any single paper" — true only for the *exact* Chameleon×MosaicMem pairing.
- *F3*: The action-conditioned-geometric-memory frontier the card frames as open is being actively filled. Supported by Skeptic (Mem-World 2606.18960, r=0.97, 58→72%; MEM). Challenged by Practitioner (those are still bespoke per-WM, not deployed manipulation control loops) — partial defense, not refutation.
- *F4*: The substrate-agnostic layer (H1) is the most original but least supported claim and may reduce to engineering. Supported by Skeptic + Historian (universal-representation-fragments precedent) + the card's own H1/H5 falsifiers. Challenged by Economist (even if it's "just" a shared interface, the three-cluster positioning has citation value).
- *F5 (least settled)*: Explicit world-frame geometry's superiority is conditional, not absolute. Supported by Historian + Academic (Echo-Memory metric-dependence, JanusVLN implicit beats explicit +10.8% SR). Challenged by the card's "settled" framing and the explicit-memory rows (MosaicMem 0.51° vs SEVA 1.42°). This *conditionality is the opportunity* (it is H3).

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Economist's "own the benchmark axis, not the model," the Academic's "H3 is the real knife, not H2," and the Historian's "reward the boundary-drawer, not the champion" are the *same* claim from three angles: C4's durable contribution is not a method or a layer but the **conditional measurement of when geometric vs episodic memory wins** (H3's per-subtask decomposition on RoboMemArena). Method-novelty is contested and perishable; the *measurement* of the boundary is uncontested whitespace because RoboMME proves "no single representation dominates" without decomposing the geometric×episodic compound by failure mode, and RoboMemArena proves demand without decomposing by representation.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK (do not commit as-framed; do not drop).** The bet survives but the center of gravity must shift from H1 (substrate-agnostic layer) to H3 (the conditional compound boundary). Concretely: (a) De-scope H1 from "layer over all of C1/C2/C3" to a single-substrate proof first — claiming three transfers invites the "three adapters" refutation and triples experimental risk for the least-defensible claim. (b) Re-anchor the headline on H3, framed as a *measurement* paper: "when does persistent world-frame geometry beat episodic-event memory, and where do they compound." (c) Next experiment: run events-only ([[2603.24576|Chameleon]]) vs geometry-only ([[2603.17117|MosaicMem]]) vs both vs the strongest *published* compound baseline (a MEM-style visual+semantic or JanusVLN-style geometric+semantic memory) on [[2605.10921|RoboMemArena]], **decomposed by subtask type** (occlusion/counting/revisit per H3), and report the per-subtask margin — this single experiment simultaneously tests H2, executes H3, and pre-registers against the Skeptic's pre-emption (MEM/Mem-World) by including them as baselines rather than ignoring them. (d) Add a write-side falsifier the card lacks: measure memory *poisoning* under failed 3D lifting / acted-on hallucination across minute-scale horizons (the blind spot), since Mem-World's predicted-obs-fed-back-into-memory loop makes this the most likely real failure mode.

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Is "memory representation" even the right axis — or does a sufficiently-pretrained generalist with dense short-context (MEM's video encoder + the field's scaling) make *explicit persistent geometry obsolete* the way end-to-end learning ate hand-built SLAM front-ends, so that the winning long-horizon memory is learned-implicit + selective-keyframe and world-frame geometry survives only as a training signal, not a deployed structure?
