#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. The briefing is internally coherent and unusually honest about its own retreat, but it leans on a chain of "already published, so incremental" claims that are themselves only as strong as the analogies behind them. My job is to find where the synthesis over-credits its own evidence.*

##### 1. Confidence scores (1-10, reliability)

- **F1 — Memory demand is real and action-conditioned: 9/10.** This is the one finding the whole card can stand on. It is over-determined: RoboMemArena's 68.9% history-required figure, MemER 59/60, BPP +70%, 3D-ALP 0.650 vs 0.006 reactive (memory = 82% of gain) all point the same way, from independent benchmarks. The only deduction is that "demand exists" is a weak claim to anchor a thesis on, not that it is unreliable.
- **F2 — The two-memory compound is already published, so C4's compound is incremental: 6/10.** Directionally right but the supporting citations measure *different* compounds. JanusVLN is geometric × **semantic** and **implicit** and **navigation**; MemoryVLA++ is perceptual × cognitive (both semantic, the card itself concedes this on line 827); Long-Term Spatial Memory WM is camera-video, not action-conditioned. None of them is the geometric × **episodic-event** compound on a manipulation policy that H2 proposes. So "already published" is true for *a* compound, not *this* compound. The card's own defense ("neither demonstrated by any single paper" for the exact Chameleon×MosaicMem pairing, line 797) is the honest version, and the briefing slightly overstates the pre-emption.
- **F3 — The action-conditioned-geometric frontier is actively filling: 5/10.** This rests heavily on two papers the card does **not** cite (Mem-World 2606.18960, MEM at pi0.6 scale). I could not verify either from the card itself, and the MCP path to confirm them is degraded per standing project notes. The r=0.97 and 58→72% figures for Mem-World are uncorroborated in this document. The Practitioner's rebuttal ("bespoke per-WM, not deployed control loops") is correct and materially weakens the "window is months" urgency: a generative-WM result is not a manipulation control-loop result. Treat the urgency as plausible but unverified.
- **F4 — H1 (substrate-agnostic layer) is most original but least supported and may reduce to engineering: 7/10.** This is the card's own honest verdict and I agree with it. The H1/H5 falsifiers (lines 844, 864) are explicitly self-incriminating: "three adapters with a shared interface" and "engineering convenience" are the failure modes the card pre-writes. The deduction is only that the Economist's citation-value defense is real, so "may reduce to engineering" does not equal "should be dropped" — and the briefing's recommendation (de-risk, do not drop) gets this right.
- **F5 — Explicit world-frame geometry's superiority is conditional, not absolute: 6/10.** The evidence is genuinely two-sided (Echo-Memory's metric-dependence, JanusVLN's implicit-beats-explicit +10.8% SR), which *contradicts* the card's own line-785/839 framing that the headline is "settled." That tension is the finding's strength as an opportunity (it is H3/H4) but also a structural inconsistency the card has not resolved: the Thesis row calls geometry's win settled while H4 still proposes to test world-frame vs robot-frame and F5 says the win is conditional. You cannot both bank the headline and re-litigate it.

**Mean confidence: 6.6. Lowest: 5.0 (F3).**

##### 2. Weakest link

**F3 is the weakest link** because the urgency of the entire recommendation ("the window is months, not years") rests on two uncited 2026 papers whose headline numbers (Mem-World r=0.97, 58→72%; MEM dense×semantic at pi0.6) appear nowhere in the C4 card and cannot be checked against it. **What would verify it:** pull the actual Mem-World (2606.18960) and Physical-Intelligence MEM abstracts and confirm three things specifically — (a) is the memory *action-conditioned in a closed manipulation control loop*, or only fed back into a generative world model's prediction (the Practitioner's exact objection); (b) is the compound *geometric × episodic-event*, or geometric × semantic / dense × semantic (which would make it a JanusVLN/MemoryVLA++-class precedent, not an H2 pre-emption); (c) is there a RoboMemArena-style per-subtask decomposition, because if not, the "measurement whitespace" claim in finding 3 of the synthesis survives even if the methods land. Until those three are checked, the "months not years" framing is an assertion, not evidence.

##### 3. Bias check

**The Skeptic over-dominated; the Practitioner is under-weighted.** Four of the five findings are "supported by Skeptic" and the Skeptic supplies the two load-bearing uncited papers that drive the urgency. The synthesis has internalized the Skeptic's "this is already being done" lens so thoroughly that it has talked itself into a months-long window on the strength of two unverified citations. The Practitioner appears mainly as a *rebuttal* ("still bespoke per-WM, not deployed control loops") and that rebuttal is correct and should have been promoted, not parenthesized — it directly punctures the urgency. The Economist and Historian are used well (the "own the benchmark axis" / "reward the boundary-drawer" convergence in section 3 is the briefing's best move). The Academic is balanced. Net: the synthesis is Skeptic-led, and that bias inflates F3's urgency and slightly inflates F2's pre-emption.

##### 4. Missing perspective

**A systems / deployment-cost engineer focused on write-side correctness and memory poisoning.** Every cited perspective reasons about whether memory *helps when it is correct*. None owns the regime where memory is *wrong*: failed 3D lifting, acted-on hallucinations, and (the real danger) predicted observations fed back into the memory store over minute-scale horizons compounding error. The briefing's own actionable insight (d) gestures at this as "a write-side falsifier the card lacks," but it is buried as a sub-bullet rather than treated as a perspective. This is conclusion-changing: if persistent geometric memory is *more* brittle than episodic-event memory under acted-on hallucination (because a wrong world-frame voxel poisons every future revisit, whereas a wrong event is self-limiting), then H4's "world-frame beats robot-frame" could invert, and the safer deployable bet flips from geometry to episodic selection (BPP/MemER-style). A sixth reviewer who priced write-side failure would not endorse "geometric × episodic compound is the more deployable bet" without a poisoning experiment first.

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.** This is a strong, intellectually honest briefing: it concedes its headline is settled, retreats to genuinely-unrun claims, identifies the right knife (H3, the conditional measurement), and proposes one experiment that tests H2 + executes H3 + pre-registers against the Skeptic's pre-emption. That is graduate-level research taste. It is held below A-range by three things: (1) the urgency rests on two uncited, unverified papers; (2) an unresolved internal contradiction between "geometry's win is settled" (Thesis/H1) and "geometry's win is conditional" (F5/H3/H4); (3) the most dangerous failure mode (memory poisoning / write-side correctness) is a footnote, not a hypothesis.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Verify or down-rank F3.** Pull Mem-World 2606.18960 and MEM, check whether they are action-conditioned manipulation control loops and whether their compound is episodic vs semantic. If they are generative-WM or semantic compounds, the "months" window is wrong and the recommendation should say "years, defensible," which materially changes the calculus.
2. **Resolve the settled-vs-conditional contradiction.** Pick one: either the headline is settled (then drop H4 and stop hedging) or geometry's win is conditional (then the Thesis row must say so and H3 becomes the actual headline, as the briefing's own recommendation (b) already argues). Right now the card claims both.
3. **Promote memory poisoning from sub-bullet (d) to a first-class hypothesis H6** with its own falsifier, run *before* the compound experiment — it is cheaper, it is the likeliest real failure mode, and its outcome can invert H4 and the "geometric is more deployable" verdict.
4. **Re-anchor the headline on the measurement (H3), not the layer (H1)** — the synthesis already recommends this; the card's Thesis row should be rewritten to match, since H1's own falsifier predicts it degrades to "three adapters."

---

### D (Cluster review)

Topic: the coherence and central shared-bottleneck thesis of Cluster D — "Reconstruction for Embodied Perception" — which groups D1 (interaction-ready *scene* reconstruction) and D2 (object-level physical-asset *generation*) under one claim: *reconstruction optimizes radiance, not interaction-readiness; the embodied target is readiness-over-fidelity, applied at two scales (scene D1, object D2)*. Cluster-Overview row: "Reconstruction optimizes radiance, not interaction-readiness; assets aren't physics- or kinematics-ready... D1 builds the scene you act in; D2 builds the objects that populate it (D2 → D1 composition)." Anchors [[2404.09833|Video2Game]] and [[2605.21572|PhysX-Omni]].

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys robot policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The readiness-over-fidelity inversion is correct and matches what kills real deployments — [[2505.17966|Single-View Mesh for Robotics]] is the honest report: CV-SOTA reconstructions miss the 2 mm bar (median Chamfer ~5 mm–1 cm), produce colliding/unstable meshes, and yield only ~50% grasp transfer. That is the exact failure I see when I drop a NeRF/3DGS asset into a sim. But the cluster's deliverable — a *controlled* PSNR-vs-readiness dual-objective measurement — is an academic exercise; what I actually need is the *threshold* (H3) and the *end-to-end* drive (H4), and the card buries those as hypotheses 3 and 4.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2505.17966|Single-View Mesh for Robotics]] ~50% grasp transfer; [[2601.03200|3DGS Digital Twin]] gets Chamfer 0.0020 / F1 0.9989 and 90% real pick-place with *zero* collisions — proof readiness is reachable *from* a fidelity 3DGS asset via post-hoc cleaning, which directly undercuts the "you must optimize readiness as the objective" framing.
- *Only this perspective says*: The compute and latency line items decide adoption, not the per-criterion scorecard. [[2505.17966|Single-View Mesh for Robotics]]'s own <2 s criterion is failed by *most* models (tens-to-hundreds of seconds/scene). If the readiness-optimal pipeline is slower than meshing a fidelity asset, the practitioner uses [[2601.03200|3DGS Digital Twin]] / [[2605.26115|TriSplat]] (33–249× faster) and the dual-objective study never ships in a robot stack. H5 (feed-forward sim-mesh vs post-hoc) is the only hypothesis with deployment teeth.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The orthogonality claim is *already documented* by the card's own sources, which means the contribution cannot be "discover the orthogonality" — it must be the controlled isolation, and that is a thin slice. [[2510.05560|HoloScene]] (NeurIPS'25) reports the fidelity-vs-stability trade-off as a Table-3 ablation; [[2505.17966|Single-View Mesh for Robotics]] (RA-L'25) shows it one-sided; [[2602.10116|SAGE-Scene]] closes the readiness loop with an Isaac-Sim physics critic (99.9% stability / 1.9% collision).
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: For D2 the literature is even further ahead — [[2605.21572|PhysX-Omni]] already ships the *unified* rigid+deformable+articulated generator + PhysX-Bench scorecard, and [[2504.12684|SOPHY]] already ran D2's *own* H1 ablation (joint vs geometry-first: material-class +>20%, Sim-CD 5× lower) and H2 prediction (it *skips* rigid because rigid nearly suffices). The card concedes both in its own prose.
- *Only this perspective says*: The cluster's honesty is its liability. By admitting "already built / already refuted / already hints," it reduces the bet to two narrow falsifiers (the matched controlled comparison, and energy-predicts-OOD). That is a *workshop-paper* contribution unless the controlled study surfaces a *counterintuitive* result (e.g., the advantage is NOT where the first-principle predicts). The framing "no one ran the controlled measurement" is true but weak: a controlled measurement that confirms what three papers already showed as side-effects is not a strong accept.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: The whitespace is largely *pre-empted at the framing level*, and the cluster's "controlled measurement" deliverable is a methodological fig leaf over an already-crowded field. [[2602.08058|Picasso]] (MIT, Carlone) is the smoking gun for D1: it opens with the exact thesis ("geometrically accurate reconstructions can still be physically incorrect"), ships a NEW contact-rich dataset + a Scene Plausibility Score (SPS) + Non-Penetration Score, runs the with/without-physics-constraints ablation (physical metrics improve *more* than geometric: NPS +28.56% vs ADD-S +35.34%, SPS +10.21% on its dataset), and runs a human study validating that physical plausibility separates from geometric accuracy.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2602.08058|Picasso]] + [[2603.02133|SimRecon]] (Tsinghua/Galbot, Perception-Generation-Simulation pipeline, scene-graph physics assembly, no floating/penetration) + [[2602.10116|SAGE-Scene]] together already occupy "measure and optimize scene-level readiness separately from fidelity." On the object side [[2605.21572|PhysX-Omni]]/[[2504.12684|SOPHY]]/[[2511.13648|PhysX-Anything]]/[[2511.21887|UniArt]] occupy the generator + per-class scorecard.
- *Only this perspective says*: The card conveniently ignores that "no one ran the *controlled* per-criterion split" is a claim about a *specific experimental design*, not a capability gap — and reviewers reward capability gaps, not unrun ablations. The real risk: D1 and D2 collapse into "we ran the ablation everyone already implied." The articulation-from-single-video gap (H2) is the *only* genuine capability whitespace, and it is half-owned by [[2604.05621|FunRec]] / [[2509.17647|VideoArtGS]] / [[2507.02861|LiteReality]].

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: This cluster rides the industry sim-asset gold rush — Galbot ([[2603.02133|SimRecon]]), NVIDIA-adjacent Isaac-Sim tooling ([[2602.10116|SAGE-Scene]], [[2605.26115|TriSplat]]), Shanghai AI Lab ([[2507.12465|PhysX-3D]]/[[2605.21572|PhysX-Omni]]) — all funded to mass-produce sim-ready assets because real-robot data is the binding cost. The narrative profits whoever sells the asset pipeline; an academic "controlled measurement" paper captures none of that value and competes against teams with Isaac-Sim engineering budgets.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: The anchor papers are overwhelmingly industry/large-lab: [[2605.21572|PhysX-Omni]] (8.7K assets, human-verified), [[2507.12465|PhysX-3D]] (PhysXNet 6M assets), [[2602.08058|Picasso]] (MIT). Dataset/benchmark production (PhysX-Bench, PhysXVerse, Picasso Dataset, SAGE-Bench, FunRec's RealFun4D) is the moat — and the card's H1/H2 *depend on* those benchmarks it does not own.
- *Only this perspective says*: The de-risked move is to *own a benchmark*, not run a comparison on someone else's. The card's single defensible asset is a *standardized cross-class readiness scorecard with a public protocol* (the survey's four criteria operationalized), because that is citation-compounding and benchmark-chasing-resistant. Competing on "best sim-ready generator" against PhysX-Omni's compute is a losing capital allocation for an academic lab.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixel-vs-physics reconstruction cycle replaying the Real2Sim / system-ID lineage (PAC-NeRF → PhysGaussian → PhysTwin), and the readiness-over-fidelity inversion echoes the perennial "task metric, not proxy metric" correction (ImageNet-accuracy-vs-downstream, PSNR-vs-perceptual, render-quality-vs-control). Every wave that said "optimize the downstream-relevant property, not the proxy" was directionally right but won by *owning the downstream benchmark*, not by running the proxy-vs-task ablation once.
- *Strongest supporting evidence*: [[2303.05512|PAC-NeRF]] (joint geometry+material recovery, best in 14/17 categories) → [[2311.12198|PhysGaussian]] (continuum mechanics in the Gaussians) → [[2503.17973|PhysTwin]]/[[2606.16202|EgoPhys]] (deformable twins → category prior) is a clean 3-year lineage the cluster sits at the end of. The "annotate-onto-static vs infer-from-motion vs generate-jointly" split ([[2510.22975|VoMP]] vs [[2505.16971|UniPhy]] vs [[2605.21572|PhysX-Omni]]) is the modular-vs-end-to-end swing recurring.
- *Only this perspective says*: Historically, the "controlled comparison proving end-to-end > staged" paper (cf. end-to-end-vs-modular debates) ages poorly — within a year a bigger joint model just wins everywhere and the controlled ablation becomes a footnote. The durable contribution from these waves was always the *benchmark* (SAPIEN, PartNet-Mobility) or the *constitutive insight* (continuum mechanics in Gaussians), not the head-to-head. Bet on the artifact that outlives the next model, not the ablation.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

**(1) Direct clashes.**
- *Practitioner vs Academic/Skeptic on the deliverable*: Practitioner says the threshold (H3) and end-to-end drive (H4) are what matter; Academic/Skeptic say the *named* deliverable is the controlled PSNR-vs-readiness split (H1) — which they judge thin/pre-empted. They disagree on *which hypothesis is the real paper*.
- *Skeptic vs Card on novelty*: Skeptic says [[2602.08058|Picasso]] + [[2603.02133|SimRecon]] + [[2602.10116|SAGE-Scene]] already pre-empt the D1 *measurement framing*; the card asserts "no one ran the *controlled* dual-objective." Specific clash: is "controlled same-scene PSNR-objective-vs-readiness-objective" a real gap or a re-packaging of Picasso's with/without-physics ablation?
- *Economist vs Academic on the artifact*: Economist says own a *benchmark*; Academic says the contribution is the controlled *measurement*. These prescribe different papers (a protocol/leaderboard vs a comparative study).
- *Historian vs Card on durability*: Historian says the controlled-comparison paper ages out; the card's H1/H2 are built precisely on that comparison.

**(2) Strongest vs weakest.**
- *Strongest: SKEPTIC* — it is the most evidence-bound (names [[2602.08058|Picasso]], [[2603.02133|SimRecon]] with specific overlapping contributions) and it attacks the load-bearing novelty claim directly rather than the framing.
- *Weakest: ECONOMIST* — directionally useful (own a benchmark) but its case is incentive-narrative, not a refutation of the bet's correctness; it would survive even if the science were wrong.

**(3) The one question that resolves the biggest contradiction.**
Does [[2602.08058|Picasso]]'s with/without-physics-constraints ablation (SPS / NPS improving more than ADD-S) already *constitute* the controlled readiness-vs-fidelity measurement D1 claims is unrun — or is "reconstruct the same scene under a PSNR objective vs a four-criteria readiness objective, reported per-criterion pass/fail across articulation" a materially distinct experiment Picasso does not perform? (Picasso *retrofits/corrects* poses under physics constraints; it does not train two reconstruction objectives and compare per-criterion — so the gap may survive, narrowly.)

**(4) What every perspective agrees on (likely true).**
Interaction-readiness is genuinely orthogonal to visual fidelity, the embodied target is readiness, and this is *already empirically demonstrated* (HoloScene Table-3, Single-View-Mesh ~50% grasp transfer, Picasso's physics-vs-geometric metric split). The first-principle is sound; the dispute is only over whether *re-demonstrating it controlled* is a publishable contribution.

**(5) The blind spot (none addressed).**
No perspective addressed **dynamic / temporal readiness and the sim2real transfer of the readiness asset itself** — every readiness criterion in the cluster (collision, stability, kinematics, material) is *static-scene / single-object* and validated *in simulator* (Isaac/PyBullet/MuJoCo). Whether a "readiness-passing" asset actually closes the *real-robot* sim2real gap under contact dynamics — the thing the practitioner ultimately cares about — is untested, and the cluster explicitly routes that to Sim2Real, leaving D's central value claim (readiness → embodied usefulness) unvalidated on real hardware.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** Cluster D carves the problem at a real and well-chosen joint: reconstruction for robots should optimize *interaction-readiness* (collision, stability, kinematics, material), not radiance, and splitting that into scene (D1) and object (D2) is a clean, non-overlapping decomposition with a genuine D2→D1 composition story. The first-principle is correct and the cluster is honest about it — almost to a fault: its own sources ([[2510.05560|HoloScene]], [[2505.17966|Single-View Mesh for Robotics]], [[2504.12684|SOPHY]], [[2605.21572|PhysX-Omni]]) already *demonstrate the orthogonality and ship the pipelines*, reducing both directions' bets to narrow controlled-comparison falsifiers. A 2026 literature check surfaces [[2602.08058|Picasso]] (MIT) and [[2603.02133|SimRecon]] (Galbot), which independently operationalize "physical-plausibility measured separately from geometric accuracy" with new datasets and metrics — so the *measurement framing* is partially pre-empted, though the *specific* same-scene dual-objective per-criterion split (with articulation, H1+H2) is not yet run by any single paper. The durable opportunity is not the ablation but a standardized cross-class readiness *scorecard/benchmark* plus the one genuine capability gap: articulation-readiness from in-the-wild single video of a whole scene.

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Readiness ⊥ fidelity is real and already demonstrated* (highest reliability). Supported by: all five perspectives; HoloScene Table-3, Single-View-Mesh ~50% grasp / 2 mm miss, Picasso physics-vs-geometric metric split. Challenged by: none — but this very strength makes "discovering" it non-novel.
2. *The pipelines and generators the bets propose largely already exist.* Supported by: Academic, Skeptic, Economist ([[2605.21572|PhysX-Omni]] unified generator + PhysX-Bench; [[2504.12684|SOPHY]] already ran D2's H1/H2; [[2602.10116|SAGE-Scene]] readiness-optimizing loop). Challenged by: Practitioner (existence ≠ deployment-ready: latency/compute unmet).
3. *The novelty reduces to two controlled falsifiers, and one is partially pre-empted.* Supported by: Skeptic, Academic, Historian ([[2602.08058|Picasso]]/[[2603.02133|SimRecon]] occupy the measurement framing). Challenged by: the card's claim that the *controlled same-scene dual-objective* is genuinely unrun (narrowly survives — Picasso corrects poses, doesn't compare two reconstruction objectives).
4. *The one real capability whitespace is articulation-readiness from single video of a whole scene (D1-H2).* Supported by: Skeptic, Academic, Historian. Challenged by: half-ownership — [[2604.05621|FunRec]] (functional scenes from egocentric video), [[2509.17647|VideoArtGS]], [[2507.02861|LiteReality]] are closing it object-by-object.
5. *The durable artifact is a benchmark/scorecard, not the comparison* (lower reliability, prescriptive). Supported by: Economist, Historian. Challenged by: Practitioner (wants a threshold/end-to-end result, not a leaderboard).

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Practitioner's "I'll just post-hoc-mesh a fidelity 3DGS asset" ([[2601.03200|3DGS Digital Twin]], 90% real SR, zero collisions) and the Historian's "the controlled end-to-end-vs-staged paper ages out" are the *same* argument from opposite ends: both say *the readiness-vs-fidelity dichotomy is a false binary in practice* — you can recover readiness from a fidelity asset cheaply (post-hoc cleaning, physics correction à la Picasso), so optimizing readiness *as the objective* is not obviously necessary. This is precisely the H1 falsifier ("fidelity-optimal assets also pass readiness") and the H5 falsifier ("post-hoc meshing matches feed-forward"), meaning the cluster's *own* falsifiers encode its biggest risk.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK (do not commit as-framed; do not drop).** The first-principle is sound and the cluster is coherent, but the named deliverable (controlled PSNR-vs-readiness split) is partially pre-empted by [[2602.08058|Picasso]]/[[2603.02133|SimRecon]] and judged thin by the Academic lens. De-risk by *re-centering on the one capability gap the field has not closed*: **articulation-readiness (joints/DoF → URDF/MJCF) from in-the-wild single video of a whole multi-object scene** (D1-H2), which [[2510.05560|HoloScene]] explicitly names as future work and which [[2604.05621|FunRec]]/[[2509.17647|VideoArtGS]] only solve object-by-object or from interaction video. Concrete next experiment: take HoloScene-style single-video scene reconstruction, port the [[2603.14010|URDF-Anything+]]/[[2509.17647|VideoArtGS]] articulation path into the scene pipeline, and report URDF-exportability + joint-axis/type error on [[2604.05621|FunRec]]'s RealFun4D — establishing the *single-video-vs-multi-view articulation ceiling* as the contribution, with the H1 readiness scorecard demoted to a supporting protocol you *publish as a benchmark* (the Economist/Historian-durable artifact). Fold D2 in only as the object-asset supplier for the articulated scene, not as a parallel "joint-vs-staged generator" paper (that race is owned by PhysX-Omni's compute).

**(5) Frontier question that would change everything.** Does a "readiness-passing" reconstructed/generated asset actually close the *real-robot* sim2real gap under contact-rich dynamics — i.e., is the static four-criteria scorecard predictive of real-hardware task success, or is *dynamic/temporal* readiness (contact response, friction-under-motion) the true binding constraint the entire cluster's static-simulator validation misses? If static readiness does not transfer to real contact dynamics, D's central value claim collapses into the Sim2Real problem it tries to route away.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. Cluster D (Spatial-4D.md L873–1035), D1 interaction-ready scene reconstruction + D2 object-level physical-asset generation. Briefing claims verified against the card and a 2026 literature probe (Picasso 2602.08058, SimRecon 2603.02133, FunRec 2604.05621 confirmed real and characterized below).*

---

##### 1. Confidence scores (1–10, reliability)

| # | Finding | Score | One-line justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Readiness ⊥ fidelity is real and already demonstrated | **9** | Multiply attested in the card's own sources (HoloScene Table-3 trade-off, Single-View-Mesh ~50% grasp / 2 mm miss) and independently by Picasso's physics-vs-geometric split (verified). The self-defeating corollary — demonstrated means non-novel — is correctly drawn. Only −1 because "demonstrated" papers surface it as a side-effect, not as the controlled claim, so a pedant could argue it is *suggested* more than *proven orthogonal*. |
| 2 | The pipelines/generators the bets propose largely already exist | **8** | Strong: PhysX-Omni ships the unified generator + PhysX-Bench, SOPHY already ran D2's H1/H2 on deformable, SAGE-Scene closes a readiness-optimizing loop. The Practitioner caveat (existence ≠ deployment-ready) is real but does not rescue novelty — it shifts the goalposts to engineering, which is not the cluster's stated contribution. |
| 3 | Novelty reduces to two falsifiers, one partially pre-empted | **7** | The "partially pre-empted" half is verified (Picasso/SimRecon own the *measurement framing*: separate physical-plausibility metrics + datasets). The "narrowly survives" half (Picasso corrects poses, does not compare two reconstruction objectives) is also verified — neither Picasso nor SimRecon runs a same-scene dual-objective per-criterion split. But "reduces to two falsifiers" undersells: H3/H4/H5 exist as distinct bets; the briefing's compression to "two" is a rhetorical move, not a faithful count of the card. |
| 4 | The one real whitespace is articulation-readiness from single video of a whole scene (D1-H2) | **6** | The capability is genuinely less saturated than H1 — but the briefing overstates the gap. FunRec (verified) *already* reconstructs functional, URDF/USD-exportable articulated whole scenes from in-the-wild egocentric video across 60 apartments, and SPARK (2512.01629) + ArtiWorld (2511.12977) surfaced as further scene-level articulation entrants the card does not cite. The surviving whitespace is narrower than "single video of a whole scene": it is *passive single-video without interaction cues* (FunRec needs the interaction to disambiguate joints). That is a real but thin slice. |
| 5 | The durable artifact is a benchmark/scorecard, not the comparison | **5** | Lowest reliability and the briefing flags it as prescriptive. It is a values claim, not an empirical one: "publishable durable artifact = benchmark" is a community-fashion bet that the Practitioner directly contradicts (wants a threshold/end-to-end SR number, not a leaderboard). A benchmark with no accompanying surprising result tends to under-cite; the briefing asserts durability without evidence that *this* scorecard would be adopted over PhysX-Bench / Single-View-Mesh's existing 5-desiderata protocol, both of which already exist. |

**Mean = 7.0, lowest = 5.**

---

##### 2. Weakest link

**Finding 5 (durable artifact = benchmark, score 5)** is the least-confident, and finding 4's whitespace claim is the most *consequential* soft spot because the entire DE-RISK pivot rests on it. Taking the weakest as stated:

- **The claim**: a standardized cross-class readiness scorecard is the durable deliverable.
- **What would verify it**: evidence that a *new* readiness benchmark would be adopted rather than the field consolidating on the two protocols that already ship — Single-View-Mesh-for-Robotics' 5 desiderata (object-level) and PhysX-Bench's six attributes (D2). Concretely: (a) a coverage map showing what fraction of the four readiness criteria each existing benchmark already measures, to prove a *gap* exists rather than a re-skin; (b) at least one adoption signal (a second group reporting on the proposed protocol, or a leaderboard the card does not author). Absent that, "durable" is an assertion. A benchmark that merely re-packages two existing protocols under one survey's four-criteria frame is a contribution of organization, not of measurement — exactly the thinness the Academic lens flags for H1.

A secondary weak link worth a second probe: the DE-RISK experiment ("port URDF-Anything+ into HoloScene, eval on RealFun4D") sits *inside FunRec's own benchmark and setup*. Reviewers will ask why this is not simply "run an ablated baseline on FunRec's dataset," i.e. a delta on someone else's contribution rather than a contribution.

---

##### 3. Bias check — dominant perspective

**The Skeptic over-dominated.** Findings 1–4 are all carried by Skeptic+Academic+Historian (the three deflationary lenses), and the synthesis's center of gravity is "this is already done / partially pre-empted / narrowly survives." The Economist appears mainly to endorse the Skeptic's downgrade (benchmark-as-artifact) and the Practitioner is consistently cast as the *challenger* who is then dismissed ("existence ≠ deployment-ready, but that shifts goalposts"; "wants a threshold, not a leaderboard"). That dismissal is the tell: the Practitioner is the only lens arguing the cluster has a *deployable* payoff (a sufficiency threshold, H3), and the synthesis routes around it rather than weighing it. A balanced synthesis would have taken H3 (the readiness *threshold* below sim-accurate fidelity) as seriously as it took H1, because H3 is the one bet that is genuinely *not* pre-empted by Picasso/SimRecon/PhysX-Omni and is the most decision-relevant for a robot lab. The briefing's own ranking buries H3 entirely. Net: deflationary lenses ~70% of the weight, Practitioner present but de-weighted.

---

##### 4. Missing perspective — the 6th angle

**The Dynamicist / contact-physicist.** Every lens in the synthesis evaluates readiness as a *static, single-frame* property (geometry validity, no-collision, stability tilt, material label, URDF-exportability). None asks whether static readiness *transfers to closed-loop contact dynamics* — friction-under-motion, restitution, contact-force-vs-motion response. This is not a fringe concern: the 2026 sim2real literature names the contact-force/motion mismatch as the *primary* bottleneck in contact-rich manipulation, and the briefing itself raises it only as the closing "frontier question," never folds it into the findings. A Dynamicist would invert the cluster's premise: the four-criteria scorecard is *necessary but not sufficient*, and a "readiness-passing" asset can still fail on hardware because the binding constraint is dynamic. This would change two conclusions: (a) it strengthens H3 (threshold) into the *central* bet, since a sufficiency threshold for *dynamic* parameters is the unowned question; and (b) it reframes finding 5 — the durable benchmark must include a contact-dynamics arm validated against real hardware, or it inherits exactly the static-simulator blind spot the frontier question warns about. As framed, D validates entirely in static simulators and routes the dynamics question to Sim2Real — which means if the Dynamicist is right, D's central value claim collapses into Sim2Real, precisely as the frontier question concedes.

---

##### 5. Overall grade + top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is an unusually *honest* briefing — it correctly identifies that the cluster's first principle is sound but that its own sources have largely cashed it out, and it does the literature check (Picasso/SimRecon are real and correctly characterized; the "narrowly survives" nuance is accurate). That intellectual honesty is A-level. It loses the A on three counts: (1) it over-rotates on the deflationary lenses and de-weights the one lens (Practitioner/H3) carrying the non-pre-empted bet; (2) the DE-RISK pivot to articulation-from-single-video is a thinner whitespace than claimed — FunRec already owns scene-level articulation from in-the-wild egocentric *interaction* video, so the surviving slice is "passive video, no interaction," and the proposed experiment runs *on FunRec's own benchmark*, risking a delta-on-someone-else's-contribution critique; (3) it treats "benchmark = durable artifact" as settled when two readiness protocols already ship and adoption is unproven.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Promote H3 to the headline bet.** The readiness *sufficiency threshold* (physical parameters good enough, below sim-accurate fidelity, with the SR-preserving knee located) is the single claim Picasso/SimRecon/PhysX-Omni do *not* pre-empt and the one a robot lab can act on. Re-center the de-risk here, not on the contested articulation whitespace.
2. **Add a contact-dynamics arm.** Fold the frontier question into the design: validate at least one "readiness-passing" asset on real hardware under contact-rich motion, so the scorecard is shown to *predict* hardware success rather than asserted to. Without this, the cluster is a static-simulator exercise.
3. **Differentiate the articulation experiment from FunRec.** If the articulation pivot stays, state explicitly what the proposed run adds *over* FunRec/SPARK/ArtiWorld (e.g. passive-video-without-interaction as the contribution, with the single-video-vs-interaction-video ceiling as the measured result) — and cite SPARK (2512.01629) and ArtiWorld (2511.12977), which the card currently omits.
4. **Justify the benchmark before promising it.** Map the four readiness criteria onto what Single-View-Mesh's 5 desiderata and PhysX-Bench already measure; show the gap a new scorecard fills, or demote it to "report on the existing protocols" rather than "publish a new leaderboard."

### D1 (Direction review)

Topic: the FP bet — *readiness, not fidelity, is the embodied target of reconstruction, and it is reachable from single video* — delivered as (i) a **controlled** PSNR-vs-four-readiness-criteria per-criterion measurement on a matched scene (beyond HoloScene's ablation co-product), and (ii) **single-video articulation** recovery (joints/DoF → URDF/MJCF) that HoloScene names but does not cross.

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys policies daily).**
- *Core position*: The orthogonality of readiness and fidelity is real and already bites in deployment — a CV-winning mesh that grasps at ~50% ([[2505.17966|Single-View Mesh for Robotics]], median Chamfer ~5 mm–1 cm vs the 2 mm bar) is useless on a real Franka. But the *controlled dual-objective* deliverable (H1) is a measurement paper, not a capability; what a practitioner actually needs is the asset that passes, and [[2510.05560|HoloScene]] (81.7% stable, 100% object-recon) and [[2602.10116|SAGE-Scene]] (99.9% stable / 1.9% collision in Isaac Sim) already ship that.
- *Strongest evidence*: [[2601.03200|3DGS Digital Twin]] already hits Chamfer 0.0020 / F1 0.9989 with 90% zero-shot real pick-place and *zero* collisions on a Franka — i.e. someone has crossed the 2 mm robotics bar D1 treats as open, just via post-hoc meshing. [[2604.25459|GS-Playground]] gets 90% real SR from a 3DGS sim.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The deliverable that earns its keep is the *articulation* half (H2), not the measurement half (H1). Per-criterion pass/fail tables don't change what runs on a robot; recovering a drawer/door joint from one video does. Cut H1 to a section, make H2 the paper. Also: "single video" hides a capture-cost lie — HoloScene/SimRecon-grade results assume a slow orbit of a *static, decluttered* scene, which is not how a deployed robot sees the world.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
- *Core position*: The framing is well-grounded — the survey [[2604.26509|3D Generation for Embodied AI Survey]] does name "simulation-readiness over visual fidelity" and "scarcity of physical annotations" as *the* bottleneck, and [[2505.17966|Single-View Mesh for Robotics]] (RA-L'25) is a clean one-sided probe. But the literature contradicts the card's "no one runs the controlled comparison" on two fronts: HoloScene's Table-3 *is* a fidelity-vs-stability trade-off ablation, and [[2510.21307|Physically-Executable 3DGS Nav]] already scores act-in-ability (collision/path-feasibility) *separately from* PSNR.
- *Strongest evidence*: H2's articulation path is the most defensible, most contested slot. The single-video / casual-capture articulation field is already dense: [[2509.17647|VideoArtGS]] (revolute-axis 0.32°, position 0.42 cm), [[2506.08334|iTACO]] (joint-axis 0.32 rad), [[2603.14010|URDF-Anything+]] (Part-IoU 0.879), and crucially [[2604.05621|FunRec]], which already does *whole-scene* articulated digital twins from egocentric interaction video (~5° axis error, 77.9 mIoU on OmniFun4D) and ships RealFun4D — the very suite H2 proposes to evaluate on.
- *Only-this-perspective*: H1's falsifier is weak. "Fidelity-optimal assets also pass readiness" is plausible for *simple* scenes (a single isolated rigid object reconstructed at high PSNR often is stable and grasp-able), so the orthogonality you publish may be an artifact of clutter/occlusion, not a fundamental law. State the regime where orthogonality holds, or the result is a tautology dressed as a finding.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
- *Core position*: H1 (the controlled measurement) is a non-result — everyone in this space already reports physical-readiness metrics *alongside* PSNR ([[2510.05560|HoloScene]], [[2602.10116|SAGE-Scene]], [[2603.02133|SimRecon]] with PSNR 24.43 *and* compositional stability), so "controlled per-criterion isolation" is a reviewer-friendly framing of a delta nobody disputes. H2 (whole-scene single-video articulation) is being actively solved as I write — [[2604.05621|FunRec]] does it from video, [[2512.01629|SPARK]] does sim-ready part-level articulation, and [[2603.02133|SimRecon]] does sim-ready compositional scenes from real video.
- *Strongest evidence the card ignores*: FunRec is in the card's *own* H2 test row as the eval suite but is not confronted as a *method* that already delivers whole-scene articulated readiness from interaction video — the card frames articulation-from-video as open while citing the paper that does it. New from alphaxiv: [[2603.02133|SimRecon]] (Tsinghua/Galbot, Mar 2026) = Perception-Generation-Simulation pipeline, compositional sim-ready scenes from real video, Chamfer 4.34 / F-Score 62.65 *and* physics-stable assembly via a scene-graph synthesizer — the "joint objective from video" the card says only HoloScene approximates.
- *Only-this-perspective*: The card's whitespace is an *evaluation-protocol* gap, not a *capability* gap. Reviewers reward capabilities. If the only thing nobody has done is run two objectives on one matched scene and tabulate it, that is a workshop benchmark, not a main-conference bet — unless H2's articulation delivers a capability FunRec/SimRecon don't (e.g. articulation from *passive* single video with *no* interaction/egocentric signal).

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
- *Core position*: The narrative is funded by the sim-data-engine economy: NVIDIA (SAGE-Scene, Isaac Sim), Intel (HoloScene), and every VLA lab needs cheap diverse environments, so "reconstruct-an-environment-you-can-act-in" rides a real budget wave. But the same incentive explains why the *capability* (sim-ready twins) is crowded and the *measurement* (a per-criterion scorecard) is empty — nobody funds a benchmark that makes their own pipeline look incomplete.
- *Strongest evidence*: The metric proliferation is the tell — HoloScene's "Stable-Ratio", SAGE's Isaac-Sim collision/stability %, [[2510.21307|Physically-Executable 3DGS Nav]]'s SAGE-Bench (Continuous-Success-Ratio / Integrated-Collision-Penalty), FunRec's OmniFun4D/RealFun4D. Every lab ships *its own* readiness metric riding with its method (the card flags this for Physically-Executable Nav and 3DGS-Digital-Twin). A standardized cross-method scorecard threatens that fragmentation, which is exactly why it has no champion and is genuine whitespace — but also why it is a thankless, low-citation deliverable.
- *Only-this-perspective*: A benchmark paper's ROI depends on adoption, and adoption requires neutrality + a leaderboard the incumbents will submit to. If you own the scorecard (H1) *and* a method (H2), incumbents won't adopt your benchmark. Pick one: be Switzerland (the benchmark, H1, no competing method) or be a competitor (H2 capability, borrow others' metrics). Doing both dilutes both.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
- *Core position*: This is the pixels-vs-structure cycle replaying in reconstruction. The field optimized radiance (NeRF/3DGS PSNR race) exactly as model-based RL optimized pixel reconstruction before Dreamer-era latent models showed the proxy was wrong; D1's "readiness, not radiance" is the same "the loss you minimize is not the thing you want" correction. The DR/sim2real lineage warns the next move: once you can *measure* readiness, the field will *optimize* it directly, and the measurement paper's shelf-life is one cycle.
- *Strongest evidence*: The prior wave already turned — manually authored sim assets ([[2003.08515|SAPIEN]] URDFs) → reconstructed-then-converted ([[2601.03200|3DGS Digital Twin]] post-hoc mesh) → readiness-in-the-loop ([[2510.05560|HoloScene]] Isaac-Sim-in-the-loop, [[2602.10116|SAGE-Scene]] physics-critic). The card's H5 (feed-forward sim-format [[2605.26115|TriSplat]] vs post-hoc meshing) is the *exact* end-to-end-vs-modular swing that recurs every wave.
- *Only-this-perspective*: Every "the benchmark reveals the proxy is wrong" paper is valuable for ~18 months, then the community internalizes the criteria and the scorecard becomes table-stakes (cf. how FID/PSNR ablations are now mandatory boilerplate). H1 is a *timing* bet: publish the scorecard before SAGE-Bench / RealFun4D consolidate into the default, or it's pre-empted by adoption rather than by a paper.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

1. **Direct clashes.**
   - *H1 is novel (card) vs H1 is a non-result (Skeptic, Academic)*: The card asserts "no one runs the controlled readiness-vs-fidelity per-criterion comparison." Academic counters HoloScene Table-3 and Physically-Executable Nav's PSNR-separate metrics; Skeptic counters that SimRecon/SAGE/HoloScene all report fidelity AND readiness jointly, so "controlled isolation" is framing, not finding.
   - *H2 is open (card) vs H2 is being actively solved (Skeptic) vs H2 is the defensible core (Academic, Practitioner)*: The card lists FunRec/VideoArtGS/iTACO as the *path to port in* but the Skeptic reads FunRec + SimRecon + SPARK as already delivering whole-scene sim-ready articulation from video. Practitioner/Academic split the difference: H2 is the only part worth doing but its whitespace has narrowed to "passive single video, no interaction signal."
   - *Build a method vs own the benchmark (Economist) vs do both (card's H1+H2+H4)*: The card wants the scorecard (H1), the capability (H2), and the downstream consumer chain (H4). Economist says incumbents won't adopt a benchmark owned by a competitor — H1 and H2 are economically antagonistic.

2. **Strongest vs weakest perspective.**
   - *Strongest*: ACADEMIC — every claim is pinned to a real metric/paper from the card or alphaxiv (FunRec whole-scene articulation, HoloScene Table-3, the 2 mm-bar regime question), and it correctly localizes both the strongest slot (H2) and the fatal soft spot (H1's tautology risk).
   - *Weakest*: HISTORIAN — the pixels-vs-latent analogy is illuminating but the "18-month shelf-life" claim is unfalsifiable narrative, not evidence, and it doesn't change the COMMIT/DROP decision the way the Academic/Skeptic's concrete pre-emption evidence does.

3. **Resolving question.** Does *passive single video with no interaction or egocentric-hand signal* recover articulated joints/DoF reliably enough for URDF export — or do all current wins ([[2604.05621|FunRec]] egocentric interaction, [[2506.08334|iTACO]] casual capture *with* manipulation, [[2603.02133|SimRecon]] rigid-only) secretly depend on an interaction/multi-view signal D1 disclaims? The answer collapses the biggest clash: if passive-video articulation works, H2 is a real capability and the card commits; if it doesn't, H2 reduces to porting FunRec and the bet is the H1 scorecard alone.

4. **Universal agreement (likely true).** Readiness and fidelity are genuinely orthogonal — a higher-PSNR reconstruction is *not* a better embodied asset — and the sim-ready-twin space is crowded with joint-objective methods that surface this trade-off as a side-effect rather than measure it head-on. Every perspective accepts this; it is the one safe foundation.

5. **Blind spot (none addressed).** *Deformables, contact dynamics, and friction/restitution sufficiency.* The whole card is rigid-body (+ articulation as the frontier); H3 names a physical-parameter *threshold* but no perspective and no cited method validates mass/friction/restitution against actual *contact* outcomes — [[2411.12789|Sim-GS]] predicts material zero-shot but the card itself flags it is "not validated against contact." The survey names deformables as a bottleneck; D1 silently scopes them out. Readiness for *contact-rich* manipulation (the regime where physics fidelity actually matters) is unaddressed by every voice here.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

**(1) CEO summary.** D1 bets that reconstruction's embodied value is interaction-readiness, not radiance, and that the open deliverables are a *controlled* readiness-vs-fidelity scorecard (H1) plus *single-video articulation* recovery (H2). The first-principle is correct and universally agreed, but the bet is split across a contested seam: H1 is a measurement that the crowded joint-objective field (HoloScene, SAGE-Scene, and the newly-surfaced SimRecon) treats as a settled side-effect rather than open whitespace, while H2's whitespace has narrowed sharply because FunRec already delivers whole-scene articulated sim-ready twins *from video* and ships the exact RealFun4D suite H2 names. The direction survives only if it is re-pinned to the one gap none of these pre-empt: articulation (or a readiness threshold) from *passive* single video without interaction/egocentric signal — otherwise it is a benchmark paper (valuable but low-leverage, and economically antagonistic to also shipping a method).

**(2) Five key findings, ranked by reliability.**
1. *Readiness ⊥ fidelity is real and documented* (Single-View Mesh ~50% grasp at CV-winning Chamfer; HoloScene Table-3 trade-off). Support: all five. Challenge: Academic adds it may only hold under clutter/occlusion, not for isolated rigid objects — so the *regime* must be stated.
2. *The sim-ready-twin capability is crowded with joint-objective methods* (HoloScene 81.7% stable, SAGE 99.9%/1.9%, SimRecon Chamfer 4.34 + stable assembly, 3DGS-Digital-Twin 0.0020 Chamfer / 90% real SR / zero collisions). Support: Practitioner, Skeptic, Economist, Historian. Challenge: card (claims none runs the *controlled* split).
3. *H2's whole-scene-articulation-from-video whitespace has narrowed to passive-video-only* (FunRec whole-scene from egocentric interaction video, RealFun4D; SimRecon compositional-from-video; SPARK part-level sim-ready). Support: Skeptic, Academic. Challenge: Practitioner (still the highest-value half if the passive-video distinction holds).
4. *H1 is a real but low-leverage, hard-to-monetize deliverable* (metric fragmentation across labs is the evidence: every method ships its own readiness metric). Support: Economist, Historian, Skeptic. Challenge: card, Academic (a neutral cross-method scorecard genuinely doesn't exist yet).
5. *Contact-rich / deformable readiness is the unguarded frontier* (Sim-GS material predicted-not-contact-validated; survey names deformables a bottleneck). Support: implied by all via omission. Challenge: none — it is the blind spot, so least reliable as a *finding* but highest as an *opportunity*.

**(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection.** The Economist's "metric fragmentation → no benchmark champion" and the Historian's "18-month scorecard shelf-life" are the same mechanism viewed from two angles: H1's whitespace exists *precisely because* it is economically unrewarding to standardize (incumbents prefer their own metric), which also means the field will route around it (SAGE-Bench, RealFun4D consolidating as de-facto standards) rather than adopt a neutral one. The scorecard gap is structural, not technical — which makes H1 simultaneously *real whitespace* and *a trap*.

**(4) Actionable insight for the researcher — DE-RISK (re-pin, don't drop).** Do NOT commit to H1 as the headline; it is a genuine but low-leverage, economically antagonistic measurement that SAGE-Bench/RealFun4D may consolidate before publication. COMMIT instead to the narrowed H2: articulation (and an H3-style physical-sufficiency threshold) from *passive* single video with no interaction/egocentric/multi-view signal — the one slot FunRec (interaction video), iTACO (casual capture with manipulation), and SimRecon (rigid-only) do not occupy. Keep H1 as the *evaluation section* of that capability paper (borrow RealFun4D + the Single-View-Mesh 5-desiderata protocol; do not try to own a standalone benchmark). *Concrete next experiment*: take the FunRec RealFun4D sequences, strip the interaction/contact frames to a *passive observation* subset, and measure whether joint-axis/type error and URDF-exportability degrade past usability — quantifying exactly how much of current articulation success is *interaction-signal-dependent*. If passive-video articulation holds → publish the capability; if it collapses → the honest finding is "articulation needs interaction signal," which is itself a publishable boundary result and the correct DROP signal for the passive-video framing.

**(5) Frontier question.** Is there a *physical-sufficiency threshold* (mass/friction/restitution/joint-accuracy) below sim-accurate fidelity above which a policy transfers — and does that threshold survive *contact-rich and deformable* interaction, where the card is currently silent? If a measurable readiness threshold exists and generalizes to contact-rich tasks, "readiness, not fidelity" becomes a quantitative law that reshapes the entire reconstruction-for-robotics objective; if SR scales smoothly with parameter fidelity (H3's own falsifier), the whole direction collapses back into the transfer-gap question Sim2Real already owns.
