#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics faculty. I read the D1 card (Spatial-4D.md L877–954) and the upstream synthesis (014-D1-1-analyst.md) before scoring. The briefing is unusually self-aware — it has already absorbed most of the obvious objections — so my job is to pressure-test the claims it treats as settled, especially the FunRec/SimRecon pre-emption argument that the whole COMMIT/DROP recommendation rests on.

##### 1. Confidence scores (each finding, 1–10 for reliability)

1. **Readiness ⊥ fidelity is real and documented — 9/10.** The best-supported claim. Two independent, citable data points (Single-View-Mesh ~50% grasp at CV-winning Chamfer, RA-L'25; HoloScene Table-3 fidelity-vs-stability trade-off, NeurIPS'25) plus a survey naming it the bottleneck. The Academic's regime caveat (may collapse to a tautology on isolated rigid objects) is correctly flagged and only costs one point — it is a scoping requirement, not a refutation. The orthogonality is the safe foundation.

2. **Sim-ready-twin capability is crowded with joint-objective methods — 8/10.** Strongly supported for the *named, card-cited* methods (HoloScene, SAGE-Scene, 3DGS-Digital-Twin all appear in the card with verifiable numbers). Docked two points because the headline number list smuggles in **SimRecon (2603.02133), which has no KH note, is not in the D1 card, and is sourced only to "alphaxiv" inside the review** — i.e. an unverified citation doing load-bearing work in a "key finding." The crowdedness conclusion survives without SimRecon; the specific "Chamfer 4.34 + stable assembly" figure does not yet meet this vault's own verification bar.

3. **H2 whitespace has narrowed to passive-video-only — 5/10.** This is the pivot of the entire briefing and it is the **weakest load-bearing claim**. It rests on three legs, two of which are unverified: FunRec (verified, KH-confirmed egocentric *interaction* video), SimRecon (no KH note, not in card), and SPARK (2512.01629, no KH note, not in card). The *logic* is sound — if those three occupy interaction-video / casual-capture-with-manipulation / rigid-only respectively, then passive-single-video articulation is genuinely unclaimed. But "narrowed to passive-only" is asserted as fact when two of the three boundary stones are uncorroborated. If SPARK or SimRecon turns out to also work from passive video, the wedge closes and the COMMIT recommendation inverts. Conditional, not established.

4. **H1 is real but low-leverage and hard to monetize — 6/10.** The *whitespace-exists* half is well-argued (metric fragmentation is genuine; no neutral cross-method scene-level four-criteria leaderboard exists — the card's own Open-Problems table L1065 concedes this). The *low-leverage* half is an economic/sociological prediction ("incumbents won't adopt a competitor's benchmark," "SAGE-Bench/RealFun4D will consolidate first") presented with more confidence than its evidentiary basis warrants. Benchmark papers from non-incumbents do sometimes win (this is an existence claim the briefing treats as a frequency claim). Plausible, directionally right, oversold.

5. **Contact-rich / deformable readiness is the unguarded frontier — 4/10 as a finding (8/10 as an opportunity).** The briefing itself rates this "least reliable as a finding." Correctly so: it is an argument from omission ("no perspective addressed it"), and Sim-GS being "predicted-not-contact-validated" is one data point. The survey naming deformables a bottleneck is real but generic. This is a well-chosen blind spot, not a demonstrated finding. The score reflects the finding's reliability, which the briefing honestly under-claims.

**Mean: 6.4. Lowest: 4 (finding 5).**

##### 2. Weakest link

The least-confident *load-bearing* claim is **finding 3** (the passive-video wedge), and the single most fragile piece is the **concrete next experiment** that operationalizes it: "take RealFun4D, strip the interaction/contact frames to a passive observation subset, and measure whether articulation degrades." This experiment may be ill-posed. RealFun4D sequences are, per the FunRec KH note, *defined by* egocentric human interaction — the articulation signal IS the human opening the drawer/door. Strip the interaction frames and you may not have a "passive observation of the same articulated event"; you may have a static scene with no articulation event to recover at all, making degradation trivially total for reasons that have nothing to do with the scientific question. **Info needed to verify:** (a) the FunRec paper's actual RealFun4D data format — does each sequence contain pre/post-interaction static observations from which a passive method could in principle infer joints, or only the during-interaction frames? (b) the real arxiv abstracts for SimRecon (2603.02133) and SPARK (2512.01629) to confirm they are rigid-only / part-level-with-interaction respectively, since the entire "passive-only is unclaimed" conclusion depends on them not occupying that slot. Both are currently uncited-in-card, KH-absent, alphaxiv-sourced claims that no one in this vault has independently checked.

##### 3. Bias check — which perspective over-dominated

**The SKEPTIC over-dominated, with the ECONOMIST as amplifier.** The synthesis's center of gravity — "H1 is a non-result, H2 is being actively solved, the only survivor is passive-video" — is the Skeptic's thesis verbatim, and the Skeptic is also the perspective leaning hardest on the *unverified* SimRecon/SPARK citations to make its case. The Economist then supplies the "don't own a benchmark" framing that hardens the de-risk recommendation. The net effect is a briefing that argues itself toward DROP-most-of-it / COMMIT-to-one-narrow-slot.

The **Practitioner is under-weighted**: its position that H2 (articulation) is the only deliverable that changes what runs on a robot, and that "single video" hides a capture-cost lie (HoloScene-grade results assume a slow orbit of a static decluttered scene), is the most deployment-relevant objection in the document and gets folded into a parenthetical "Challenge" on finding 3 rather than driving the recommendation. The Academic's tautology warning on H1 is well-integrated; the Practitioner's capture-realism warning is not. A briefing this Skeptic-led naturally discounts capabilities in favor of gaps.

##### 4. Missing perspective — the 6th angle

**The DATA/CAPTURE-COST engineer (or: the person who has to actually collect the input).** Every perspective here debates what the *output* should be (readiness vs fidelity, articulation vs rigid) and who *owns* it (benchmark vs method). None owns the *input distribution*. The whole D1 bet says "from single video," and the entire passive-video wedge is an *input-side* distinction — yet no perspective interrogates whether passive single video of a real cluttered home scene even *contains* the information needed to recover articulation. Articulation is, almost by physical definition, observable only through motion or interaction; a single passive video of a closed drawer has no joint to recover. This reframes the frontier question: the briefing assumes "passive-video articulation" is an open *capability* slot, but the missing perspective suggests it may be an *ill-posed problem* (you cannot infer a revolute axis you never see actuated), in which case FunRec's reliance on interaction signal is not a competitive moat to route around but a statement of what the problem *requires*. If that is true, finding 3 and the entire COMMIT recommendation collapse — the honest result the briefing files under "publishable boundary result" would actually be the *expected* result, and a weak paper. This sixth angle would change the conclusion from "re-pin to passive-video articulation" to "the passive-video framing is probably the wrong axis; the real open slot is articulation from *minimal* interaction (one push) at home-scene capture cost."

##### 5. Overall grade and top fixes

**Grade: B+.**

This is a strong, intellectually honest briefing — it pre-empts the tautology objection, surfaces its own blind spot, ranks findings by reliability, and the de-risk-don't-drop recommendation is the correct *shape* of advice for a contested-seam direction. It earns well above a B for self-awareness and for not rubber-stamping the card's "no one runs the controlled comparison" claim (it correctly catches HoloScene Table-3 and Physically-Executable-Nav's PSNR-separate metrics as partial counter-evidence). It falls short of A-range for three reasons: the central pivot rests on two unverified citations, the headline experiment may be ill-posed, and a deployment-realism perspective that would temper the recommendation was under-weighted.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Verify SimRecon (2603.02133) and SPARK (2512.01629) before letting them carry findings 2 and 3.** Pull the real abstracts; confirm rigid-only / part-level-with-interaction. If either works from passive video, the wedge closes and the recommendation must change. Right now two load-bearing claims sit on KH-absent, card-absent, single-source citations — below this vault's own evidentiary standard.
2. **Re-derive the concrete experiment.** Confirm RealFun4D actually supports a meaningful passive subset (pre/post-interaction static frames a passive method could exploit) before proposing to strip interaction frames. If it cannot, the experiment measures an artifact, not the science. Consider the better-posed variant: articulation from *minimal* (single-push) interaction vs FunRec's full interaction sequence — quantifying *how much* interaction is needed, not the binary passive-vs-not.
3. **State the orthogonality regime for H1 (the Academic's caveat) and promote the Practitioner's capture-cost objection** from parenthetical to a first-class risk. "Single video" of a static decluttered orbit is not the deployed-robot input distribution; the briefing should say which one it is betting on.
4. **Down-rate the economic certainty on finding 4.** "Incumbents won't adopt a competitor's benchmark" is a tendency, not a law; present it as a risk to manage (release the scorecard neutrally, decouple from the method) rather than a reason the deliverable is structurally doomed.

---

### D2 (Direction review)

Topic: Object-level physical-asset generation, the bet that joint physics-with-geometry generation beats geometry-first-then-annotate staged pipelines on per-object simulation-readiness, decomposed per class (rigid/deformable/articulated), with energy/momentum-consistency (not fidelity) predicting out-of-category readiness. The card's verbatim bet: "the controlled falsifier nobody has run unified... (i) generating the *same* matched object set (a) geometry-first-then-annotate vs (b) jointly via a PhysX-Omni-style unified head, the staged pipeline fails the kinematic/material readiness criterion substantially more often, decomposed per-class with the advantage concentrated on deformable + articulated (≈0 on rigid props)... and (ii) a physical-consistency metric (energy/momentum) predicts out-of-category readiness better than fidelity (PSNR/Chamfer)."

#### Prompt 1 — Five Perspectives

**1. PRACTITIONER (builds/deploys robot policies daily).**
Core position: The card's deliverable is a *measurement* (a scorecard + regression), not an asset pipeline, and what a practitioner actually needs is one generated object dropped into a sim that a policy can act on without the sim exploding, which is H4, the weakest-tested hypothesis here. The per-object readiness criteria that matter on a real robot are narrow: mass/friction for grasping, joint axis/limit for articulation, contact stiffness for deformables, and most of those are unobservable from a single image regardless of joint-vs-staged.
Strongest supporting evidence: EgoPhys (2606.16202) is the only paper on the card that closes the loop to a *physical* robot (xArm6, 77.6% sim-to-real config-error reduction, zero-shot on 11 held-out deformables) and it does so deformable-only with a codebook prior, not a cross-class joint generator. UniArt (2511.21887) and Infinigen-Articulated (2505.10755, 18/30 vs 3/30 zero-shot door-opening) show articulated assets transfer when the URDF joint is right. PhysX-Omni's (2605.21572) "deployed in policy learning" claim is a demonstration, not a controlled transfer study.
The one thing only this perspective says: the binding constraint at deploy time is parameter *identifiability* from the input modality (single image / sparse RGB-D), not the geometry-physics coupling the bet is about, so even a perfect joint generator inherits the under-determination the card's own Risk #2 flags. Build H4 first, because if neither material nor kinematic ablation gates transfer, the whole readiness scorecard is academic.

**2. ACADEMIC (knows the peer-reviewed literature).**
Core position: The card is honest that the "build a unified generator" question is settled (PhysX-Omni, SOPHY, PhysX-Anything 2511.13648, UniArt, PhysGM 2508.13911 all exist), and it correctly retreats to the un-run *controlled comparison*, which is a legitimate, publishable methodological contribution. But the literature already contains the core finding the comparison would produce: SOPHY (2504.12684) ran joint-vs-geometry-first and got material-class +>20%, Sim-CD 5x on deformable, plus the diagnostic "skip rigid" choice that is H2's whole thesis.
Strongest supporting evidence: SOPHY's ablation is exactly H1/H2 on one class; the open question is genuinely only "does it hold cross-class on one scorecard." For the energy-consistency arm (H3), NeuROK (2605.30347, energy-conserving, 83.33%/81.43% user-pref on unseen categories) and GausSim (2412.17804, mass/momentum conservation, L2 1.85 vs 11.32) supply the consistency-side priors, and VoMP (2510.22975, ALRE 0.0409 vs NeRF2Physics 0.1346) is the strongest staged-annotation baseline a falsifier must beat.
The one thing only this perspective says: H3 (consistency-predicts-OOD-better-than-fidelity) is the *only* hypothesis on this card that is not already half-answered by an existing paper, and it is the one stated most loosely (a regression with no specified estimand, no held-out category protocol). That is where the academic novelty actually lives, and it is currently the least-developed test.

**3. SKEPTIC (thinks the bet is wrong or already solved).**
Core position: The whitespace is a definitional artifact. The card defines novelty as "matched-object joint-vs-staged, decomposed per-class, on ONE scorecard," and every conjunct is a knob it added to escape pre-emption, SOPHY already did joint-vs-staged, PhysX-Bench already is a scorecard, the per-class split is the only genuinely new pixel and it is a confirmatory study whose result (deformable/articulated win, rigid tie) the card already predicts and SOPHY already evidenced. A confirmatory measurement of an outcome you can already name is a benchmark paper, not a research bet.
Strongest supporting evidence: The card concedes its own anchor "already ships the unified generator + PhysX-Bench scorecard" and that SOPHY "ran D2's own H1." DSO (2503.22677, Oxford) shows you can align a generator to a physical criterion (stability) via simulation feedback without ground-truth 3D, generalizing the "physics-with-geometry beats fidelity-only" point beyond what the card credits. PhysX-3D (2507.12465, 6M PhysXNet assets) already reports physics+fidelity jointly at scale.
The one thing only this perspective says: the convenient omission is that "staged" is a strawman, nobody serious proposes generate-then-annotate as a competitor; the real frontier competitor is *better feed-forward joint generators* (PhysGM, DSO), so the H1 falsifier beats a baseline no one defends while the genuinely hard comparison (joint-generator-A vs joint-generator-B on OOD readiness) is the one the card does not commit to.

**4. ECONOMIST (follows money/incentives).**
Core position: This sub-field is a benchmark-and-dataset land-grab (PhysXVerse 8.7K assets, PhysXNet 6M, PhysAssets 50K, SOPHY's 3K-object set), and whoever owns the scorecard owns citations, which is exactly why PhysX-Omni shipped PhysX-Bench alongside the generator. A pure-measurement paper that adopts *someone else's* generator and *someone else's* four criteria (the survey's) is structurally a citation-feeder for the incumbents, not a moat.
Strongest supporting evidence: The card's own targets are all denominated in incumbent benchmarks (PhysXVerse kinematic 0.9185, PhysX-Bench readiness, NeuROK Chamfer-L1 0.028, 3DGen-Bench 2503.21745 as the fidelity arm). The compute asymmetry is stark: dual-pipeline cross-class generation requires running PhysX-Omni-class models on a matched set, which favors the labs that already trained them.
The one thing only this perspective says: the cheapest defensible asset here is *the matched-object benchmark itself* (a curated rigid+deformable+articulated readiness suite with a public leaderboard), because the field's stated Risk ("no shared leaderboard across categories") is a market gap a small lab can own, whereas the joint-vs-staged finding will be absorbed into the next incumbent's appendix.

**5. HISTORIAN (has seen prior waves).**
Core position: This is the pixel-vs-latent and end-to-end-vs-modular cycle replayed on the asset layer, "generate geometry then annotate physics" is the modular pipeline, "joint single-pass generation" is the end-to-end bet, and history says end-to-end wins on the hard, coupled cases (deformable/articulated where geometry and dynamics co-determine) and ties or loses on the easy decoupled cases (rigid props), which is *exactly* what H2 predicts. The bet is on the right side of a recurring pattern.
Strongest supporting evidence: PhysGaussian (2311.12198) and PAC-NeRF (2303.05512) are the canonical "bake physics into the representation" lineage; PAC-NeRF won 14/17 physical-property categories via joint recovery. The shift from per-object optimization (PhysTwin 2503.17973) to feed-forward category priors (EgoPhys, VoMP, PhysGM) recapitulates the optimization-to-amortization arc seen in NeRF-to-feedforward and DreamFusion-to-LRM.
The one thing only this perspective says: every prior end-to-end-vs-modular swing eventually re-modularized once the coupled case was solved (the field keeps a thin modular seam for controllability); so the durable contribution is not "joint wins" but *characterizing the boundary* (which classes/properties genuinely need coupling), which is H2's per-class decomposition, the part most likely to still be cited in three years.

#### Prompt 2 — Contradiction Map

(1) Direct clashes:
- ACADEMIC vs SKEPTIC on whether the cross-class scorecard is novel. ACADEMIC: "the open question is genuinely only does-it-hold-cross-class, that is publishable." SKEPTIC: "per-class split is the only new pixel and it is confirmatory of an outcome you already named." Same fact (SOPHY did one class), opposite verdict on whether generalizing to three classes clears the novelty bar.
- PRACTITIONER vs HISTORIAN on where value sits. PRACTITIONER: build H4 (does the object transfer to a policy) first because the rest is academic; identifiability, not coupling, is the real constraint. HISTORIAN: the durable contribution is H2 (the per-class coupling boundary), H4 transfer is a demo that dates quickly. They disagree on which hypothesis survives.
- SKEPTIC vs ECONOMIST on the strawman. SKEPTIC says "staged" is a baseline nobody defends, so beating it is hollow. ECONOMIST says beating-the-strawman is fine *if* you ship the benchmark, because the benchmark is the asset regardless of the headline finding.

(2) Strongest vs weakest. Strongest: SKEPTIC, because it lands the one structural objection the card cannot fully rebut, the whitespace is constructed by conjunction and the genuine competitor is a stronger joint generator (PhysGM/DSO), not staged annotation, an argument grounded in the card's own concessions and corroborated by alphaxiv (DSO generalizes the physics-via-feedback point). Weakest: ECONOMIST, insightful on the benchmark-moat but its prescription (own the suite) is orthogonal to whether the *scientific* bet is true, it changes the packaging, not the falsifier.

(3) Resolving question: On a matched object set, does a *strong joint generator* (PhysX-Omni / PhysGM) beat the *strongest staged-annotation baseline that exists* (VoMP, volumetric ALRE 0.0409) on out-of-category deformable+articulated readiness, by a margin larger than the gap between two joint generators? If yes, the coupling is load-bearing and the bet survives the SKEPTIC; if the joint-vs-joint gap dominates, the contribution collapses to a generator bake-off.

(4) Universal agreement (likely true): the joint-generation advantage, wherever it exists, is concentrated on deformable and articulated objects and is near-zero on rigid props. SKEPTIC predicts it, HISTORIAN explains it as the coupled-case pattern, ACADEMIC has SOPHY's skip-rigid evidence, PRACTITIONER agrees rigid mass/friction nearly suffices, ECONOMIST is indifferent but does not contest it. H2 is the safest claim on the card.

(5) Blind spot (none addressed): the *generator quality confound*. Every comparison runs "staged" and "joint" through different model families with different training data and capacity, so a joint-vs-staged readiness gap is not cleanly attributable to the coupling, it could be the joint generator simply being a better-trained model. No perspective demanded that the staged and joint arms share a backbone/data budget, which is the single methodological hole that would let a reviewer dismiss the headline result.

#### Prompt 3 — Synthesis Briefing

(1) CEO summary. D2 is a well-grounded, honestly-scoped bet that has already paid out its easy half: the "build a unified physics-with-geometry generator" question is settled (PhysX-Omni, SOPHY, PhysX-Anything, UniArt, PhysGM), and the card correctly retreats to the un-run *controlled comparison* and a per-class readiness decomposition. The trouble is that the retreat is narrow: SOPHY already ran joint-vs-staged on deformables and even made the skip-rigid choice H2 is built on, so the cross-class extension is confirmatory of an outcome the card itself can already name, and the genuine 2026 competitor is not "staged annotation" (a baseline no one defends) but stronger joint generators. The two pieces of real whitespace are H3 (whether energy/momentum-consistency predicts out-of-category readiness better than fidelity, the one hypothesis no existing paper answers and also the one specified most loosely) and a defensible cross-class readiness benchmark; everything else risks being absorbed into an incumbent's appendix.

(2) Five key findings ranked by reliability:
- F1 (highest): The joint-vs-staged advantage is concentrated on deformable + articulated and ~0 on rigid. Supported by ALL five perspectives; evidenced by SOPHY's +>20%/5x deformable result and explicit rigid skip. Challenged by: none directly, only the confound caveat (could be generator quality).
- F2: The "build a unified generator" question is settled, so D2's only live contribution is the comparison/scorecard. Supported by ACADEMIC, SKEPTIC, ECONOMIST (card concedes it). Challenged by PRACTITIONER (existence demos are not controlled transfer, H4 still open).
- F3: H3 (consistency-predicts-OOD-better-than-fidelity) is the most novel and most under-specified hypothesis. Supported by ACADEMIC; NeuROK/GausSim supply priors, VoMP supplies the fidelity-error yardstick. Challenged by SKEPTIC (loosely stated regressions invite p-hacking).
- F4: The real competitor is a stronger *joint* generator (PhysGM, DSO), not staged annotation, so the H1 falsifier beats a strawman. Supported by SKEPTIC, corroborated by alphaxiv (DSO 2503.22677 aligns generators to physics via sim feedback). Challenged by ACADEMIC (staged-via-VoMP is a real, strong, volumetric baseline, not a strawman).
- F5 (lowest, most contested): The durable asset is the benchmark/leaderboard, not the finding. Supported by ECONOMIST, partially HISTORIAN (boundary-characterization endures). Challenged by PRACTITIONER (a leaderboard without robot transfer is academic) and the bet's scientific framing.

(3) Hidden cross-perspective connection: the SKEPTIC's "constructed whitespace," the ECONOMIST's "citation-feeder," and the blind-spot "generator-quality confound" are the same defect viewed three ways. Because the comparison borrows the incumbent's generator and the survey's criteria and a strawman baseline, it cannot cleanly attribute its result to the coupling, and an unattributable result is exactly what gets absorbed into the incumbent's next appendix. Controlling the confound (shared backbone/data across joint and staged arms) is simultaneously the fix for the science (clean attribution), the moat (a controlled protocol others must cite), and the rebuttal to the strawman charge.

(4) Actionable insight for the researcher: DE-RISK, do not commit as framed. The headline "joint > staged" bet is too pre-empted by SOPHY and too confounded to anchor a paper. Re-center on the two live pieces: (a) H3 made rigorous, and (b) a shared-backbone controlled protocol. Concrete next experiment: take ONE generator family (PhysX-Omni-class), produce two arms that differ *only* in the physics pathway, joint single-pass vs a geometry-first head with VoMP (2510.22975) bolted on for material annotation, on a matched rigid+deformable+articulated set; hold out whole categories; then regress per-class out-of-category readiness on (i) an energy/momentum-consistency metric vs (ii) fidelity (PSNR/Chamfer, via 3DGen-Bench 2503.21745) and report which predicts held-out-category pass-rate. If consistency wins the regression AND the joint-vs-staged gap exceeds the joint-vs-joint gap, you have a defensible paper; if not, pivot to publishing the controlled readiness benchmark itself (ECONOMIST's moat) and drop the causal claim.

(5) Frontier question that would change everything: Is per-object readiness even the right unit, or does embodied usefulness only emerge at the object-in-contact-with-policy level, such that a "ready" object by any static scorecard still fails under the specific contact distribution a policy induces (PRACTITIONER's identifiability point made structural)? If readiness is irreducibly policy- and contact-conditioned, then every static scorecard on this card, PhysX-Bench, the survey's four criteria, ALRE, measures the wrong thing, and the field needs a closed-loop readiness metric instead.

---

#### Prompt 4 — Peer Review (independent)

*Reviewer stance: skeptical Stanford robotics professor. The briefing is unusually self-aware; my job is to test whether its honesty is load-bearing or decorative.*

---

##### 1. CONFIDENCE SCORES (1-10, reliability)

- **F1 — joint>staged concentrates on deformable+articulated, ~0 on rigid — 8/10.** This is the single best-supported claim. SOPHY's `material +>20% / Sim-CD 5x` and its explicit *skip-rigid* design choice are direct, in-card evidence, and the first-principle (a watertight mesh nearly suffices for a rigid prop; physics only bites where geometry under-determines behavior) is mechanistically coherent. Docked two points because the evidence is *single-paper* (SOPHY) on a *single class* (deformable). "~0 on rigid" is asserted as a prediction (H2), not measured cross-class, and the confound (could be generator quality, not the joint-vs-staged factor) is conceded but not yet controlled. Strong directional claim, not yet a result.

- **F2 — "build a unified generator" is settled; D2's only live contribution is the comparison/scorecard — 8/10.** Five anchor papers (PhysX-Omni, SOPHY, PhysX-Anything, UniArt, PhysGM) independently ship unified or feed-forward joint generators with OOD generalization; the existence question is genuinely closed. The card *concedes this against its own original framing*, which is intellectually honest and rare. I dock two points only because "the comparison is the contribution" is exactly the claim the SKEPTIC's "constructed whitespace" worry targets — a comparison can be settled-by-construction the moment someone reports an ablation, so "live" is fragile, not robust.

- **F3 — H3 (consistency-predicts-OOD-better-than-fidelity) is most novel and most under-specified — 7/10.** The novelty is real: no cited paper regresses out-of-category readiness on a consistency metric against a fidelity metric, and NeuROK/GausSim/VoMP supply exactly the priors and yardsticks needed. But the under-specification is severe enough to threaten the finding itself: "energy/momentum-consistency metric" is never operationalized (which energy? measured how, on what rollout horizon? a generation-time penalty or a post-hoc diagnostic?), "out-of-category readiness" is a held-out-category pass-rate whose threshold is unstated, and a loosely-stated regression with researcher-chosen metrics on both axes is a p-hacking surface. High novelty, low specification — a genuinely open question dressed as a near-ready experiment.

- **F4 — the real competitor is a stronger joint generator (PhysGM/DSO), not staged annotation — 6/10.** I find this the most internally *contested* finding and the SKEPTIC and ACADEMIC are both partly right. The SKEPTIC is correct that "staged annotation no one defends" is a soft falsifier — beating an undefended baseline proves little. But the ACADEMIC's rebuttal is stronger than the briefing credits: VoMP (2510.22975) is a *volumetric, internal-parameter* material annotator with a hard ALRE yardstick (0.0409 Young's vs NeRF2Physics 0.1346), and the card itself flags it as "the strongest staged-annotation baseline H1 must beat." So staged-via-VoMP is not a strawman; it is a real, strong, falsifiable arm. The finding conflates two different competitors (a stronger *joint* generator that pre-empts the *novelty*, vs a stronger *staged* baseline that pre-empts the *result*) and treats the strawman charge as settled when the card's own table refutes it. Docked for that conflation.

- **F5 — the durable asset is the benchmark/leaderboard, not the finding — 5/10 (lowest).** This is the load-bearing strategic claim and it is the weakest. The ECONOMIST's "a controlled protocol others must cite" is plausible *only if* the protocol is the one everyone converges on — and a static, per-object, simulator-side readiness scorecard is precisely what the Frontier Question (Q5) argues measures the wrong thing. If readiness is policy- and contact-conditioned, the leaderboard is a moat around the wrong hill, and the PRACTITIONER's "a leaderboard without robot transfer is academic" lands hard. The H4 (object→sim-policy transfer) row is the only thing tying any of this to embodiment, and it is the *least* developed hypothesis on the card. Lowest confidence because the asset's durability depends on a metric-validity assumption the card's own frontier question attacks.

**Mean = 6.8. Min = 5 (F5).**

---

##### 2. WEAKEST LINK

**F5 (the leaderboard is the durable asset).** What would verify it: a demonstration that *static per-object readiness predicts downstream policy success* on at least one closed-loop transfer task — i.e., evidence that an object scoring "ready" on PhysX-Bench / the survey's four criteria / ALRE actually yields higher sim-trained-policy success than an object scoring "not ready," *and that this ordering survives the specific contact distribution a policy induces* (Q5's challenge). Concretely: run H4 first, not last. If readiness rank-correlates with policy transfer success across a held-out object set, the scorecard is a durable asset and F5 is verified. If the correlation is weak or contact-distribution-dependent, the leaderboard is academic and the strategy should re-center on the closed-loop metric. Until that correlation exists, "the benchmark is the moat" is an assertion, not a finding.

---

##### 3. BIAS CHECK

**The SKEPTIC over-dominated; the PRACTITIONER was under-weighted.** Four of the five findings (F2, F3, F4, and the entire §3 cross-perspective connection) are framed as *defects to de-risk* — constructed whitespace, p-hacking surface, strawman baseline, unattributable-result-absorbed-into-incumbent's-appendix. This is good adversarial hygiene and it correctly demolishes the easy half of the bet. But it produces a briefing whose center of gravity is "why this paper might not be defensible" rather than "what embodied capability this unlocks." The PRACTITIONER's objection — that *none of this touches a robot* until H4, and H4 is the thinnest hypothesis — appears only as a challenger on F2 and F5, never as a driver. The result: the recommended next experiment (§4) is a clean *generation-side* regression with zero robot in the loop, which is exactly what the PRACTITIONER and the Frontier Question say is the wrong unit of analysis. A balanced synthesis would have promoted H4/transfer from "challenge" to "co-equal first experiment."

---

##### 4. MISSING PERSPECTIVE (a 6th angle that would change conclusions)

**The COMPUTE/DATA-ECONOMICS realist (a "scaling-curve" perspective).** Every joint generator cited is feed-forward and trained on large physics-annotated corpora (PhysXNet 6M assets, PhysX-3D's scale). The unasked question: *is the joint-vs-staged gap a fixed structural advantage, or does it close as either pipeline's training data scales?* If a staged pipeline with a strong volumetric annotator (VoMP) plus more annotated data closes the gap that SOPHY observed at small scale, then "joint > staged" is a small-data artifact, not a first principle — and the entire bet inverts. This perspective would change the conclusion because it reframes H1's falsifier: the controlled comparison must hold *data and compute matched across arms* (which §3 half-acknowledges via "shared backbone") AND *across a data-scale sweep*. Without the scale axis, a single-scale joint-beats-staged result is exactly the kind of finding an incumbent re-runs at scale and absorbs. None of the five perspectives raises scaling behavior, and it is the most likely way the headline bet is quietly wrong.

---

##### 5. OVERALL GRADE + TOP FIXES

**Grade: B+.**

This is a strong, honest briefing that correctly identifies that the easy half of the bet is pre-empted and retreats to defensible whitespace (H3 + a controlled protocol). The §3 insight — that constructed-whitespace, citation-feeder, and generator-confound are one defect viewed three ways, fixed by a shared-backbone protocol — is genuinely good and is the most valuable sentence in the document. It is held back from the A range by three things: (1) it lets the SKEPTIC crowd out the PRACTITIONER, so the recommended experiment never touches a policy; (2) F4 conflates "stronger joint generator" with "stronger staged baseline" and prematurely calls VoMP-staged a strawman when the card's own table says otherwise; (3) the durability claim (F5) rests on a metric-validity assumption that the briefing's own frontier question demolishes.

**Top fixes, in priority order:**
1. **Promote H4 (object→policy transfer) to a co-equal first experiment** and gate the leaderboard claim on a demonstrated readiness→transfer-success correlation. This simultaneously answers the PRACTITIONER and pre-validates the moat. *(Top fix.)*
2. **Operationalize H3's consistency metric before running it** — name the energy/momentum quantity, the rollout horizon, the held-out-category pass threshold, and pre-register the regression — to neutralize the p-hacking charge.
3. **Stop calling staged-via-VoMP a strawman.** Make it the named, volumetric, ALRE-anchored baseline arm with matched backbone/data, and add a data-scale sweep so the joint-vs-staged gap is shown to be structural, not a small-data artifact.

---
